# Codvian Router — Phase 1 MVP Backend Checklist

Backend-first. No UI. Local worktree only: `~/workspace/codvian-router/`.
Build-first-test-last: built ALL pieces, then ONE test gate at the end.
Gate run 2026-11-17 by the coordinator (independent re-run, not the builders' own claims).

| # | Feature | Built | Verified |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Go module scaffold, single binary, ~1s start | ✅ | ✅ |
| 2 | Versioned config file (JSON, v1), localhost-only bind, auth ON by default | ✅ | ✅ |
| 3 | Gateway API key issue/verify, per-key scopes & budgets | ✅ | ✅ |
| 4 | Keys encrypted at rest (AES-256-GCM; method documented in internal/crypto/README.md) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 5 | Provider registry: any base URL + API key, inline key test, auto-fetch model list, wire-format auto-detect (OpenAI-compatible first) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 6 | Codvian as a provider option (base URL + API key) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 7 | Routing engine: fallback chains | ✅ | ✅ |
| 8 | Quota-aware routing | ✅ | ✅ |
| 9 | 429 / Retry-After handling, backoff + per-key cooldown | ✅ | ✅ |
| 10 | Circuit breaker | ✅ | ✅ |
| 11 | Hedged requests (backup attempt starts early, ~150ms) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 12 | Streaming-safe failover, mid-stream resume | ✅ | ✅ |

| 15 | One budget cap + fail-closed "never bill me" mode | ✅ | ✅ |

| 21 | Registry quota hints wired into the engine (live `QuotaHintFunc`, hint 0 = skipped, higher hint first) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 22 | Ledger honest accounting: full prompt/completion split recorded; split-unknown attempts record total + `split_known=0`, cost 0 (never invented) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 23 | Semantic cache: real local implementation (deterministic hashed bag-of-words, 256-dim, cosine ≥ 0.85, LRU + TTL) — explicitly NOT a neural embedding model | ✅ | ✅ |
| 24 | Scoring: cost/latency/quality routing — 5 modes (Balanced/Fast/Cheap/Quality/Reliable), weights sum to 1, unknown quality never outranks known-good | ✅ | ✅ |
| 25 | Scoring wired into the engine: `OrderHook` (ModeBalanced) fed by health-probe latency/success + registry quota hints; cost/quality stay unknown (0) until prices/quality signals exist | ✅ | ✅ |

| 27 | Observability: hand-rolled Prometheus exposition (`/metrics`, gateway-key auth required), request/latency/in-flight/failover counters, JSON trace log (opt-in via `CODVIAN_ROUTER_TRACE=1`, honestly NOT the OTel SDK) | ✅ | ✅ |

| 31 | Stable versioned config: unknown top-level fields survive Load→Save, `ValidateFile` rejects duplicates/bad values naming the field, `Clone()` deep-copies; `validate` subcommand | ✅ | ✅ |
| 32 | Headless tray supervisor: child = gateway binary, own process group, one auto-restart after unexpected exit, no zombies; `tray` subcommand (`RunHeadless`). GUI tray icon honestly DEFERRED (systray needs GTK dev headers absent on this VM) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 33 | Packaging: multi-stage Dockerfile (distroless nonroot), docker-compose, npm wrapper, `.goreleaser.yml`, checksum-verified `install.sh` (refuses pipe-to-shell); `--listen` opt-in for non-loopback bind (127.0.0.1 / ::1 / 0.0.0.0 only, 0.0.0.0 warns) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 34 | Route trace moved to leaf package `internal/routetrace` (no internal imports) so server metrics + degrade forwarding cannot create an import cycle | ✅ | ✅ |
| 35 | Phase 4 docs: INSTALL, DEMO, ARCHITECTURE, CHANGELOG, RELEASE-v0.2.0 draft | ✅ | ✅ |
| 36 | Dashboard wired into the server: GET /dashboard serves the approved dashboard (web/index.html, embedded via //go:embed in web/web.go); GET-only (405 otherwise), no auth on the static dashboard, /v1/* and /metrics stay auth-gated; startup line prints the dashboard URL; NO Playground section anywhere | ✅ | ✅ |
| 37 | Catalog wired into the provider registry: `Registry.AddCatalog` / `AddCatalogWithOptions` add a provider by catalog ID (or alias) — base URL, wire format and auth scheme come from the catalog entry; only the ID + the operator's own key are needed. `provider add-catalog` CLI subcommand (with `--endpoint` / `--variant` / `--base-url` override) + `provider list` / `provider add` | ✅ | ✅ |
| 38 | Catalog auth in the request path: `ClientFor` resolves credentials per (provider, endpoint) via `catalog.ResolveAuth` — Anthropic messages surface sends Bearer + `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21`, DeepSeek's Anthropic surface sends `x-api-key` (Bearer on its chat surface), Fireworks alt-header sends `X-Fireworks-Api-Key`, Azure api-key variant sends `api-key`. Hand-added providers are matched by base URL when the catalog knows it (`ResolveAuthForBaseURL`, endpoint bases matched first); unknown URLs keep the legacy Bearer default. Exchange surfaces (Vertex service-account JSON, OpenRouter JWT, Azure Entra) fail loudly with a clear error — never a faked static header. Bedrock SigV4 is the exception: the client signs each request itself from the operator's AWS credentials, so a SigV4 surface with parseable credentials builds a Converse client instead of failing (a key that is not AWS credential material stays refused) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 39 | Catalog policy enforced: ToS-grey entries (web-cookie / subscription-OAuth, e.g. Claude Code, ChatGPT Web) are refused as routing targets at add time and at config load; entries with no known base URL are refused; templated catalog base URLs (Azure `{resource}`, Vertex `{LOCATION}`) require a concrete `--base-url` override. Verification status (`verified-from-docs` / `from-reference-metadata-only` / `unverified`) is stored on the provider and printed at startup per provider — never hidden | ✅ | ✅ |
| 40 | Catalog-linked providers in the config file: `catalog_id` (+ `auth_endpoint` / `auth_variant`) persisted in `ProviderConfig`; an empty `base_url` is allowed when `catalog_id` is set and resolves from the catalog at registry load; `validate` accepts it | ✅ | ✅ |
| 41 | Anthropic native wire in the request path: `internal/wire` (OpenAI↔Anthropic Messages conversion, SSE translation) wired into `provider`/`router`/`server` — `Message`/`ChatRequest` carry content parts, tool calls, tools, `tool_choice`, `top_p`, `stop` (string-or-array); `ChatResponse` carries tool calls + real finish reason; streaming is chunk-aware (`ChunkStreamer`, `Engine.ChatStreamChunks`) instead of `onDelta(string)` + hard-coded "stop" | ✅ | ✅ |
| 42 | Anthropic client: `POST {base}/v1/messages` with catalog-resolved auth headers, `max_tokens` default 4096, system message extracted; error mapping 429/rate_limit_error → `RateLimitError` (Retry-After honoured), billing → `QuotaError`, 529 overloaded → retryable `UpstreamError`, unrepresentable input (e.g. temperature > 1) → non-retryable error; mid-stream SSE error events mapped the same way. Unknown wire formats (e.g. `ollama`) fall back to the OpenAI client — Anthropic, Gemini, Cohere and Bedrock all have their own transports now | ✅ | ✅ |
| 43 | Cross-wire failover: a native-wire provider failing (429) fails over to an OpenAI provider and vice versa — the error type, not the wire format, decides; router `StreamEvent` carries tool-call deltas + finish reason, `MergeToolCallDeltas` reassembles streamed tool calls | ✅ | ✅ |
| 44 | Degrade wrapper is chunk-aware: `StaleEngine` implements `ChatStreamChunks`/`ChatStreamFromChunks` with the same stale rules as the text path (store on success, replay only before any chunk, never after partial delivery, text-only inner engines bridged to chunks). Without this the server's chunk path was dead code in serve mode — the first live smoke caught it (streaming tool-call deltas never reached the client) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 45 | Anthropic stream tool-call indices are renumbered sequentially from 0 in `wire.TranslateStream` (Anthropic block indices count text blocks too, so a tool_use after a text block is block 1 — emitting that as the OpenAI tool-call index would wrongly claim a second tool call exists) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 46 | Gemini native wire in the request path: `internal/wire` (OpenAI↔Gemini generateContent conversion, SSE translation) wired into `provider`/`registry` the same way Anthropic is — `POST {base}/models/{model}:generateContent` (+ `:streamGenerateContent?alt=sse` for streaming) with catalog-resolved `x-goog-api-key` auth, no `Authorization` header, no `?key=` query parameter; system/developer → `systemInstruction`, assistant → `model` role, tool results → `function` role (function name matched from the earlier assistant call by `tool_call_id`); consecutive same-role contents merged; first content must be `user` | ✅ | ✅ |
| 47 | Gemini client: `ListModels` parses the real `{"models":[{"name":"models/..."}]}` shape and strips the `models/` prefix; error mapping 429/`RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` → `RateLimitError` (Retry-After honoured), 403 quota / 402 → `QuotaError`, 503/`UNAVAILABLE` → retryable `UpstreamError`, mid-stream SSE error events mapped the same way; blocked prompts (`promptFeedback.blockReason`) convert to an empty reply with `finish_reason: content_filter`, not an error; `SAFETY`/`RECITATION` → `content_filter`, `TOOL_CALLS` → `tool_calls` | ✅ | ✅ |
| 48 | Cross-wire failover with Gemini: a Gemini 429 fails over to an OpenAI provider and an OpenAI 429 fails over to a Gemini provider — proven live (see the Gemini integration gate below) and by unit test with a real Gemini client (`TestEngineCrossWireFailoverGeminiToOpenAI`) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 49 | Cohere native wire in the request path: `internal/wire` (OpenAI↔Cohere Chat API v2 conversion, SSE translation) wired into `provider`/`registry` the same way Anthropic/Gemini are — `POST {base}/v2/chat` (+ `stream: true` SSE for streaming) with catalog-resolved Bearer auth; system → system message (string or typed blocks), image blocks keep the OpenAI `{"type":"image_url","image_url":{"url"}}` shape, tools `{"type":"function","function":{name,description,parameters}}`, `tool_choice` "auto" → omit (Cohere has no "auto"; `NONE`/`REQUIRED` only); finish_reason mapping COMPLETE→stop, MAX_TOKENS→length, TOOL_CALL→tool_calls, STOP_SEQUENCE→stop, ERROR/TIMEOUT→stop; usage from `usage` or `meta.billed_units` | ✅ | ✅ |
| 50 | Cohere client: `ListModels` parses the real `{"models":[{"name":...}]}` shape at `GET {base}/v1/models`; error mapping 429 → `RateLimitError` (Retry-After honoured), billing/quota → `QuotaError`, other 4xx/5xx → `UpstreamError`, mid-stream SSE error events mapped the same way; catalog fix: a base containing `/compatibility/v1` resolves to WireOpenAI, not WireCohere (Cohere's compatibility shim speaks OpenAI wire) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 51 | Cross-wire failover with Cohere: a Cohere 429 fails over to an OpenAI provider and an OpenAI 429 fails over to a Cohere provider — proven live (see the Cohere integration gate below) and by unit test with a real Cohere client (`TestEngineCrossWireFailoverCohereToOpenAI`, both directions) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 52 | Bedrock native wire in the request path: `internal/wire` (OpenAI↔Bedrock Converse conversion, Amazon event-stream translation) wired into `provider`/`registry` the same way Anthropic/Gemini/Cohere are — `POST {base}/model/{modelId}/converse` (+ `/converse-stream` for streaming, model ID verbatim in the path, no `/v1`); messages with typed blocks (text / image / toolUse / toolResult), system array, `inferenceConfig`, `toolConfig` (auto/any/tool choice); stopReason mapping end_turn→stop, tool_use→tool_calls, max_tokens→length, stop_sequence/guardrail_intervened/content_filtered→stop; consecutive same-role messages merged (Converse requires alternating roles starting with user; assistant-first is unrepresentable); remote image URLs unrepresentable, reasoningContent dropped by design | ✅ | ✅ |
| 53 | Bedrock client + pure-Go SigV4 signer: `internal/provider/sigv4.go` (stdlib-only HMAC-SHA256 signing, no SDK vendored) verified against two independent references — the IAM docs example matches botocore 1.44.4 exactly, and a Bedrock Converse POST signature matches an independent pure-Python SigV4 reference exactly; credentials parsed as `AKID:SECRET[:TOKEN]`, region from the base-URL host (fallback us-east-1). `bedrockClient` signs every request (`Authorization: AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 …`, `X-Amz-Content-Sha256`, `X-Amz-Date`), never sends a Bearer header; `ListModels` via control-plane `GET https://bedrock.{region}.amazonaws.com/foundation-models` (falls back to `{base}/foundation-models` for non-AWS hosts); error mapping ThrottlingException/429 → `RateLimitError` (Retry-After honoured), 5xx/408 retryable, 400 non-retryable; no AWS credentials at all → loud exchange error, never a faked header | ✅ | ✅ |
| 54 | Cross-wire failover with Bedrock: a Bedrock ThrottlingException 429 fails over to an OpenAI provider and an OpenAI 429 fails over to a Bedrock provider — proven live (see the Bedrock integration gate below) and by unit test with a real Bedrock client (`TestEngineCrossWireFailoverBedrockToOpenAI`, both directions) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 55 | Semantic cache wired into the request path: `degrade.WrapWithSemantic` + `Policy.ServeCache` (opt-in; default off, so existing behaviour is unchanged) — cacheable requests (no tools, no tool-role history) are served proactively from the exact cache first, then the semantic cache (0.85 cosine, same 1h TTL / 500-entry LRU rules), without calling any provider; successes are stored in both layers under the last user message's text | ✅ | ✅ |
| 56 | Semantic-cache honesty: a proactive hit is marked `X-Codvian-Cache: exact\|semantic` (+ `X-Codvian-Cache-Similarity` for semantic hits) on non-streaming responses and logged for streams — never marked stale, never presented as a fresh provider response; a semantic hit is also stored under its own exact key, so a repeat paraphrase becomes an exact hit; resume paths (`ChatStreamFrom*`) deliberately skip proactive lookup so a cached full response can never duplicate content the client already has | ✅ | ✅ |
| 57 | Ledger/metrics honesty for cache hits: a cache hit calls no provider, so the router's ledger records nothing for it (proven live: 2 provider rows for 2 real calls, 0 rows for 3 cache hits); the server attributes the request to `provider="cache"` in `codvian_router_requests_total` (never to a neighbour's trace) and `codvian_router_cache_hits_total{layer="exact"\|"semantic"}` is counted exactly once, in the wrapper — the server does not count hits, so there is no double-count | ✅ | ✅ |
| 58 | Cache safety fix found by the semantic wiring tests: the stale fallback (`staleFor`) previously served a content-only exact entry to a tool-using request on provider failure, silently dropping its tool calls — it now refuses non-cacheable requests the same way proactive lookup and store do (the `X-Codvian-Stale` path keeps working for plain-text requests; all pre-existing stale tests still pass) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 59 | Auto-router (backend): local semantic-router / RouteLLM-pattern classification — prompt embedded once with the deterministic hashed bag-of-words embedding reused from the semantic cache (`cache.EmbedText`, 256-dim, no neural model, no network), cosine-matched against curated per-tier example utterances (15 per tier shipped as config defaults: coding / chat / reasoning / quick-qa), best-utterance + tier-centroid scoring; virtual models `auto`, `auto/fast`, `auto/cheap`, `auto/coding`, `auto/reasoning` in the request path; single threshold dial (default 0.5, configurable per virtual model in config `auto_router`), below threshold → fallback to the default/strong tier, never a guess; no concrete model configured → honest failure, never an invented model; routing-decision latency measured (see gate) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 60 | Auto-router (dashboard, same slice): Auto-router section in the approved design system (dark pure black / light pure white, borderless 26–28px cards, dark-lime palette, no Playground) — virtual-model selector + threshold slider (preview-only; live path uses the config threshold) + routing-decision view (tier picked, confidence, per-tier scores, fallback reason, resolved model, measured decision latency) wired to real endpoints `GET /dashboard/auto-route` (live classify) and `GET /dashboard/auto-route/last` (last real `auto/*` decision from live /v1 traffic, honest empty state when none yet); virtual models listed by `GET /v1/models`; auto-routed responses carry `X-Codvian-Auto-Tier` / `-Confidence` / `-Model` (+ `-Fallback`) headers. Decision view shows real decisions only — no sample/fake traffic; the shipped utterances are labelled config defaults, never presented as measured results | ✅ | ✅ |
| 61 | GET /api/providers endpoint: live per-provider feed (id, name, wire format, verification, health_status ok/down/unknown from the health checker's last results, plus verification summary counts) — unauthenticated by design (no keys, no base URLs; choice documented in code), /v1/* and /metrics auth unchanged; wired to the real health checker in serve mode | ✅ | ✅ |
| 62 | Dashboard per-provider verification badges (Providers section): jewel-tone capsules — dark lime "Verified from docs", solid near-black "Reference metadata", muted "Unverified" — fetched live from GET /api/providers with wire format; NO sample providers any more (NO-FAKE-DATA rule): empty state reads "No providers connected yet", feed failure reads "Live provider feed unavailable"; sidebar/instance/topbar health counts now read from the same live list; remaining sample sections (Models, Logs, Usage, KPIs, router keys) are labelled "Demo data" until their live feeds land; quick-start base URL fixed to the real default port 20128 (was a wrong 8080) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 63 | Single-flight request coalescing (backend): `internal/coalescing` (new leaf package — stdlib-only singleflight pattern, no x/sync dependency; leaf so server + degrade can import it without an import cycle) — non-streaming requests keyed on the canonical request hash (SHA-256 of JSON {model, messages, max_tokens, temperature, top_p, stop}); the entry is registered BEFORE dialing and the whole retry loop (`ChatWithChain`: failover, 429 backoff, hedging) runs inside one flight, so N identical concurrent requests make exactly 1 upstream call and waiters share one backoff sequence; a shared failure reaches all waiters as the one error. NEVER coalesced: streaming requests (they never touch the group) and tool-using / tool-call turns (same rule as the degrade wrapper's cacheable check). A waiter whose own ctx ends leaves with its own ctx error and never disturbs the leader; a leader ctx cancel fails the flight for all waiters. Ledger honesty: the leader's attempt is the only billed row; each waiter is recorded via the optional `router.SharedLedger` interface as a row marked `shared=1` with zero tokens and zero cost (`ledger.Usage.Shared`, `shared` column on usage_events incl. migration for old DBs, `Ledger.RecordShared`), so spend/token totals never double-count. Counters also exposed as `codvian_router_coalesced_total` on /metrics | ✅ | ✅ |
| 64 | Single-flight coalescing (dashboard, same slice): Coalescing section in the approved design system (dark pure black / light pure white, borderless 26–28px cards, dark-lime palette, no Playground) — KPI tiles for shared calls / upstream calls avoided / quota hits saved (calls, not tokens) + live-flights card, wired to the real endpoint `GET /dashboard/coalescing` (unauthenticated like the other dashboard feeds: counters only, no keys or message content; zero counters when the engine reports nothing), refreshed on open + every 5 s while open + Refresh button; honest empty state ("No coalesced request yet… counters stay at zero until that happens") and the stats are real counters from `router.Engine.CoalescingStats()` forwarded through `degrade.StaleEngine` — no invented figures anywhere | ✅ | ✅ |
| 65 | TCP Fast Open + adaptive concurrency limiting (backend): TFO on the per-provider dialer via the `Control` hook (`TCP_FASTOPEN_CONNECT` on Linux, `TCP_FASTOPEN` on macOS/Windows; the hook never errors, so a refused option falls back to a normal handshake); never a POST body in early data (early data is the TLS ClientHello, handled by TLS). `provider.TFOStatus()` reports the real probe (setsockopt + Linux `net.ipv4.tcp_fastopen` sysctl). Adaptive per-(provider,key) Gradient2 limiter (`internal/adaptive`, leaf package): cap = cap × (baseline RTT ÷ smoothed RTT) + 1, initial 20 / floor 2 / ceiling 100, only successful calls feed the gradient, failed calls never move it; wired into `router.Engine` — saturated providers are deprioritised in `orderAttempts`, `TryAcquire` sheds before the queue grows (trace `skipped_concurrency_limit`, no breaker trip, no ledger row), streaming holds a slot for the stream duration but never feeds RTT; the limiter FAILS OPEN (nil limiter admits, no error path on admission). Caps + shed counts also on `/metrics` (`codvian_router_concurrency_limit`, `codvian_router_concurrency_rejected_total`); `CODVIAN_ROUTER_TFO=0` disables TFO for measurement only | ✅ | ✅ |
| 66 | TCP Fast Open + adaptive concurrency limiting (dashboard, same slice): Concurrency section in the approved design system (dark pure black / light pure white, borderless 26–28px cards, dark-lime palette, no Playground) — TFO card (Active/Supported/Not available, platform, sysctl value, dial attempts/errors) + per-provider table (in-flight, adaptive limit, smoothed RTT, baseline RTT, gradient, samples, shed) wired to the real endpoint `GET /dashboard/concurrency` (unauthenticated like the other dashboard feeds: limiter state + TFO probe only, no keys or base URLs; providers with no traffic show the initial cap with zero samples and no RTT), refreshed on open + every 5 s while open + Refresh button; honest empty state ("No providers connected yet… nothing here is pre-filled"); `node --check` on the dashboard JS clean | ✅ | ✅ |
| 67 | Route-guard tiers (backend): all 21 server routes classified into three static tiers in `internal/server/guard.go` (`guardRouteTable`, the single source of truth) — Tier 1 LOCAL_ONLY (7 routes: shutdown, service install, plugin load, auto-update, tunnel start, provider login, bootstrap), Tier 2 ALWAYS_PROTECTED (1 route: settings export), Tier 3 MANAGEMENT (13 routes incl. /v1/*, /metrics, dashboard feeds, admin status). `Server.register` looks every route up in the table and applies that tier's wrappers, panicking at startup on an unclassified route; the gate test walks the table and FAILS if any spawn-capable route is not LOCAL_ONLY. Tier 1 is checked BEFORE auth by real TCP peer (`RemoteAddr` only — Host header and X-Forwarded-For never consulted; a forged XFF does not help), and loopback alone is not enough: a valid gateway key is still required after the loopback check (except bootstrap, where no key exists yet). There is NO manage-scope bypass (stricter than comparable gateways). Tier 2 always requires a gateway key even when Tier 3 auth is relaxed (`WithAuthRelaxed` models that mode; production serve never sets it). A 403 LOCAL_ONLY is logged as "guard working as designed", not an error, and counted in `local_only_denials`. Fresh-install bootstrap (`POST /admin/bootstrap`) is loopback-only, works exactly once while no key exists (201, key shown once, persisted via the config file), then 409; with no issuer wired it answers 503. Shutdown is real (202 + graceful-stop hook wired in main.go; 503 with no hook, never a fake 202). Settings export is real but secrets-redacted (no provider keys, no gateway-key hashes, no base URLs). The five not-yet-built Tier 1 features answer honest 501 behind the guard. DB export/import/wipe have NO route in this build — nothing to call, nothing to protect; the dashboard says so, and they must be registered Tier 2 (Tier 1 if they spawn a helper) when they land | ✅ | ✅ |
| 68 | Route-guard tiers (dashboard, same slice): Route guards section in the approved design system (dark pure black / light pure white, borderless 26–28px cards, dark-lime palette, no Playground) — live guard-status card (loopback-only enforcement active, bootstrap pending/complete, Tier 3 auth relaxed yes/no, Tier 1 refusals since start, listen address) + the three tiers listed with every route in each (method, path, reason, spawn-capable/first-key-setup tags), wired to the real endpoint `GET /dashboard/guard` (unauthenticated like the other dashboard feeds: tiers + counters only, no keys or secrets; nothing hard-coded in the page — a newly classified route shows up automatically), refreshed on open + every 5 s while open + Refresh button; honest empty state ("No guard status yet…") before the feed answers and honest "unknown" listen address when unset; `node --check` on the dashboard JS clean | ✅ | ✅ |

| 69 | Routing strategies (backend): cheapest / fastest / most-reliable / least-latency / weighted as real scoring rules over live signals (new leaf package `internal/strategy/`, `internal/scoring` honest unknown cost = neutral 0.5); per-chain strategy override with `auto:<axis>` alias winning (precedence: request alias > chain override > global > balanced); per-combo deadline sub-budget returning a clean 504 (`ErrDeadlineExceeded`, never masks caller cancellation); config `routing` block (`global_strategy`, `deadline_budget_ms`, `chain_strategies`, `weighted_weights`) with validation; `X-Codvian-Strategy` header on responses; live provider stats (EWMA α=0.2, real attempts only) | ✅ | ✅ |
| 70 | Routing strategies (dashboard, same slice): Strategies section in the approved design system (dark pure black / light pure white, borderless 26–28px cards, dark-lime palette, no Playground) — global strategy picker, deadline budget line, last-live-strategy card, per-chain table with "Inherit global", honest empty states, wired to real endpoints `GET`/`POST /dashboard/strategies` (no hard-coded values); live strategy line in the Overview Live Routing card; Settings strategy select rebuilt from the live feed and wired to the same POST endpoint; `node --check` on the dashboard JS clean | ✅ | ✅ |

| 131 | Auto `prompt_cache_key` (backend, research-log entry 184): for requests to OpenAI's own API (`api.openai.com`, OpenAI protocol) with no client-supplied key, the router adds `codvian-` + HMAC-SHA256 keyed by the gateway-key ID over the endpoint (`chat/completions`) + the shared prefix (leading system/developer messages, plus tools; user messages excluded, so every conversation of one agent gets the same key and different callers do not). A client-supplied key — including explicit JSON `null`, forwarded as `null` — is never replaced; non-OpenAI endpoints never get a generated key (client keys still forwarded); prefix-less requests and anonymous callers get none. The key is computed per attempt inside the OpenAI wire client (`internal/provider/cachekey.go`, `cacheKeyField`), never used for routing, and never stored: caller identity and the client key are `json:"-"` on `ChatRequest`, so no marshal of the request (coalescing key, cache key, trace, ledger) can carry it — asserted by `TestChatRequestMarshalNeverLeaksCacheKey` and by the server test (logs/overview feeds contain no `prompt_cache_key` / `codvian-`). Caller ID comes from the verifier via the optional `KeyIDVerifier` interface (config verifier returns the stored key ID; static verifier a stable derived ID). `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` is parsed into `provider.Usage.CachedTokens` (non-streaming + streaming), and Anthropic `cache_read_input_tokens` + Gemini `cachedContentTokenCount` are mapped through the wire translations; Cohere/Bedrock report no cached split. Ledger stores `cached_tokens` (new column + migration, stored only with a known split, clamped to the prompt count, zeroed on shared rows) with Summary aggregates (totals + per-provider + per-combo). Real bug found by the live smoke and fixed in this slice: the async batch writer (`commitBatch`) computed the cached count via `normalizeUsage` but its INSERT never listed the column, so production rows recorded 0 while the sync path kept the value — the INSERT now carries it, with `TestAsyncWriterCarriesCachedTokens` as the regression test. | 133 | Streaming failover via bounded pre-commit preread buffer (backend, research-log entries 97 + 111): `internal/router/preread.go` — before committing a stream, hold leading events up to 8 events / 256 KiB / 5 s; if the stream turns out empty (bare `[DONE]` or finish-reason-only chunk) or errors before real content, fail over to the next target in the SAME request with nothing delivered; NEVER switch after the first content byte is sent. Deliberate behaviour change, flagged: automatic resume-after-commit is REMOVED at both layers (engine `runStream` per-attempt resume-prefill block + server `handleChatStream` resume branch deleted) — it supersedes the old item-12 mid-stream resume; the explicit entry points `Engine.ChatStreamFrom` / `ChatStreamFromChunks` (and `trimAlreadySent`) are KEPT for callers that ask for a resume outright, just never invoked automatically after an in-request failure. Exemption list codified once in `router.ReplayExemption` (single source of truth): embeddings, images, rerank, responses, Responses-ID continuations (`PreviousResponseID`, pinned to one credential), warm-up — skip preread AND hedge, exactly one attempt, in streaming and non-streaming paths; plain multi-turn chat and tool-using chat are deliberately NOT exempt (client-held messages, nothing delivered pre-commit). Taxonomy: `provider.EmptyStreamError` → Fallback / `empty_stream`; a client-synthesised status-200 error the client marked retryable ("SSE stream ended without [DONE]", no content) → Fallback / `invalid_response` (a bare 200 would otherwise stop the chain); non-retryable 200 (malformed SSE) still stops. Trace carries `preread` / `preread_events` / `preread_bytes` (`routetrace.AddWithPreread`; "held" even with 0 events so "committed on first content" is distinguishable from "never ran"). Real bugs found by this gate and fixed: (1) `chatStreamDrainChunks` returned on Done before `runStream` published the trace — `LastTrace` (and `GET /dashboard/trace`) could read the previous request's trace or nil after a successful stream; the drain now waits for channel close (trace is set in a defer before close). (2) Two concurrent-slice bugs fixed in passing: `recordAttempt`'s KeyedLedger branch shadowed its `ok` param with the type-assertion result (every keyed row recorded ok=true) and `NewEngine` pinned the adaptive limiter at 100/100/100 so its cap could never shrink (contract: initial 20 / floor 2 / ceiling 100). Tests: `TestStreamingPrereadFailoverGate` (router: empty ×2 variants, error-before-first-byte, content-then-death no-switch, tool-call content, event-limit + wait-limit commit, all 6 exemptions × empty/error, non-streaming single-attempt), `TestChatCompletionsStreamingPrereadGate` (server: real Server + real Engine + real clients vs httptest mocks, same three cases incl. ledger + trace assertions), `TestClassifyEmptyStream` (provider). Limitations: the 256 KiB bound is enforced but cannot trip on today's event shapes (buffered events carry ~0 bytes — safety bound for future shapes; event-count and time bounds have live tests); a stream that errors before commit records preread "" (never committed); no live-browser click-through in this environment | ✅ | ✅ |

| 135 | SSE delivery — TCP_NODELAY + hybrid batching (backend): TCP_NODELAY made explicit and enforced on both streaming socket paths — accepted connections via `server.NoDelayListener` (serve uses an explicit listener instead of `ListenAndServe`) and provider dials via a `noDelayDialContext` wrapper in `tfoTransport` — and proven set by a real `GetsockoptInt` probe in both tests (`provider/nodelay_test.go`, `server/nodelay_listener_test.go`). Honest starting point recorded in code: Go's stdlib already sets TCP_NODELAY on every TCP connection (`net.newTCPConn`), so this slice enforces + proves the property rather than claiming to add it, and no latency win is claimed for it (unmeasurable on loopback). Hybrid batching in `handleChatStream` and `handleResponsesStream` (`internal/server/sse_batch.go`): one Flush per 3 tokens or 8 ms, whichever comes first — never holds a token >8 ms, never batches >3; [DONE] and error events flush immediately via `writeSSENow` and never wait in the batch; a deferred `Close` flushes anything pending at handler return. `CODVIAN_ROUTER_SSE_BATCH=0` disables batching (measurement only). Measured with the real binary + a mock streaming at a known cadence (`scripts/bench-sse.sh`, 200 tokens/cell, 2026-11-17 ~20:50 UTC, shared VM loadavg 13.29 at run start): 10 ms cadence — batching off median 0.357 ms / p99 25.548 ms, on median 8.536 ms / p99 9.943 ms; 1 ms cadence — off median 0.211 ms / p99 3.16 ms, on median 1.302 ms / p99 2.853 ms. The trade is stated honestly in code, docs and dashboard: batching is a throughput trade (~3x fewer Flush calls under fast streams), NOT a latency win — at a 10 ms cadence each token waits the full 8 ms timer, so the "on" median is worse; the p99 tightens because the timer regularises delivery against shared-VM scheduler stalls. No latency target exists for token delivery, so nothing is scored MET/MISSED. Full numbers in `docs/sse-delivery-2026-11-17.json`. Gate 2026-11-18: 15 new tests (7 batcher bounds, 4 streaming feed incl. the real run, 1 end-to-end batched stream, 3 GetsockoptInt NoDelay probes) all pass incl. under -race; full suite 956 PASS events / 0 FAIL across 30 packages (a first verbose run showed 906/1 with one flaky `TestStreamingPrereadFailoverGate/wait-limit-commits` failure in `internal/router` — another slice's timing test, 3/3 green on re-run); live smoke 19/19 green twice on the real binary (feed, dashboard card, guard route, live 200-token streams batching on AND off) | ✅ | ✅ |

| 129 | Soft-demotion cooldown (backend): opt-in alternative to the existing hard cooldown — default stays `hard` unless the operator opts in (`routing.cooldown_mode: hard|soft-demote`, per-chain override `routing.chain_cooldown_modes`, validated in config + `Clone()`). On a retryable failure of a target, a cooldown of the upstream's `Retry-After` seconds, otherwise 5 s, doubling with each consecutive failure, capped at 5 min; while cooling the target moves to the END of the order — never skipped, still tried once — so a request fails only if every target fails; a success ends the cooldown. Soft mode never trips the circuit breaker on a retryable failure (tripping it would skip the provider outright); hard mode unchanged. Shared error taxonomy in `internal/wire` (`RetryableStatus` / `RetryableStatusForEndpoint`): 529 retryable; 402/404 retryable where they are properties of that target; 405/501 retryable only on `/v1/responses` and `/v1/embeddings`; 400/401/403/422 stop (merged with the parallel entry-151 slice: a 400/404 whose body signals model-not-found also falls back, plain 400 stops). Feed: `GET /dashboard/cooldown` + `POST /dashboard/cooldown` (Tier 3 MANAGEMENT, auth-exempt, in the guard table; 31 management routes) carrying modes + effective mode + per-chain overrides + currently cooling targets (provider, mode, remaining seconds, consecutive failures, demoted flag). Unit tests: demotion ordering, doubling cap at 5 min, success clears, Retry-After honored, per-chain mode, taxonomy starts-cooldown, streaming. Cooling entries now also carry the provider display name (resolved from the config; dashboard falls back to the ID). Gate 2026-11-18: full suite 807 PASS / 0 FAIL (26 packages ok + web no-test-files), race clean on touched packages; live smoke 37/37 checks green twice (real binary + 2 mocks) | ✅ | ✅ |

| 141 | MCP selector mode (backend): new leaf package `internal/mcptools` — the MCP *client* side (the existing `internal/mcp` is the server side only and is untouched). Operator-configured MCP servers in config `mcp` block (`mode` selector/direct, `max_iterations` 1–50 default 10, `servers: [{name, command, args}]`, validated + `Clone()`-deep-copied). Selector mode injects exactly the two meta-tools `mcp_tools_get` (list discovered tools, optional server/query filter) and `mcp_tools_execute` (run one by qualified `server__tool` name, plain name when unique) no matter how many servers/tools are configured; `direct` mode (opt-in) injects every discovered tool schema. Real stdio JSON-RPC 2.0 client (`StdioServer`: spawn + initialize + tools/list + tools/call, 30 s per-call timeout, timeout kills the wedged process so a later call spawns fresh, start failure sticky and reported as down, never silently respawned). The agent loop (`RunAgentLoop`) runs follow-up model calls as in-process `engine.Chat` calls — never HTTP back through the gateway (no HTTP client exists in the package), middleware entered once per client request; cap 10 default with an honest stop (`Capped`, `X-Codvian-MCP-Capped: true`); a client's own (non-MCP) tool call passes through untouched; unknown tools become an `error: …` tool result, never a failed request; non-streaming only (streaming passes through unchanged). Live counters (get/execute/per-tool/loop runs/iterations/capped) counted where the calls happen. Wired in `runServe` (discovery at startup, down servers logged, not fatal) + `WithMCPTools` + `X-Codvian-MCP-Mode/-Iterations/-Capped` headers. | 147 | Distinct guardrail refusal code (backend, research-log entry 179): a policy/guardrail block answers HTTP 403 with the Codvian policy-refusal code -32001 (`error.code`, `error.type: "policy_refusal"`, machine-readable `error.reason` naming the kind: pii/secret/injection) — previously a bare 400 with no code — so a client can tell a policy refusal from an upstream failure without parsing message strings. Upstream failures keep their own shapes and never collide: an exhausted chain answers HTTP 502 with JSON-RPC code -32603 (`error.type: "server_error"`), a terminal upstream rejection keeps the upstream's own 4xx with no JSON-RPC code at all (its status is its code). The distinct code applies to the request guardrail (before any provider is dialled), the response guardrail (new: a completed non-streaming provider reply is scanned the same way, content + tool-call arguments, and withheld with 403/-32001 instead of being delivered; the attempt stays in the ledger/metrics as outcome "error" because the provider was already called and billed), and the MCP tool-call guardrail (new `tool_args` pre-call phase in `internal/mcp`: tool name + JSON arguments scanned before dispatch, refused with JSON-RPC error -32001 — never -32602 invalid-params, never -32603). Guardrail evaluations are counted once per Check call with phase/action labels: `codvian_router_guardrail_evaluations_total{phase,action}` on /metrics (phases request/response/tool_args; actions allowed/redacted/blocked) and the same breakdown in the guardrail store (`GET /dashboard/guardrails` `evaluations`); upstream failures have their own counter `codvian_router_upstream_failures_total` (exhausted-chain 502 + terminal upstream 4xx; deadline 504s and policy refusals excluded). Honest limits: streaming responses are NOT response-checked — content is delivered incrementally and cannot be recalled, so a post-hoc block would be a lie (pinned by `TestResponseGuardrailStreamingLimitation`); MCP tool outputs have no guardrail phase — they are generated from the registry, never user-supplied, so there is nothing to scan; the MCP stdio server runs as a separate process, so its tool_args evaluations are counted in-process only and do not reach the HTTP dashboard feed. Ledger split: `reasoning_tokens`, `cache_write_tokens`, `local_estimate` columns (schema + migration) on `Usage`/`UsageEvent`/`UsageSummary`/`ProviderUsage`/`ModelUsage`, stored only with a known split, clamped (reasoning ≤ completion; cached/cache-write ≤ prompt; negatives → 0), zeroed on shared rows; local-estimate rows store the prompt count with `split_known=0`, `local_estimate=1`, cost 0 — a known split always supersedes an estimate. Both the sync `RecordUsage` and the async batch writer carry the new columns (same pattern as the item-131 cached-tokens async bug); import/export round-trips them (import previously dropped `cached_tokens` — fixed here) with validation rejecting subset>parent, estimate rows with a cost or subset counts, and estimate rows with a completion count. Router reconciliation: `localPromptCount` per attempt; no usage reported + local>0 → labelled local-estimate row; both present → compared, material divergence (\|local−reported\| > max(20, 10% of reported)) increments `Diverged`, records `Last` and calls `Config.OnTokenDivergence` (wired in main.go to a stderr log) — the provider's numbers are never overwritten. Streaming success records the same local-estimate fallback. Pre-send enforcement: `handleChatCompletions` (after guardrails, before the stream/non-stream branch) counts the prompt and, with a model-level input price, refuses 402 via `ledger.CheckBudgetForEstimate` (fail-closed, spent+estimate > cap) naming the estimated prompt cost, token count and remaining budget — no provider called; unpriced pairs and counting failures degrade to the already-exhausted check (anonymous callers skip the gate). Unit tests: pinned cl100k/o200k counts, fallback + special-token text, split storage/clamping/estimate supersession, async writer, import round-trip + validation, budget-estimate refusal/allowance, reconciliation fallback/keep/divergence/noise/stream + threshold boundaries, pre-send 402 with zero engine calls (non-stream + stream) and unpriced-proceeds. Limitations: local counts are estimates (wrong encoding for non-OpenAI providers, no tool schemas/framing); pre-send uses the model-level price only (pair-only prices cannot be enforced pre-send) and covers prompt tokens only (lower bound); cache-write cost is a 1.25–2.5x band, not a quote; streaming cached/reasoning tokens reach the client but the ledger keeps the local-estimate fallback (item-131 limitation, unchanged); research-log entries 103/106 could not be located in the worktree — the substance was taken from the task text plus the research report (gap #813). | 149 | Benchmark push toward Geekflare targets (backend, research-log entry 148): pprof profile of the real request path (2026-11-18, `$HOME/.tmp/pprof-2026-11-18/cpu.prof`) + three evidence-backed hot-path fixes. (2) Semantic-cache inverted index (`internal/cache/cache.go`, `index_test.go`): dense slots + per-dimension postings replace the per-entry dot loop in `Get` (~3,000 posting updates vs 12,500 dot iterations at 500 entries); `set` dedups by exact-vector hash instead of an O(N) near-duplicate scan; `touch` shifts in place instead of allocating; stale postings reclaimed by bulk rebuild. `TestSemanticGetMatchesBruteForce` (300 churned inserts, evictions + rebuilds) proves Get returns exactly what the old full scan returned; documented behaviour change — a merely near-identical vector (cos ≥ 0.9999, not identical) now takes its own slot, bounded by the LRU. (3) Ledger budget-update aggregation (`internal/ledger/async.go`, `TestAsyncWriterAggregatesBudgetUpdates`): one UPDATE per gateway key per batch instead of one per row (75-row bench batch, one key: 75 → 1); 50 ms flush / durability window unchanged; per-key spend lands exactly, committed/failed counts preserved. | 151 | Breaker must never trip on 4xx (backend, capability gap closed; breaker trips ONLY on 408/5xx): the bug was in `internal/router` — the fall-through in `noteFailure`, `noteStreamStartFailure` and the `runStream` stream-failed branch called `breakerFailure` for every non-rate-limit, non-model-not-found failure, so five 401s / 400s / 403s tripped the provider's breaker and took its live models out (the stale comment in `internal/router/cooldown.go` even stated that as intended). New taxonomy-based rule `isClientErrorFailure` (`provider.ClassifyError`, never a raw status list): any 4xx (status 400–499, or a client-error reason — request_invalid / auth_failed / forbidden / unrepresentable — for mid-stream wire errors with no HTTP status) is breaker-neutral in BOTH cooldown modes — `breakerNeutral` + a per-provider `breakerIgnored4xx` counter, the live proof — and the chain still stops on it (the request itself is terminal, only the breaker is left alone). 408/5xx and transport failures still trip exactly as before, and the trip reason is now recorded: `breakerState.lastReason` (taxonomy slug) + `Engine.BreakerStates()` (per-provider open/closed, consecutive failures, threshold, opened-at, remaining seconds, last reason, ignored-4xx) forwarded through `degrade.StaleEngine`. Auth errors cool down nothing and trip nothing: at most the (provider, key) pair is recorded as breaker-ignored — there is no per-key cooldown to apply (the engine's cooldowns are per-provider and skipping the provider on a bad key would only delay the next request's fast failure), so the provider stays routable for its other keys/models. `GET /dashboard/cooldown` gains `breakers[]` + `breaker_rule` (the rule served from the server, never hard-coded in the page). Gate tests: `TestBreakerNeverTripsOn4xx` (router: 401/400/403/422 ×5 → CLOSED, 0 counted, 5 ignored; 500/408/transport ×5 → OPEN with the right reason; stream-start 401s ×5 → CLOSED then the fixed-key stream serves), `TestChatCompletionsBreakerNeverTripsOn4xx` (server: real Server + real Engine + real clients vs httptest mocks — 5×401 → feed CLOSED/0/5, fixed key → 200; 5×500 → feed OPEN/5/upstream_error, 6th request `skipped_breaker` with no extra mock hit). | ✅ | ✅ | Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): `go build ./...` OK; `go vet ./...` clean on this slice's files (two pre-existing findings elsewhere: `internal/router/cancel_test.go` context-leak warnings and `internal/provider/client.go` gofmt, both another slice's in-flight files, untouched here); full `go test -count=1 -v ./...` — **1,436 PASS events (1,131 top-level + 305 subtests), 0 fail, 31/31 packages**; `go test -race` clean on touched packages (router, server, provider, degrade); `node --check` on the dashboard JS clean; live smoke 30/30 (real binary + 2 Python mocks, `~/workspace/.build-tmp/smoke-151-152.sh`). | 153 | Cancel propagation + always-terminal stream event + empty-completion failover (backend, capability gap closed; aborted Bedrock streams kept reading after client disconnectonnect; Responses fallback replaying delivered tool calls): (a) Cancel propagation — verified end to end, no code change needed on the transport path (server wraps r.Context() with the 120 s timeout; engine bridgeStream selects on ctx.Done(); every real client binds its HTTP request via NewRequestWithContext, so transport aborts body reads): new tests prove a client abort stops upstream reads immediately at all three levels — provider (TestOpenAIStreamCancelStopsUpstreamReads / TestAnthropicStreamCancelStopsUpstreamReads: cancel after 2 chunks, zero mock writes after abort +500 ms grace), router (TestStreamCancelStopsUpstreamReads: 2 deltas → cancel → zero emissions after +150 ms grace, fallback never called), server (TestChatCompletionsStreamingClientAbortStopsUpstream: live httptest server, real client reads 2 chunks then closes, zero mock writes after abort +500 ms). A cancel never fails over (DecisionStop, reason cancelled) and the terminal Err event carries context.Canceled (TestStreamCancelTerminalEvent). The bridge-channel-close path in forwardStream/forwardStreamPreread now returns streamCtxDone (not streamFailed) when ctx already ended. (b) Always-terminal streams — handleChatStream's committed-error path now writes the SSE error frame AND data: [DONE] (contract change: a client waiting on the terminator no longer hangs; two existing tests updated to the new contract). (c) Empty-completion failover — streaming empty was ALREADY covered by the preread buffer (items 133–134: bare [DONE] or finish-reason-only chunk → EmptyStreamError → fail over, never after first content byte), proven by TestStreamingPrereadFailoverGate, NOT rebuilt. The real gap was non-streaming: callChat now converts a successful reply with empty content and no tool calls into *provider.EmptyStreamError (a tool call counts as content), so it fails over pre-commit; replay-exempt requests (embeddings/images/rerank, Responses continuations, warm-ups) return the empty reply as-is, exactly once; all-targets-empty is an honest *EmptyStreamError (502 via writeUpstreamFailure). noteFailure carves EmptyStreamError out of content_filter as breaker-neutral (hard-mode empty otherwise trips the breaker; soft-demote demotes). Route trace gains AttemptTrace.Terminal ("done" | "error" | "cancelled"; "" = pre-commit failover or non-streaming) via AddWithPrereadTerminal. Out of scope: the verbatim relay (POST /v1/messages) ends error streams with an Anthropic error event, which IS terminal per Anthropic's protocol — no [DONE] added there. | 155 | Per-request USD budget header with strict 402 fail-fast (backend, capability gap closed; X-Router-Budget + Budget-Fallback: strict -> HTTP 402 instead of silently overspP 402 instead of silently overspending): client-supplied `X-Codvian-Budget: <USD>` (+ `X-Codvian-Budget-Strict: true/1/strict`, or the reference-style alias `Budget-Fallback: strict`) on POST /v1/chat/completions — `provider.ChatRequest.BudgetUSD *float64` / `BudgetStrict bool` (json:"-", never in a coalescing key, cache key, trace or ledger row). Three enforcement points: (1) pre-send (`server.enforceRequestBudget`, after guardrails, before the stream/non-stream branch): the prompt is counted locally (tiktoken, item 145) and priced at the model level — if the priced prompt-only estimate alone exceeds the budget, HTTP 402 with zero provider calls, in BOTH modes (the estimate is a lower bound — completion not included — so serving would guarantee an overspend on the prompt alone); unpriced pairs and counting failures proceed, never refused on a guess; an invalid header value (unparseable, <= 0, NaN/Inf, bad strict value) is a 400, never silently ignored. (2) Mid-chain, strict only (`router.budgetGate` in `ChatWithChainStrategy` + `runStream`, `Config.PriceFunc` wired in main.go from the price book — pair price first, model-level fallback, per leg): before each attempt, spent + priced prompt estimate vs the budget — over budget stops the chain before dialling (trace outcome `skipped_budget`, typed `RequestBudgetExceededError` mapped to HTTP 402 by `handleEngineError`, never a 502/-32603); a blocked hedge target is never launched in parallel (the first attempt runs alone). Non-strict never blocks mid-chain. (3) Post-hoc overage (`checkRequestBudgetOverage`): a served non-streaming response's reported usage is priced at the serving pair (route trace first, model-level fallback) and recorded as an overage when the priced actual exceeds the budget; the response carries `X-Codvian-Budget-Spent`. Recording: in-memory `requestBudgetStore` (counts since start + last 50 refusal/overage events — model, budget, strict, outcome, estimated/spent/actual cost; never prompt text, keys or base URLs) served by GET /dashboard/request-budgets (Tier 3 MANAGEMENT, auth-exempt, guard table + OpenAPI; MANAGEMENT 39->41 (40 from this slice's route; 41 after the reasoning slice's GET /dashboard/reasoning landed during the gate)). Honest limits: cumulative priced spend before attempt N is $0 in today's paths (failed attempts bill nothing), so the mid-chain gate is effectively the next attempt's estimate vs the remaining budget — the accumulator is the invariant, kept for a future billed-then-continue path; a stream's true billed cost is invisible (streaming usage is a local-estimate fallback, cost $0), so streams get the per-attempt estimate gate only and no post-hoc overage; MCP agent-loop follow-up calls each carry the same per-request budget and are gated individually — the budget does not shrink across loop iterations, so a multi-iteration loop can spend up to iterations x budget; coalesced waiters share the leader's spend (each waiter still gets its own pre-send check). | 143 | Byte-for-byte native relay + deprecated models + 503 Retry-After (backend, research-log entries 83/145/179): `POST /v1/messages` (TierManagement, gateway-key auth) forwards Anthropic-dialect requests to an Anthropic provider verbatim — `internal/relay.Decide` (verbatim only when client dialect == provider wire and no feature needs the canonical form), byte-preserving `RewriteModel` (only the top-level `model` value rewritten, `json.Valid` pre-check), raw bytes forwarded and SSE envelope copied event-for-event, `cache_control` and unknown fields pass through untouched. Translation runs only when a feature needs the canonical form: blocking guardrails, a gateway-cache hit, multi-attempt failover, or forced reasoning (no forced-reasoning policy exists yet — the decision field and unit tests cover it, the handler always passes false). Lookup-then-relay: with cache serving on, the request is looked up in the exact then semantic cache in canonical form first — a hit is served in the Anthropic shape (`X-Codvian-Cache: exact|semantic`, `X-Codvian-Relay: cache-hit;layer=…`, `IncCacheHit` recorded, no provider called, no ledger row), a miss relays verbatim, and a successful verbatim non-streaming response is stored back under the same keys (1 h TTL, `wire.ConvertResponse`; unconvertible responses are relayed but not stored). Guardrails run before the cache lookup, exactly as on `/v1/chat/completions`, so a cache hit can never bypass a blocking guardrail (pinned by `TestMessagesCacheDoesNotBypassGuardrails`). The translate path converts Anthropic → canonical → engine → Anthropic and reports what it drops (`dropped_cache_control=N` in `X-Codvian-Relay` + server log); `thinking` blocks are rejected with 400 (`UnrepresentableError`), never silently dropped; translate-path streaming `message_start` carries no usage block (a 0 would be invented — true usage arrives in `message_delta`); tool calls are emitted whole at stream end. Deprecated models: `internal/catalog/deprecated.go` seeds 16 exact IDs verified against OpenAI's deprecations page (e.g. `gpt-4-0314`, `gpt-3.5-turbo-0301`, the 2026-10-23 batch) — exact-ID match only, no prefix guessing; `/v1/models` and `/dashboard/models` hide them by default (`deprecated_hidden` count in the feed), `?include=deprecated` re-lists them with `deprecated:true`; a request naming a deprecated model is never blocked and still forwards (operator `Model.Deprecated` flag also honoured). | 159 | Context-window pre-call fit check + dedicated fallback chain (backend, routing-docs gap): `internal/router/contextfit.go` — before dialling, the prompt is counted locally (tiktoken BPE, offline — `internal/tokencount`, same estimate as item 145) and compared against each candidate model's context window minus a response reserve; oversize prompts NEVER dial the too-small model — too-small attempts are removed from the chain before the first call (route-trace outcome `skipped_context_fit`, never retried) and the request jumps straight to the dedicated context-window fallback chain (`Config.ContextFallbackChain`, operator-configured long-context models). The reserve is documented in code + `docs/context-fit.md`: the request's own `max_tokens` when set, else `routing.context_reserve_tokens` when configured, else the default 4096 (`DefaultContextReserveTokens`); required = prompt + reserve, `required <= window` fits, `required == window + 1` does not. A model whose window is unknown (registry reports 0) is treated as fitting — unknown is not proof the model is too small — and the decision records it as `window_known: false`. If NO configured model fits anywhere, the request fails honestly pre-call with `ErrContextTooLarge` naming the required tokens vs the largest available window, zero provider calls, no ledger row. A counting failure never fails the request: the check is skipped, the full chain is tried, and the decision records `checked: false` (same rule as item 145). The gate runs in `ChatWithChainStrategy` and `runStream` (after ordering, before the first dial), so hedging/429-retry/failover can only touch fitting models; the decision is kept as `Engine.LastContextFit` and forwarded through `degrade.StaleEngine`. Config: `routing.context_fallback_models` (model IDs in preference order; an entry no provider offers contributes nothing, not an error) + `routing.context_reserve_tokens` (≥ 0, 0 = default 4096), validated + deep-copied in `Clone()` + documented in `config/SCHEMA.md`. Server maps `ErrContextTooLarge` to HTTP 400 in `handleEngineError` (a property of the request + the operator's configuration, not an upstream failure — no upstream-failure counter). `GET /dashboard/context-fit` (Tier 3 MANAGEMENT, auth-exempt, guard table + OpenAPI) serves the last decision (`has_decision: false` before any request reached the gate). Main wires `ContextWindow` from the registry's model metadata and the fallback chain from config. | 157 | Per-request routing contract (backend, capability gap closed; OpenRouter provider object + per-request config header): the `routing` request-body field on POST /v1/chat/completions is the ONE canonical spelling — `order` / `only` / `exclude` / `sort` (price/latency/reliability) / `max_price` (input/output USD per 1M tokens) / `data_collection` (deny/allow) — applied for that request only (`internal/provider.RequestRouting`, strict decode: unknown keys inside `routing` are a 400 naming the key; `internal/router/contract.go` `applyContract` filters before any scoring). An `X-Codvian-Routing` header is rejected with a 400 pointing at the body field, never silently ignored. Validation is fail-closed: unknown provider → 400 naming it; only∩exclude, order+sort, bad sort/data_collection, negative or empty max_price, order naming a provider that does not serve the model → 400 naming the control; empty result set → 400, except the price ceiling emptying it → 402 naming the ceiling and the over/unknown counts. Filters run only → exclude → data_collection → max_price → order; a half-known price (a ceiling leg set but that leg unpriced) is excluded as price_unknown, never assumed cheap. `sort` maps to a strategy (price→cheapest, latency→least-latency, reliability→most-reliable) and wins over the auto:<axis> alias, the chain override and the global strategy for that request only. `data_collection: deny` trusts only what the router can verify: operator-marked `no_training_data` on the provider config, or a local provider by construction (catalog category local, or a loopback base URL) — no built-in vendor-policy database, nothing guessed safe from a name; with no verifier it fails closed. Engine config gains `ContractPriceFunc` (operator price book, pair then model-level) and `TrainingSafe`, wired in main.go, plus `ResolveProvider` (main.go, from the registry: exact ID, then exact display name, then case-insensitive display name; ambiguous name → 400; only enabled providers with a live client resolve — the contract's `order`/`only`/`exclude` names are resolved to registry IDs before validation, and the trace records the resolved IDs; found live: clients address providers by the display names they see, and the engine's chains are keyed by `prov_<hash>` IDs, so without this every name-based contract 400'd). The contract is honoured on both paths (non-streaming `ChatWithChainStrategy` and streaming `ChatStreamWithChainStrategy`/`runStream`); a rejected contract publishes its trace (contract + Error, no attempts) so the dashboard shows the refusal. No silent fallback: only=[cheap] with cheap 429ing fails instead of trying mid/pricey. Contracted requests never share a single-flight (`coalescing.RequestKey` carries the contract) and never touch the response cache (`degrade` lookup/store/staleFor all skip a non-empty contract — a cached entry holds content only, with no record of which provider produced it). The contract never reaches a provider (wire clients build their own payload; `TestRequestRoutingNeverReachesUpstream` pins it). No new routes: the contract rides the existing trace feed. Honest limits: the verbatim relay (`POST /v1/messages`) takes no contract — it forwards the Anthropic dialect untouched; `max_price` consults the operator price book only (provider-reported model prices are not consulted); a `routing: {}` empty object is the same as no contract. COORDINATOR GATE (2026-11-18): live smoke first exposed a REAL product bug — the balanced strategy hook re-ranked the chain by probe latency AFTER applyContract, so order [pricey, cheap] was served by cheap (also flagged by the 153-154 slice). Fixed in the coordinator pass: orderAttempts gains preserveOrder (contract.Order non-empty skips the quota-hint sort + StrategyHook/OrderHook; zero-quota filter, adaptive-concurrency partition and soft-demotion still apply as safety). Two regression tests TestContractOrderSurvivesStrategyHook (+Streaming) prove it — they FAIL with the flag forced off and PASS with it on. Smoke script bug also fixed (ID extraction quoting: the "-quoted python -c lost its inner quotes, IDs were empty). Re-run smoke 47/47 green (real binary + 3 Python mocks). Final coordinator gate: go build/vet/fmt clean, go test -count=1 -v ./... EXIT 0, 1,133 top-level PASS + subtests, 0 fail, 31 packages (log $HOME/.tmp/full-v-sprint8-final.txt). Known flake re-confirmed: TestEngineSlowProviderCapShrinksAndSheds failed the first full run, passed the verbose re-run and 1/3 standalone runs — timing-sensitive under VM load, not a regression (pre-dates sprint 8). | ✅ | ✅ |

| 167 | Retry-After abandon rule (backend, capability gap closed; Retry-After ≥60s ends retries for that target; research-log entry 146): a present Retry-After ≥60s abandons THAT target for this request — the engine moves to the next target immediately, or fails honestly if none remain; it never sleeps out the wait and retries the same target. Per-target, never per-request: healthy targets are still tried. Applies to the non-streaming chain (no-further-target branch breaks instead of sleeping) and to streaming (the same-provider retry is gated on `retryWait > 0`, and the abandon yields 0, so it never fires). The cross-request cooldown still applies (floored/capped wait, at most `RetryAfterCap`), so the next request does not hammer the abandoned target. To tell a present hint from an absent one, `parseRetryAfter` (provider + wire) now returns `(duration, present)` and `RateLimitError` (+ the four wire error structs) carry `RetryAfterPresent` — an explicit `Retry-After: 0` is a present hint, an absent/unparsable header means exponential backoff. `internal/router/retryafter.go` holds the single decision point (`classifyRetryAfter`: abandoned / floored / capped / honoured; `RetryAfterAbandonAt` 60s, `RetryAfterFloor` 10s); the trace records the raw seconds + decision + wait used per rate-limited attempt (`routetrace.RetryAfterInfo`, `GET /dashboard/trace`). Docs: `docs/retry-after-abandon-floor.md`. Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): full `go test -count=1 ./...` — all 31 packages ok (+ web, no tests), 1,118 test events pass, 0 fail (verbose recount, log `$HOME/.tmp/fulltest-167-168.log`); `go test -race` clean on router/server/provider/routetrace/wire; `go build ./...` clean; `go vet` clean on this slice's packages (the tree-wide vet run still flags other slices' in-flight files — provider `routing_test.go` and `cancel_test.go` lost-cancel warnings — not this slice); `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files (3 other-slice server files still unformatted, left to their owners); live smoke 12/12 (real binary + Python mocks, `$HOME/.tmp/smoke-167-168.sh` — scratch, not committed): lone 120s target → 502 in 184 ms, 1 hit, trace abandoned/120; 120s target abandoned for the healthy target (200 in 1,599 ms, 1 hit each, `wait_seconds` 0); 2s hint floored (200 in 11,347 ms, 2 hits, trace floored/2); dashboard rule line present, no Playground. | ✅ | ✅ |
| 168 | Retry-After floor (backend, 10s floor, research-log entry 146): a present Retry-After below 10s is treated as 10s in the retry path — the same-target retry wait (`rateLimitWait`), the hard cooldown and the soft-demotion cooldown (`softCooldownDuration` base, floored; an absent hint keeps the 5s base). 0s/1s/5s/9.999s all wait 10s; 10s is honoured as-is; 45s/59s are capped at `RetryAfterCap` (30s default); ≥60s abandons (item 167). An absent hint is NOT floored — it keeps exponential backoff, so a header-less 429 still fails over immediately. The health checker's `QuotaResetsAt` still records the raw Retry-After (probe bookkeeping, not a retry wait — out of scope, documented). Dashboard: Live Routing trace rows render the hint + decision per attempt (`Retry-After 120s — target abandoned (≥60s), moved on` / `Retry-After 2s — floored to 10s` / capped / honoured), and the Live Routing card states both rules in one line; `node --check` clean, "Playground" absent.  Gate 2026-11-18: same gate as item 167 — full suite 1,118 pass / 0 fail, race clean on touched packages, live smoke 12/12 incl. the floored retry (2s hint → 11,347 ms elapsed, trace `floored`/2s) and the dashboard rule line. | ✅ | ✅ |
| 169 | POST /v1/responses (backend, capability gap closed; the OpenAI Responses API is now a first-class surface in comparable LLM gateways): a real `POST /v1/responses` endpoint (create + `GET /v1/responses/{id}` retrieve + streaming), OpenAI-compatible request/response shapes, implemented as a **translation over the canonical chat engine** — never an upstream Responses passthrough, so Responses conversations work on ANY provider (native-wire Anthropic/Gemini/Cohere/Bedrock included). Decode (`decodeResponsesRequest`): model (required), input (required: string, or array of message / `function_call` / `function_call_output` items; content string or `input_text`/`output_text`/`text` parts), instructions (per-turn system message, NOT stored into the transcript), `previous_response_id`, stream, `max_output_tokens`, temperature, `top_p`, function tools only (other types → 400), `tool_choice` auto/none/required or `{type:function,name}` (unknown name → 400), metadata echoed, `routing` (the same per-request contract as items 157–158, strict decode, unknown keys → 400 naming the key). `reasoning` / `text.format` / `parallel_tool_calls` are NOT honoured — ignored, never faked as applied; no MCP loop for Responses (tool calls return as `function_call` output items). Gateway-minted ids (`resp_` + 24 hex, `msg_…`, `fc_…`). Response object: `id`, `object: "response"`, `created_at`, `status` (completed/incomplete with `incomplete_details` length→`max_output_tokens` / content_filter→`content_filter` / failed / in_progress), `model`, `output` (message with `output_text` parts + `function_call` items per tool call), `usage` (provider-reported only; `cached_tokens`/`reasoning_tokens` only when upstream reported them >0), `previous_response_id` (null for fresh), metadata echoed. Streaming SSE in order: `response.created`, `response.output_item.added`, `response.output_text.delta` / `response.function_call_arguments.delta`, `…done`, `response.output_item.done`, `response.completed`, then `[DONE]`; error before content = plain JSON error, after content = SSE error event + `[DONE]`; after the first chunk the stream never switches provider. Store (`internal/responses`, leaf, stdlib only): in-memory, TTL 2h, cap 200, oldest-first eviction, `Put` replaces in place (stream in_progress placeholder → terminal state); the transcript + served object are stored, the feed strips both. Semantics: **fresh creates fail over like chat** (replay-safe pre-commit); **continuations never replay** — `previous_response_id` resumes the stored transcript on the provider that served the original (`ChatRequest.PinProviderID` + engine `pinChain`, applied in `ChatWithChainStrategy` and `ChatStreamWithChainStrategy` after `applyContract`; if the pinned provider no longer serves the model the normal chain applies, still exactly one attempt) and `ReplayExemption` is split so ONLY continuations are exempt (`ExemptResponses` constant deleted; fresh Responses creates take the normal failover path). **No cache / coalescing / stale for Responses** (`Coalescable` false for `EndpointResponses` or any `PreviousResponseID`; `degrade.cacheable` false the same way, which also excludes the stale fallback — two concurrent identical creates mint two different ids). Budgets, guardrails and ledger apply exactly as on chat completions; the trace records the endpoint as `responses`. Routes (guard table, so OpenAPI picks them up automatically): POST `/v1/responses` + GET `/v1/responses/{id}` Tier 3 MANAGEMENT (gateway-key auth); GET `/dashboard/responses` Tier 3 MANAGEMENT, auth-exempt (ids/model/status/previous id/provider/stream/created_at + created/continuation/stored/evicted counts, never content). Docs: `docs/responses-api.md`. Tests: 8 store unit tests; 9 server tests (decode table 19 subtests, translation, object building, create/retrieve, continuation transcript resume, stream events, full-path failover, continuation-never-replays with 2 subtests, concurrent-creates-never-share); 2 router tests (fresh-create failover incl. streaming + pin wins / pin-missing-falls-back); coalescing + degrade exclusion tests. Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): full `go test -count=1 -v ./...` — 1,699 test events pass (1,357 top-level + 342 subtests), 0 fail in this slice's paths, 35/36 packages ok; the 2 failures are another slice's async-guardrail server tests (`TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit`, `TestAsyncGuardrailAllowPassesThrough`) — both pass 3/3 on standalone re-run, timing-flaky under full-suite load, untouched by this slice; `go build ./...` clean; `go vet ./...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `go test -race` clean on responses/router/provider/coalescing/degrade/routetrace and on this slice's server tests (`go test -race` clean on the full server package on re-run with a 25m timeout — the first full-server `-race` run hit the default 10m timeout, no data race reported, log `$HOME/.tmp/race-server-169-170.log`); live smoke 19/19 (`scripts/smoke-responses.py`, real binary + 2 Python mocks): fresh create fails over on 429 (flaky 1 hit, live 1 hit), create on first provider mints `resp_` id + retrieve works, pinned continuation 429s → honest 502 with exactly one pinned attempt and zero fallback hits, successful continuation resumes with `previous_response_id` echoed, stream event order + retrieve-after-stream completed, feed shows ids + continuation chain + counts (created 4, continuations 1), dashboard Responses blocks present with no Playground, unknown id → 404, no key → 401. Honest limits: the store is in-memory — a restart loses every stored response and a later continuation answers 404 (never a wrong replay); no upstream Responses passthrough (provider-native Responses features such as server-side tools, reasoning effort and structured outputs are not available through this endpoint); `reasoning`/`text.format` are ignored, not applied; a continuation of a failed turn resumes from the transcript without the failed turn; no list/delete endpoints yet, and branching (two continuations of one response) is allowed with no tree. Concurrent-slice build fixes made during this slice (minimal, flagged): `internal/server/compression_test.go` — the ledger adapter assigned ledger fields onto `provider.Usage` (rebuilt as a `ledger.Usage` conversion) and a `hits` field/method name collision (field renamed `hitCount`); `internal/server/learned_test.go` — `t.Context()` → `context.Background()` (module is go 1.22); `internal/degrade/degrade.go` — missing `errors` import; `internal/server/guard_test.go` — added the `GET /dashboard/groups` expected entry for a concurrent slice's route; `internal/routetrace/routetrace.go` — added the `Endpoint` field the embeddings slice needed (later superseded by their own identical field, mine removed). Two other-slice vet errors (`async_guardrail_test.go` `firstChunk`, `zz_debug_test.go` `LogFilter{}`) were fixed by their owners mid-gate; the server-package gate failure in the first full run was their `POST /api/providers/{id}/keys` route landing without its guard-table entry, fixed by its owner before the re-run. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 181 | Learned auto-router (backend, RouteLLM pattern, research-log entry 124; competitor-sweep P1 gap): new leaf package `internal/learned` (stdlib only, deterministic) — one logistic regression per tier predicting P(win | recorded heuristic scores, tier served), trained by batch gradient descent on `feedback.TrainingPairs` (per-tier scores + accept/edit/discard/regenerate outcome only; no prompts/answers/content ever — decisions without scores are excluded, never guessed). Activation gate, measured not believed: 70/30 time split, active only with >=50 labelled outcomes, >=15 evaluable test outcomes, held-out ECE <= 0.15 and log-loss beating the base rate; otherwise status off/learning and routing is exactly unchanged (item-115 no-evidence rule). Composition is additive and bounded: (p-0.5)*2*0.05, at most +/-0.05 per tier, on top of the heuristic score and the feedback nudge; a tier with <10 training outcomes contributes 0. Config flag `auto_router.learned_router` (default OFF) is a second independent gate — while off the model trains and reports but never applies. Server wires it via `learned.Refresher` (30 s cache, retrains after a new signal, keeps the previous model on load error) + `GET /dashboard/learned` (Tier 3 MANAGEMENT, auth-exempt, counts only). | 165 | Panic isolation on the request/stream path (backend, capability gap closed; a panic in a stream/chunk converter/hook must never kill the gateway): `recover()` guards at every boundary, innermost first — `callChat` (`provider_chat`) and `bridgeStream` (`provider_stream`, incl. wire chunk converters) → `ChatWithChainStrategy` (`engine_chat`, incl. order/strategy hooks) and `runStream` (`engine_stream`, incl. preread) → server handlers (`server_chat` / `server_stream` / `server_messages` / `server_messages_stream`, incl. the MCP loop and response marshalling). A recovered panic is a typed `*router.PanicError` (`internal/router/panic.go`): `Error()` returns only a fixed location string, never the recovered value, so traces/dashboard/client errors stay clean; the value is available via `Value()` for the one redacted log line (`guardrails.Redact`, never request bodies/keys). Classification falls through to `DecisionFallback` / `ReasonTransportError`, and the trace reason is overridden to `ReasonPanicRecovered` (`"panic_recovered"`) so the dashboard does not lie. Each panic is counted exactly once, at the boundary that catches it, in the real counter `codvian_router_panics_total{location}` (`internal/metrics`, `IncPanic` + `Snapshot.Panics` + `panics_since_start` in `GET /dashboard/overview`; honest zero = no series), wired in main.go via `engine.SetPanicMetrics(recorder)`. Billing still records: engine guards take the normal failure path (ledger row, zero tokens/cost), `runStream` records a row + trace entry only when no trace entry was added this iteration, and the server recover records a backstop row (provider `"unknown"`, failed, fresh background ctx) iff the engine was never called or the trace is empty — no double rows. The affected request gets a terminal SSE error frame + `[DONE]` (stream; no failover after commit) or an honest 502 (non-stream): engine-returned panics surface as `"upstream provider error"` via `handleEngineError` (the provider client faulted, and it counts as an upstream failure); the server's own recover writes `"gateway internal error"` via `writePanicFailure` (the gateway faulted, deliberately NOT an upstream failure). No new routes, so no guard/OpenAPI changes. Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): `go build ./...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; full `go test -count=1 -v ./...` — 1,424 test events pass (1,131 top-level + 293 subtests), 0 fail, 31 packages; `go test -race` on this slice's own tests clean; live smoke 12/12 (`scripts/smoke-panic-isolation.py` — real binary, isolated HOME, two mocks: failing-first request fails over to live 200, second request 200, stream 200 + [DONE], no panics series in /metrics, `panics_since_start == 0`, dashboard serves the panic card with its honest-zero text). Caveats (other slices, recorded in `docs/slice-165-166-panic-isolation-report.md`): `go vet ./...` fails on 4 pre-existing lostcancel warnings in `internal/router/cancel_test.go` (CHECKLIST 153, before this slice); the full `-race` run surfaces a deterministic data race in `internal/server/tunnel.go` (`TestTunnelGuardAndValidation`) plus two timing-flaky tests (`TestEngineSlowProviderCapShrinksAndSheds`, `TestOpenAIStreamCancelStopsUpstreamReads`) — none touched by this slice. This slice also restored the missing `Engine.reasoning` field (lost by a concurrent edit; the 163–164 slice owner should confirm). Honest limits: no HTTP-reachable input was found that panics the production converter (it is defensive — `wire.TranslateStream` returns errors, it does not panic), so panic behaviour is proven in-process by unit tests with deliberately panicking clients/hooks/engines; the live smoke covers the healthy path + the zero-count feed. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 161 | Content-policy refusal chain (backend, capability gap closed; guardrail denials leak into fallback chains and burn retries): a SEPARATE chain for upstream content refusals — when a target refuses on content policy/safety, the engine does NOT retry the same target and does NOT count it as a provider-health signal (no attempt stats, no breaker trip, no cooldown); it routes to the next target in the refusal chain, and when the chain is exhausted the client gets an honest HTTP 403 with the distinct code -32001 (`error.type: "policy_refusal"`, `error.reason: "upstream_content_refusal"`), never a fake success. Detection: `internal/wire.IsContentRefusalSignal` (canonical phrase list; bare "safety" alone does not match) + `internal/provider` taxonomy `ReasonContentRefused` / `ContentRefusalError` (400/403/422 bodies, typed errors, mid-stream wire errors, `content_filter` finish reason — incl. with partial content, which is converted to a refusal before success handling so no filtered fragment is delivered as a partial success). Config: `routing.refusal_chains` (model → provider IDs in order; default = remaining failure-chain targets once each; explicitly empty = no refusal routing for that model; unknown provider IDs skipped at request time, never dialled; validated: non-empty model/provider, no duplicates; documented in `internal/config/SCHEMA.md`). Separate counters on /metrics: `codvian_router_content_refusals_total`, `codvian_router_refusal_chain_served_total`, `codvian_router_refusal_chain_exhausted_total` — refusals never touch `upstream_failures_total`. Honest limits: a breaker/cooldown-skipped refusal-chain target counts the chain as exhausted (the trace shows the skip); replay-exempt requests still get exactly one attempt; a post-commit (partial content already sent) refusal ends the stream with a refusal error, no switch after commit. Gate 2026-11-18: full suite 1,424 PASS events (1,131 top-level + 293 subtests), 0 fail, across 31 packages; race clean on touched packages (router, provider, server, metrics, config, wire, degrade); live smoke 16/16 green (real binary + 3 Python mocks, script `$HOME/.tmp/refusal-smoke/refusal-smoke.py` — scratch, not in the repo). | ✅ | ✅ |
