Wire: Anthropic replays thinking/redacted verbatim and captures them from responses + `thinking_delta`/`signature_delta` streams; Gemini emits thought parts + attaches Gemini-origin signatures to matching functionCall parts (a foreign Anthropic signature is never attached — Gemini would reject it) and captures thought text + signatures from responses/streams; Bedrock replays `reasoningContent` verbatim (text + signature + redacted) and captures it from responses + ConverseStream deltas — this FIXES the item-52 "reasoningContent dropped by design" gap, since Converse supports it in requests; OpenAI sends `reasoning_content` only for replay models and captures it from responses + streaming deltas; Cohere has no reasoning replay field and errors on any state. Router: capture after each response (transcript + response turn key), inject per assistant message before dialling (never as fresh user content), `reasoning.UnrepresentableError` is a hop failure not a request failure — breaker-neutral, chain continues, all-unrepresentable → honest HTTP 400 naming the wires (`reasoning_unrepresentable`, never a 502, never an upstream-failure count); stream capture only on the server-facing drain path (only a stream that reached Done captures; `ChatStreamWithChain` direct callers get none); replay-exempt requests untouched. Trace: `routetrace.AttemptTrace.Reasoning` + `AddWithReasoning` funnel; a client that does not expose its wire format gets no label (guessing "openai" would mislabel a native-wire hop). Server: `GET /dashboard/reasoning` (Tier 3 MANAGEMENT, auth-exempt, guard table) serves counts only. Docs: `docs/reasoning-replay.md` (matrix, Bedrock fix, failure semantics, limits). The gate test `TestReasoningSurvivesProviderSwitch` proves Anthropic thinking → 429 → Gemini thought part in-process, and the smoke proved it live. The smoke also surfaced one real behaviour outside this slice, reported not fixed: the semantic cache is text-only (the model is not part of the semantic vector), so an identical-text request naming another model can be served another model's cached response — S2's first attempt was answered with the cached claude-sonnet-4-5 response for a gpt-4o request in 0ms.. Honest limits: the store is in-memory — a restart loses all captured state (next turn misses, never wrongly replays); stream capture only on the drain path; Gemini signature attach is best-effort by name (named call, else first part, else in order); a client must never echo `reasoning_content` back — the router re-attaches it from the store. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 173 | User-named routing groups (backend, capability gap closed; OpenRouter and comparable gateways let operators name a pool of providers and address it as one target): config `routing.groups` maps a name to an ordered list of provider IDs (`RoutingConfig.Groups`, deep-copied by `Clone()`); validated at load — empty group, empty/duplicate member, unknown member, or a name containing `:` or `/` is an honest startup error naming it; `refusal_chains` entries may name `group:` refs and are validated the same way. A group is addressable two ways in requests: `routing.group: "fast"` with a normal `model`, and the model alias `group:<name>/<model>` (only the first `/` splits, so model IDs containing `/` survive, e.g. `group:fast/meta-llama/llama-3`); alias with no model, empty group/model parts, or `routing.group` + alias naming different groups are 400s naming the offender. An unknown group fails honestly with 400 naming it, before any provider is dialled (never a silent fallback to everything); a group that expands to zero attempts for the model is a 400 naming group + model. Engine (`internal/router/groups.go`, `Config.GroupChain` hook, `groupChainFor` in `cmd/codvian-router/main.go`): the group resolves to its members in order and then flows through contract filters, context-fit, budgets, ledger and breakers exactly like any chain — failover inside the group, streaming failover, and `refusal_chains` entries starting with `group:` expanding to the group's members in order. Group order is explicit operator order: the strategy hook, strategy tree and quota-hint sort never re-rank a group-resolved chain (pinned by `TestGroupOrderSurvivesStrategyHook`, a reversing hook that must not move a group chain — found live when the balanced hook demoted a member with one ledger failure below the next member); the safety partitions still apply. The trace contract carries `Group` (`RoutingContract.Group`, echoed by `applyContract`, created even for alias-only requests) and a rejected group publishes a trace with the contract + error and no attempts. Docs: `docs/routing-groups.md` + `internal/config/SCHEMA.md` Routing row. | 175 | Multi-key pooling + preferred/reserve accounts (backend, capability gap closed; multi-key pooling: several keys per provider, preferred account first, reserve accounts hd account first, reserve accounts held back): a provider may hold several API keys (a pool, `Provider.Keys` on `provider.Provider`/`ProviderConfig`, roles `preferred`/`reserve`, `EffectiveKeys()` synthesizes one preferred key from the legacy `APIKeyEncrypted` with stable ID `key_` + first 12 hex of SHA-256(ciphertext) for providers stored before pools; `AddKey` live-tests the key and refuses 401 keys and duplicate credentials by fingerprint — including the legacy key, whose stored fingerprint is empty, via decrypt-and-compare — `RemoveKey` refuses the last key, `SetKeyRole` flips preferred/reserve; `Clone()` deep-copies `Keys`). The engine (`internal/router/router.go`, `Attempt.KeyID`/`Reserve`, `ClientKey`/`SplitClientKey` composite identity) tries the preferred key first and fails over to the next key in the pool on 401/429/quota (402): a 401 is terminal ONLY while no sibling key of the same provider remains untried (`isTerminalErrFor`/`hasSiblingKeyAttempt` at all four loop sites — hedge-not-launched, hedge-launched, sequential, streaming pre-commit); a dead key is cooled down per-key — hard mode 5 min (`deadKeyCooldown`), soft mode the doubling ladder (`coolDeadKey`, `KeyID != ''` only, so a legacy single-key 401 keeps today's behaviour: chain stops, nothing cools) — never the whole provider (breaker stays per-provider and closed on 401, item 151's breaker-neutral 4xx rule intact). Reserve attempts sort last via a stable partition in `applyTailPartitions` (the final ordering step, after scoring/adaptive/soft-demotion), are never hedge targets, and are tried only after ALL preferred attempts fail; a pool of only reserve keys still serves. Per-key spend is tracked honestly in the ledger (`Usage.ProviderKeyID`, `provider_key_id` column on `usage_events` + `ensureUsageColumns` migration defaulting `''` for pre-pooling rows — never retroactively assigned — `ProviderKeySpendTotals` groups by (provider, key), the same spend definition as `SpendFor`; the engine prefers `ProviderKeyLedger.RecordAttemptWithProviderKey`, wired in `cmd/codvian-router/main.go` `ledgerAdapter`). The trace names the key per attempt (`routetrace.AttemptTrace.KeyID`, stamped in both trace funnels) and `CooldownTargets()` splits composite keys into provider + key (`CoolingTarget.KeyID`). Wiring (`cmd/codvian-router/main.go`): one engine client per (provider, key) (`ClientForKey`, no-auth providers keep one legacy client under the bare provider ID); `chainFor`/`contextFallbackChain`/`RefusalChainFor`/group chains/`rankModels`/`ResolveProvider` expand each provider into per-key attempts (preferred in pool order, reserve marked); provider-added hook registers all keys' clients, provider-removed drops all, new `WithProviderKeyChangedHook` re-syncs one provider's clients from its current pool. Server: `POST /api/providers/{id}/keys` (201, live key test, duplicate 409, bad key 401), `DELETE /api/providers/{id}/keys/{keyId}` (200, last-key 400), `POST /api/providers/{id}/keys/{keyId}/role` (200) — all Tier 2 ALWAYS_PROTECTED (guard table + `guard_test.go` counts now 9/12/48), responses carry id/role/fingerprint only, never key material; hook failure never fails the request (`routing_live=false` + honest note). `GET /api/providers` enriches each key with live status (`active`/`cooling`/`reserve`/`cooling-reserve`, cooling-until + mode from engine `CooldownTargets()`) and honest per-key spend (`has_spend` false before any row — 'no usage yet', never $0); `''` cooling/spend rows land on the provider's legacy key (the one with no fingerprint) and on no other key; `GET /dashboard/cooldown` targets carry `key_id`. Docs: `docs/slice-175-176-keypool-report.md`. | 187 | Semantic-cache model scoping (backend, real bug found live by the 163-164 smoke): the semantic vector is text-only — the model is not part of it — so a cached claude-sonnet-4-5 response was served for a gpt-4o request in 0ms. Every cache layer is now scoped by the model the request named: `LocalSemanticCache` records the requesting model on each entry (`localSemanticEntry.model`; dedup key is now (model, vector-hash), so the same text stored under two models coexists as two entries) and lookups filter by it in both the inverted-index path and the legacy wide-vector scan — new `GetForModel`/`GetTextForModel`/`SetForModel`/`SetTextForModel`, used by `degrade.lookup`/`store` (ServeCache proactive serving in Chat/ChatStream/ChatStreamChunks) and `server.lookupMessagesCache`/`storeMessagesCache` (verbatim-relay cache, items 143-144); the exact-match key already included the model (`ExactKey`) and the stale fallback uses it, both pinned by tests. Scope is the model the REQUEST named, not the model id the response carries (an upstream alias never scopes an entry — pinned by `TestSemanticCacheScopeIsRequestedModelNotUpstreamAlias`). The provider is deliberately NOT part of the scope: the same model id served by two providers shares entries, documented in the code and pinned by `TestSemanticCacheSameModelAcrossProvidersSharesEntry`. Entries stored without a recorded model are unattributed and are never served to a request naming a model (the raw `Get`/`GetText` serve only those — fail-closed, so no caller can reintroduce a cross-model hit through the raw API). Nothing is evicted or migrated: entries are in-memory and carry their scope from the moment they are stored. Tests: `TestSemanticCacheCrossModelNeverHits`, `TestSemanticCacheCrossModelParaphraseNeverHits`, `TestSemanticCacheSameTextDifferentModelsCoexist`, `TestSemanticCacheRawGetNeverServesModelScopedEntry`, `TestSemanticCacheModelMatchIsExact`, `TestSemanticCacheWideVectorCrossModelMiss` (cache); `TestSemanticCacheNeverServesCrossModel` (exact 163-164 repro through the engine), `TestSemanticCacheSameModelStillHits`, `TestStaleServeNeverServesCrossModel`, `TestSemanticCacheStreamNeverServesCrossModel`, `TestSemanticCacheChunkStreamNeverServesCrossModel`, `TestSemanticCacheScopeIsRequestedModelNotUpstreamAlias` (degrade); `TestMessagesSemanticCacheNeverServesCrossModel`, `TestMessagesSemanticCacheSameModelStillHits` (relay). | 177 | Nestable strategy-tree config (backend, capability gap closed; nestable strategy trees): config may express a strategy as a TREE — new leaf package `internal/strategytree` (`Node{strategy, provider, model, children}`, `MaxDepth` 8, `Validate`/`Clone`/`LeafPath`/`LeafPathWithRoot`/`CanonicalStrategy`). A node is a strategy plus children (it picks among its children by its strategy) or a leaf naming a provider (optionally pinned to one model), e.g. cheapest-of[fastest-of[A, B], C]. `routing.strategy_tree` (every chain) + `routing.strategy_trees[model]` (per-chain override) in `RoutingConfig`, validated at load (`config.Validate`) and deep-copied by `Clone()`; a cycle/shared node, a depth overrun, an unknown strategy, a leaf without a provider, or a node that is both picker and leaf fails at load naming the node path (`root.children[0]...`), never at request time. The engine (`internal/router/tree.go`, `orderAttemptsByTree`/`evalTree`) evaluates recursively: each node ranks its children's winners by its own strategy hook (the same hook the flat path uses, so a node strategy means the same thing in a tree as on a flat chain) and the full ordered list of each child follows its winner; attempts no leaf names are appended after the tree-ordered ones, never dropped; an attempt named by two leaves is tried once. Evaluation guards depth and cycles defensively too, returning `ErrInvalidStrategyTree` (never a stack overflow, never a silent flatten). Composition: a per-request contract `order` skips the tree entirely (the client's try order is final); a contract `sort` or an `auto:<axis>` alias replaces only the tree root's strategy; a contract that only filters (only/exclude/max_price/data_collection) still lets the tree order the filtered chain. The strategy the trace and last-routing record name is the tree root's (or the override that replaced it), and `RouteTrace.StrategyTreePath` names the path to the leaf that served the request (root-override aware, canonicalised). Zero-quota filtering and the tail partitions (adaptive saturation, soft-demotion) apply exactly as on the flat path, shared via `filterZeroQuota`/`applyTailPartitions`. `X-Codvian-Strategy` carries the tree root's strategy. Config schema documented in `internal/config/SCHEMA.md`. Gate 2026-11-18: 20 new test funcs (7 strategytree + 11 router tree + 1 config + 1 server), all pass; `go test -race` clean on touched packages (strategytree, config, routetrace, router, server tree test); `go build` + `go vet` clean; live smoke 16/16 green (real binary + 3 Python mocks, free ports, unique prompts to defeat the prompt cache). Known interaction (not a tree bug): the engine's default 150 ms hedge races the 2nd attempt, so a cheapest-first pick slower than 150 ms loses to the hedge — the smoke keeps mock delays under the hedge threshold; see the slice report. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 179 | Opt-in live price/context sync from models.dev (backend, capability gap closed; model prices + context windows are synced from models.dev; never invent prices): new leaf package `internal/modelsync` — the sync is OFF by default (no network call at all until the operator sets `models_dev.enabled: true` in config; URL/interval/timeout validated on load whether or not it is enabled). When on, the router fetches the public models.dev catalog (default `https://models.dev/api.json`, base tier only — tiered `context_over_200k`/per-tier prices ignored, deprecated models skipped, invalid cost legs dropped with the window kept) in the background after serve starts (startup never waits on it) and every `interval_minutes` thereafter (0 = once). Matching is exact only (local `catalog_id` then registry ID → models.dev provider; exact model ID against the models the router knows the provider offers — never invents a model). Merge precedence (operator always wins): a synced price applies ONLY where the operator priced neither the pair nor the model level, and a synced window only where the registry window is 0 or exactly the value the sync last applied; a pair the operator prices later (or a window the operator changes) drops out of the synced layer on the next sync, and a synced price for a pair the operator priced before a restart is pruned at startup load. Prices live in a separate copy-on-write synced layer on the PriceBook consulted LAST at `Lookup` time (source `"models.dev"` + sync time); windows apply to the registry in memory only (never persisted to the provider store — the state file owns durability). A failed/offline fetch keeps the last good data and records the failure honestly (last error + last success, persisted so the dashboard can say "stale since …" after a restart, never silent). State persists in `models-dev-sync.json` in the data dir (0600, atomic write); a corrupt state file only warns at startup. Synced prices flow into the ledger (`buildLedgerPrices`, incl. a runtime `SetPrices` on the async writer + server so a periodic re-sync changes what is charged), scoring/strategies/auto-router/budget gates (all read the book's `Lookup`), and the context-fit gate (windows from the registry). No network call in tests except against a local httptest stand-in. Full design + limits in `docs/models-dev-sync.md`. | 189 | Tunnel data race fix + shed-path gap (backend, race/flake hardening slice): (a) REAL DATA RACE — `tunnelManager.Start()` wrote `m.ln` while a previously started `acceptLoop()` still read it without the lock (`tunnel.go:191`), deterministic under `-race` (4 warnings per run on the new regression test) and a real bug beyond the detector: a stale loop could Accept on the new tunnel's listener. Fixed at the root: `acceptLoop` now takes the listener as a parameter (`acceptLoop(ln)`), never re-reads `m.ln`; `Start`/`Stop` still touch `m.ln` only under `mu`. New regression test `TestTunnelRapidRestartNoRace` (100 start/stop cycles, dial every 10th) trips the detector 5/5 runs on the old code and passes 10/10 on the fix. (b) REAL SHED-PATH GAP, not timing — `TestEngineSlowProviderCapShrinksAndSheds` failed ~2/3 of standalone runs with a burst request returning "adaptive concurrency limit reached" while the fast provider stood idle: `provider.ClassifyError` classified `adaptive.ErrLimited` as `DecisionStop`, so `isTerminalErr` broke `ChatWithChainStrategy` out of the attempt loop on the first shed. A shed is per-target capacity, not a property of the request, so it now classifies `DecisionFallback` (`ReasonConcurrencyLimited`, status 0, unchanged); when every target sheds the loop still ends with `lastErr == ErrLimited`, so `TestEngineAllProvidersAtCapFailsOpenHonestly` is unchanged (pinned by new taxonomy cases, incl. a wrapped-ErrLimited case). No other `DecisionStop` consumer is affected (`server.go` terminal-4xx branch needs status 400–499; ErrLimited has status 0). Full non-race suite 1,711 test events pass (1,369 top-level + 342 subtests), 34/36 packages ok. | ✅ | ✅ |
| 190 | Timing-flake hardening (same slice): (a) `TestOpenAIStreamCancelStopsUpstreamReads` flaked with "delivered 3 chunks, want 2" — `bufio.Scanner` returns already-buffered SSE lines without touching the network, so a chunk buffered before the abort was delivered after it. Product fix: the OpenAI client's `emit` (and the Anthropic client's `emit`, same shape via `wire.readSSE`) now returns `ctx.Err()` unwrapped before invoking `onChunk` when the caller's ctx is done — no chunk is delivered after a cancel; the error passes through `mapAnthropicError` unwrapped so the router still recognises the cancellation. Test fix: the brittle fixed total bound (`totalWrites <= 6`, which assumed the client kept up with the 30 ms producer) is now time-derived — pre-abort lines bounded by elapsed time / 30 ms tick + 2, at most 3 lines inside the 500 ms grace, zero after it; the exact-2-delivered and zero-writes-after-grace assertions are unchanged. (b) `TestStreamCancelStopsUpstreamReads` (router) flaked on the buffered-channel send race in `bridgeStream`: `send` could still deliver a stale delta after the abort whenever the send case won the select. Product fix: `send` checks `ctx.Err()` before enqueueing. Test fix: the exact-equality `got == "t1 t2 "` becomes a prefix assertion plus a delivery-side no-delta-after-grace check (no delta arrival after `cancelAt` + 150 ms); the upstream-emissions-after-grace, terminal-`context.Canceled`, no-failover and trace-`cancelled` assertions are unchanged. (c) Concurrency burst determinism: the 10 burst goroutines now start behind a barrier (`close(start)`), so the burst is one contention window instead of ten launches smeared across scheduling jitter; all burst assertions unchanged. No dashboard change: no user-visible status changes in this slice (no new feed, no new card). Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): same gate as item 189 — each previously-flaky test 10/10 under `-race` (concurrency, router cancel, OpenAI + Anthropic cancel); tunnel tests 10/10 under `-race`. Full `-race` run: 0 data races, 33/36 packages ok (3 other-slice failures, each green in isolation — see gate section). | ✅ | ✅ |
| 205 | Stream-cancel hardening for Gemini/Cohere/Bedrock (same discipline as 189-190 for OpenAI/Anthropic): each client's `emit` now returns `ctx.Err()` unwrapped before invoking `onChunk` when the caller's ctx is done; the `map*Error` functions pass unknown errors through unwrapped and the wire translators (`TranslateGeminiStream`/`TranslateCohereStream` via `readSSE`, `TranslateBedrockStream` via `readEventStream`) return emit errors unwrapped, so the router still recognises the cancellation via `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)`. Tests: 4 new tests in `internal/provider/cancel_test.go` (`TestGeminiStreamCancelStopsUpstreamReads`, `TestCohereStreamCancelStopsUpstreamReads`, `TestBedrockStreamCancelStopsBufferedFrames`, `TestBedrockStreamCancelAbortsInflightRead`) — burst design (10 SSE lines / 10 event-stream frames in one flush, so already-buffered delivery is exercised; a slow 30 ms tick mock passed even with the fix removed, proven by sensitivity check): each 10/10 under `-race`; sensitivity proven (Gemini + Cohere burst tests fail 10/10, Bedrock buffered test fails, with the fix removed). Live smoke (real binary + mocks, script `~/workspace/.build-tmp/smoke-205-206.py`, 14/14 green): Gemini exactly 2 chunks + zero mock writes after abort+500ms, Cohere same, Bedrock no bytes while the upstream never ends + zero writes after abort+500ms, router healthy after; end-of-run late-write audit (~50 s after the aborts) zero for all three. Honest limitation: `wire.readEventStream` does `io.ReadAll` first — Bedrock delivers NO chunks incrementally until the whole body arrives; the emit check stops delivery of buffered frames after cancel and the in-flight read aborts promptly (unit test), but incremental delivery is a pre-existing design limitation, out of scope for this slice. No dashboard change: no user-visible status changes (transport behaviour only). | 206 | Health history prune slowness (flagged by 189-190): `TestHistoryPrunesOnWrite` (2,980 records) took 128.37 s — one fsync'd whole-file rewrite per record. Fix is append + periodic compaction: each `record()` appends ONE JSON line to `health-history.journal` (~6 µs, `O_APPEND`, 0600); the `health-history.json` snapshot is rewritten only at compaction (every 128 journal lines) and on every prune; the snapshot carries a `journal_seq` watermark and load replays only journal lines with seq above it, so a crash between snapshot write and journal truncate can neither lose nor duplicate a sample; the backup is now a REPLICA of the latest snapshot (not the previous ring — a predecessor backup left a gap of up to one compaction interval on corrupt-primary recovery), written from memory via `writeFileAtomicSync` (temp + fsync + rename, 0600), never by copying the primary, so a corrupt primary can never overwrite the backup and a compaction after corruption heals it; a torn journal tail is skipped at replay and repaired on load. First attempt (tiered persistence: cheap snapshot per record + fsync checkpoint every 128 persists or 5 s) was measured and REJECTED (67 s, still over the 60 s bound — the 5 s rule misfired under load and 2,980 x 7 ms is a ~21 s floor) and fully replaced. Bug found and fixed during testing: the load early return (both snapshots failed, journal empty) skipped counter initialisation, leaving `nextSeq = 0` so the next appended line had seq 0 and was skipped by watermark replay — counters are now set before any early return. `ClearHistory()` deletes the journal too (DB-wipe route covered via `ClearHistory()`). Prune semantics unchanged and pinned by rewritten tests (merged snapshot+journal helper; 300-record compaction completeness, backup==primary, corrupt-primary restore 260 incl. replica-only 256, corrupt-primary heals on compaction, ClearHistory deletes snapshot+backup+journal). Timing: `TestHistoryPrunesOnWrite` now 2.90 s (128.37 s -> ~2.9 s, ~44x), with a timing pin failing if > 60 s; full `internal/health` package passes. Live smoke: after the 60 s watcher tick, `journal_lines=3` (one per provider probe), snapshot 0; dashboard overview 200. No dashboard change: no user-visible behaviour change — the history ring is identical, only persisted differently. Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): full suite 38/38 packages ok, 0 fail, EXIT 0; `go vet` clean and `gofmt` clean on the touched packages. | ✅ | ✅ |
| 191 | Hedge vs explicit chain order (backend): the default 150 ms hedge races the second attempt, so a tree-ordered cheapest-first pick slower than 150 ms lost to the hedge — the 177–178 slice's live trace showed `hedged_cancelled` for the tree's first pick. Decision (documented in code + `docs/slice-191-192-hedge-vs-tree-report.md`): explicit-order chains get NO hedge — when the chain order is explicit (per-request contract `order`, routing-group member order, or strategy-tree order) the engine launches no hedge on either path (`hedgeOff := explicitOrder || treeOrdered` added to both launch conditions in `ChatWithChainStrategy` / `runStream`); the longer-delay alternative was rejected because any delay is still a race and the first pick's expected latency is only an EWMA estimate — only disabling the hedge guarantees the stated order. The default (engine-computed) chain keeps the 150 ms hedge exactly as before, including flat cheapest chains (pinned by test: engine-computed order still hedges). Cost is explicit: an explicit-order request loses the hedge's latency insurance and waits out a slow first pick; sequential failover on actual failure is unchanged. The trace records the decision (`routetrace.RouteTrace.Hedge`: `armed` / `disabled-explicit-order` / `disabled-exempt`, `""` when no decision was recorded, + `HedgeDelayMs` the real configured delay when armed) stamped by `Engine.stampHedgeMode` before the attempt loop; `GET /dashboard/trace` carries `hedge` + `hedge_delay_ms`. Tests: 7 new router tests (`internal/router/hedge_test.go` — contract/group/tree explicit-order slow first pick serves with fast 0 calls and no `hedged_cancelled`, default chain still hedges, flat cheapest still hedges, both streaming analogues) + 1 server feed test — all pass, and all four explicit-order tests FAIL with the rule removed (hedge wins, "fast" serves) and pass with it restored. Docs: `docs/slice-191-192-hedge-vs-tree-report.md`; smoke script `scripts/smoke-hedge-explicit-order.py`. Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): full verbose suite 1,869 PASS events (1,483 top-level + 386 subtests), 0 fail, 38/38 packages (log `$HOME/.tmp/fulltest-191-192-verbose.log`); `go test -race` clean on router + routetrace; live smoke 21/21 green (real binary + 2 Python mocks). Pre-existing: `go vet ./internal/server/` reports a test-only import cycle through another slice's untracked `compression_cache_test.go`; `go test ./internal/server/` builds and passes. | ✅ | ✅ |

(real delay from the feed, never hard-coded), "Hedging off — explicit chain order (contract, group, or strategy tree): the first pick is never raced by a hedge.", or "Hedging off — replay-exempt request, exactly one attempt."; no line when no hedge decision was recorded. `node --check` on the extracted inline script clean; "Playground" absent. | 183 | Lossless-first prompt/context compression (backend, competitor-sweep P1 gap; compressing prompts before the provider call saves input tokens, but a lossy summary must never silently change meaning): new leaf package `internal/compress` (stdlib only) — lossless steps first: (1) dedupe exact-duplicate system/developer messages (same role+content, keep first; user/assistant/tool never deduped), (2) strip redundant whitespace outside fenced code blocks (trailing spaces/tabs, blank-line runs to at most 1, trim leading/trailing blank lines; inside fences byte-identical), (3) canonicalise repeated tool results (keep LAST per `tool_call_id`). `VerifyLossless` proves the compressed form is exactly the canonical normal form (`Normalise(original) == compressed` and `Normalise(compressed) == compressed`, idempotent) — a structural guarantee, honestly documented as NOT a semantic proof; on verification failure the original messages are sent and `Applied=false`. Lossy is a SEPARATE explicit per-call opt-in (`"compression":{"lossy_max_prompt_tokens": N}`) and is extractive trimming, NOT abstractive summarisation (no summariser model exists): drop whole middle turns oldest-first (pinned: all system/developer, last user, last message of any role), then truncate longest plain-text messages head+tail (60/40) with an inline `"[…truncated by lossy compression…]"` marker; part-carrying messages dropped whole or kept, never partially rewritten; lossy never empties a message; `after_tokens` may still exceed the budget when pinned messages alone do not fit. Opt-in: per-request `"compression":{"lossless": bool, "lossy_max_prompt_tokens": int}` (strict decode — unknown keys → 400 naming the field, negative budget → 400; explicit `false` opts out even for a listed model) or per-model config `compression.lossless_models` (validated: non-empty, no duplicates; lossy is NEVER configured). Compression points: server handler `applyCompression` after guardrails, before budget gates; engine `maybeCompress` at every entry point (`Chat`, `ChatStream*`, `ChatWithChainStrategy`, `ChatStreamWithChainStrategy`, `runStream`) before the coalescing key, so the key is on the compressed prompt; a server stamp for the same model is kept as-is, a re-decision after an `auto/*` resolve keeps the original before-count. Disclosure, never silent: response headers `X-Codvian-Compression` (`lossless` / `lossy` / `lossless+lossy`), `X-Codvian-Compression-Saved-Tokens`, `X-Codvian-Compression-Prompt-Tokens`, `X-Codvian-Compression-Lossy: true`; route trace `routetrace.CompressionInfo` (`applied`, `lossless`, `lossy_applied`, `steps`, `before_tokens`, `after_tokens`, `saved_tokens`, `lossy_max_tokens`, `source`, `verified`) via `GET /dashboard/trace`; ledger columns `prompt_before_compression_tokens` / `compression_saved_tokens` on `usage_events` (sync `RecordUsage` + async batch writer + import round-trip + validation, `UsageSummary.CompressionRequests` / `CompressionTokensSaved` counting successful attempts only — the Summary SQL filters `ok = 1`; production `ledgerAdapter` in `cmd/codvian-router/main.go` maps both fields). Token savings measured with the local counter (`internal/tokencount`, items 145–146) — local estimates, wrong encoding for non-OpenAI providers, no tool-schema/framing overhead. Docs: `docs/compression.md`; config documented in `internal/config/SCHEMA.md`. Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): full `go test -count=1 -v ./...` — 1,712 PASS events (1,370 top-level + 342 subtests), 0 fail, 36/36 packages (log `$HOME/.tmp/fulltest-183-184-final.log`); `go build ./...` clean; `go vet` clean on touched packages; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files (the `internal/routetrace/routetrace.go` flag is another slice's AttemptTrace alignment, left alone); `go test -race` clean on compress, router, ledger, config, provider, routetrace, cmd and the server compression tests; live smoke 46/46 green (real binary + 1 Python mock, script `$HOME/.tmp/smoke-183-184/smoke-compression-183-184.py` — scratch, not committed; measured: lossless 25→14 prompt tokens, saved 11, verified; lossy 654→69, saved 585, after ≤ 80; Overview total 4 compressed requests, 618 tokens saved; logs agree row-for-row). Flakes / other slices, recorded honestly: (1) the first full run timed out at 10m on `internal/server` with exactly one test running — `TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit` (a concurrent slice's new async-guardrail test, not this slice) — which passes in 0.29s in isolation; the verbose gate skipped it and went 36/36 green. (2) The full server `-race` run fails on `TestAsyncGuardrailCutsStream` (same slice's test, timing-flaky under -race: failed 2/3 runs, passed 1/3; passes without -race in the full gate) — not this slice, not a data race. (3) Concurrent-slice breaks fixed minimally and flagged: `POST /api/providers/{id}/keys` (+2 sibling routes) were registered without guard-table entries, panicking every `New()` — added as TierAlwaysProtected like the sibling provider mutations (owner: confirm the tier); production `ledgerAdapter` never mapped the new compression fields — added to both adapter literals. Real bugs found by this slice's own tests/smoke (fixed, with regression tests): (a) the server test's first ledger assertion failed because the test adapter did not set SplitKnown — the adapter now mirrors production. (b) The live smoke proved streaming/local-estimate ledger rows lost their compression record — `normalizeUsage` stored it only alongside a known split; it is now stored on every row that carries it (shared rows still zeroed), with `TestCompressionStorageLocalEstimate`. (c) The Summary SQL counted failed rows toward the compression totals despite documenting successful attempts only — now filters `ok = 1`. Honest limits: lossy is extractive trimming, not abstractive summarisation; all token counts are local tiktoken estimates; `after_tokens` may exceed the lossy budget when pinned messages alone do not fit; `VerifyLossless` is structural, not a semantic proof; the semantic cache (last-user-message text, model-scoped) can serve a compressed request's response to a later request with the same last user message — harmless for lossless, and a trimmed-context response for lossy (see `docs/compression.md`). | ✅ | ✅ |

| 185 | Async guardrail mode (backend, capability gap closed; guardrails can run async so a slow guardrail never blocks the first token): per-guardrail `mode` (`sync`/`async`) on external guardrails (`guardrails.external` in config: `name`, `kind`, `mode`, `endpoint`, `timeout_ms`; new `internal/guardrails/async.go` — `AsyncChecker` interface, `HTTPClassifier` POSTing `{"text": ...}` and expecting HTTP 200 `{"verdict": "allow"|"block", "reason": ...}`, non-200/malformed/unknown verdict = checker error, no auth header, default 5 s timeout). Only external (slow) guardrails can be async: built-in kinds `pii`/`secret`/`injection` stay synchronous by validation — async + blocking kind is a config error naming the guardrail (at load, at startup via `buildGuardrailStore` in `cmd/codvian-router/main.go`, and on dashboard POST), because a secret must never reach a provider. Async verdicts run concurrently with the provider call; a BLOCK cuts a live stream with the terminal refusal frame (403 / `-32001`, `type: "policy_refusal"`, same as the sync path) + `[DONE]`, or withholds a non-streaming response (403 / `-32001`) before delivery, or the plain 403 JSON when the verdict lands before any byte was committed. Checker errors fail OPEN (request continues, outcome `error`). No failover, no health signal, no upstream-failure count on a cut; trace outcome/reason `async_guardrail_blocked`. Outcomes are timing labels matching what the client experienced: `allowed`, `blocked_after_start`, `blocked_before_delivery`, `blocked_after_delivery`, `error`. Ledger bills only what was served: a mid-stream cut records a failed attempt with the local prompt estimate (`LocalEstimate=true`) and 0 completion; a cut before the engine dialled records zero attempts and zero rows (nothing attempted, nothing billed) — and when the attempt was already in flight the same cut records one failed prompt-estimate row, so the server test asserts the invariant (trace and ledger agree) rather than the timing. A withheld non-streaming response keeps the engine's success row. Real bugs found by this slice's tests: (1) `Store.Config()`/`SetConfig()` shared the `External` slice backing array, so `json.Decode` into a returned config silently rewrote the store — a rejected POST mutated the store; both now deep-copy. (2) `AsyncRun.blockOutcome()` keyed `blocked_after_start` off the request's streaming flag instead of delivery, mislabelling a before-commit 403 as a stream cut; it now keys off `MarkDelivered()`. Docs: `docs/async-guardrails.md`; config documented in `internal/config/SCHEMA.md`. Limits: NOT wired into the `/v1/messages` relay path (sync checks apply there, no async run/cut); a BLOCK landing between the last chunk and `[DONE]` lets the stream complete (`blocked_after_delivery`); no auth on the classifier endpoint. | 171 | Embeddings + rerank passthrough (backend, capability gap closed; comparable gateways route embeddings and rerank through the same gateway): real POST /v1/embeddings and POST /v1/rerank (OpenAI-compatible embeddings shape; Cohere rerank shape — OpenAI has no /v1/rerank), routed through the engine with chain resolution, budgets, guardrails and ledger, but replay-exempt: exactly ONE upstream attempt each, never replayed onto another target (stateful/single-shot semantics — the task's loose 'failover' wording does not apply). A synthetic bookkeeping ChatRequest (input text as user messages, endpoint kind, caller, budget — never sent upstream) drives the shared machinery: reconcileUsage local-estimate fallback when upstream reports no usage (the usual rerank case), the strict per-request-budget gate (typed 402 pre-dial), recordAttempt ledger rows. Request guardrails run on the input text pre-dial (403/`-32001`, zero provider calls); no response guardrail (vectors, and rerank results echo the caller's own documents). Ledger gains an `endpoint` column (`""` = chat, `embeddings`, `rerank`) — schema + `ensureUsageColumns` migration (pre-column rows read as `""`, honest: chat was the only surface then), sync/async INSERTs, `RecentEvents`, import dedup fingerprint + validation (unknown endpoint rejected; a chat row and an embeddings row for the same model are different rows), priced honestly from the price book where known (unknown = unpriced, never `$0`-claimed). `RouteTrace.Endpoint` + `GET /dashboard/trace` passthrough; the called attempt is stamped `preread: "exempt:<endpoint>"`. `StaleEngine` forwards Embed/Rerank with no cache and no stale fallback. Server: two Tier 3 MANAGEMENT routes (guard-table count 46→48), dedicated `recordEmbedMetrics` (never the chat one — a stale LastCacheHit would misattribute the call to "cache"), 405 from the single attempt surfaces as the upstream's own 405 (the taxonomy would fall back, the exemption forbids it), 503 when the engine has no capability. | 209 | Release signing pipeline (backend, supply-chain hardening P2 gap; LiteLLM Oct 2026 compromise, Bifrost CVE-2026-90898 — signed, pinned releases from day one): Sigstore cosign KEYLESS signing in CI on tag (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, runs only on a pushed `v*.*.*` tag — no tag or release is created by this slice; pushing a tag is the release decision, Raunak's call only). GoReleaser `signs` over `checksums.txt` AND each SBOM (Sigstore bundle `<artifact>.sigstore.json`, identity pinned to the release workflow + tag ref, Rekor transparency log; no long-term signing key exists anywhere). One SPDX-JSON SBOM per archive via syft (`sboms: - artifacts: archive`; workflow installs syft first) + a dependency-free CycloneDX generator as the local fallback (`internal/release`, `cmd/release-sbom`, `scripts/release-sbom.sh`). Choice documented in `docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md`: cosign keyless chosen over minisign (no long-term key to steal — the LiteLLM failure mode — Rekor log, pinned workflow identity, native GoReleaser support); GPG rejected for the same key-management reason. | 210 | Release verification path + tests (same slice): `scripts/verify-release.sh` — the exact commands a user runs to verify a release's signature + checksums + SBOM before installing (cosign keyless verify of `checksums.txt` with pinned workflow/tag identity → `sha256sum --check` of the archive → cosign verify of the SBOM bundle + JSON validity → `gh attestation verify` when `gh` is present; `--local DIR` mode for files on disk, `--key PUBKEY` keypair mode for the pipeline's own self-test; exit non-zero naming the failure on any step). `codvian-router verify-release [DIR]` subcommand for the offline checksum check (integrity only — it never claims the manifest came from the project; the signature is cosign's job). `internal/release` (stdlib only): checksum manifest generate/verify (sha256sum-compatible, sorted, rejects malformed/duplicate/traversal entries) + CycloneDX 1.6 SBOM generation from `go list -m -json all` (replace directives honoured, unknown versions never invented). `scripts/install.sh` verifies the cosign signature over `checksums.txt` when cosign is present (refuses on failure, before trusting any checksum) and warns loudly + continues checksum-only without it, unless `CODVIAN_ROUTER_REQUIRE_SIGNATURE=1` refuses. Install docs point at verification FIRST (`docs/INSTALL.md` §§5–6, `docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md`, README install table). | 211 | Routing contract on /v1/messages (backend, closing the 203–204 live-smoke finding — `anthropicToChatRequest` silently dropped `routing`): the canonical `routing` field (order / only / exclude / sort / max_price / data_collection / group — the same shape as chat completions, CHECKLIST 157) is parsed by `relay.ParseAnthropicDetailed` (strict `DisallowUnknownFields`, error string identical to chat's — a test decodes the same bad body both ways and compares) into `ParseResult.Routing` and forwarded by `handleMessages` onto the canonical request, so the engine validates it (unknown provider → 400 naming it, empty result → 400, price ceiling → 402) and records the applied contract on the route trace — the same code path as chat, not a parallel one. A malformed contract fails closed with a 400 naming the field before any provider is dialled, even for a request that would otherwise relay verbatim; the `X-Codvian-Routing` header is rejected with the identical 400 as on chat. A contracted request never consults the gateway cache (a cached entry holds content only, with no record of which provider produced it — the same rule the engine's cache wrapper enforces on chat). New `provider.EndpointMessages = "messages"`, stamped on the route trace by the engine (`traceEndpointFor`, all four chat-path publishes) — messages requests are NOT replay-exempt and fail over like chat. | 221 | Docker image publishing + signing (backend, closing the 209-210 honest limitation — GoReleaser published no image, so the image was unsigned): GoReleaser `dockers_v2` (since v2.12, the current recommended way; the old `dockers` + `docker_manifests` pair is deprecated) publishes one multi-arch manifest (linux/amd64 + linux/arm64) per tag to `ghcr.io/raunakiitp/codvian-router:<tag>` and `:latest`, built from the new `Dockerfile.release` out of the pre-built binaries — the image carries the exact binary that ships in the release archives (context layout `linux/<arch>/codvian-router` via `$TARGETPLATFORM`; the runtime shape matches `Dockerfile` exactly: distroless nonroot, CA certs, healthcheck helper built inline from the same source, ENTRYPOINT/CMD/EXPOSE/HEALTHCHECK/USER identical, verified byte-identical healthcheck source). `docker_signs` signs the image with the SAME cosign keyless identity as the blobs (no `--key` flag — ambient GitHub OIDC, workflow + tag ref; `${artifact}@${digest}` pins each signature to the manifest digest, so a tampered image re-pushed under the same tag does not verify). Workflow (`.github/workflows/release.yml`): GHCR login (`docker/login-action` v3.7.0, SHA-pinned per the 223-224 policy) + `packages: write`; image provenance attestation via `actions/attest-build-provenance` (`subject-name` + manifest digest from `dist/artifacts.json` `extra.Digest`, `push-to-registry: true`); verify job also verifies the published image (`scripts/verify-image.sh` + `docker pull` + `docker run --rm <image> help`, retried). GoReleaser also attaches an SBOM to the image by default (syft + cosign, both installed by the workflow). Real bug found + fixed by this slice's validation: the 209-210 config failed `goreleaser check` — both `signs` entries defaulted to id `default` ("found 2 signs with the ID 'default'") — so the release as configured could never have run; both entries now carry explicit ids (`checksum`, `sbom`), `docker_signs` carries `docker-image`, and the pre-existing deprecated `archives.format` was renamed to `formats` (GoReleaser deprecation since v2.6); `goreleaser check` is now fully green on the real GoReleaser v2.20.2 (the pinned workflow version). `dist/` added to `.gitignore`. No tag or release created. Dashboard: no new UI — releases are not served by the gateway (same as 209-210); the README install-table note + docs are the status note. | 195 | GET /v1/me/status + key expiry/regeneration + per-key traffic limits (backend, capability gaps closed; the operator surfaces comparable gateways expose): `GET /v1/me/status` (Tier 3 MANAGEMENT, auth required) — authenticated gateway-key introspection: key id, fingerprint (first 12 hex of the stored SHA-256 hash), name, scopes, created/expiry (+ seconds until expiry), legacy per-key budget, rolling 5-hour budget position (spent/remaining/at-limit/window/next roll-off/unknown spend from the ledger), and traffic limits + live last-minute usage; never the key value or the full hash (asserted by test). Key expiry: operator-set `expires_at` on a gateway key (config `GatewayKey.ExpiresAt`, validated + round-tripped + deep-copied in `Clone()`); an expired key fails auth at the single verifier check and every auth wrapper answers an honest 401 naming the expiry time, never a generic auth failure. Regeneration: `POST /admin/keys/{id}/rotate` (Tier 2) — the old key is revoked at rotation (dead immediately, verifier reads the live config) and a new key is issued in its place (same name/scopes/budget/limits unless overridden), returned exactly once in a 201 and never logged; persist failure rolls the config back and the old key stays valid; 503 without a persist function rather than minting a key that would vanish on restart. Expiry/limits update: `POST /admin/keys/{id}` (Tier 2) — sets/clears expiry and requests-per-minute + tokens-per-minute, same persist-or-rollback rule. Per-key traffic limits: in-memory sliding 60 s window per key (`internal/server/keytraffic.go`) — RPM counts admitted requests, TPM counts locally estimated prompt tokens at admission (`tokencount.CountPrompt`, the same estimator the budget gates use); enforced pre-call on chat completions, messages (requests only — the relay forwards verbatim, so no prompt estimate exists), embeddings/rerank and Responses, after every budget gate, so a budget-refused request never consumes a slot; over the limit answers an honest 429 with a `Retry-After` header (seconds until the oldest window entry ages out) and error type `key_rate_limit`, distinct from an upstream 429, with zero provider calls. Honest limits: counters live since server start and reset on restart; TPM counts prompt tokens only — completion tokens are unknown before the call and are never counted. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 197 | serve --headless + `run <cli> --model` launcher (backend, capability gap closed; headless mode + `run <cli> --model` onboarding): `serve --headless` starts the gateway as a pure API process — no dashboard server, no browser/tray behaviour: `server.WithHeadless` makes `register()` answer every `/dashboard*` route 404 (headless servers serve `/v1/*`, `/metrics` and `/healthz` only). It refuses to start without a gateway key configured (auth-on-from-first-boot): `requireGatewayKey` in `runServe` checks BEFORE `openStack` builds anything, so a missing config or zero keys exits 1 naming the missing key — a headless unauthenticated gateway can never listen. `run <cli> --model <model>` launches a named local CLI with the router as its base URL (`OPENAI_BASE_URL`/`OPENAI_API_KEY` + `CODVIAN_ROUTER_BASE_URL`/`CODVIAN_ROUTER_API_KEY`/`CODVIAN_ROUTER_MODEL` env, stdio inherited, exit code propagated, extra args after `--` passed verbatim). A missing CLI binary is an honest error naming it, never a silent no-op; a missing `--model` exits 2; zero providers configured refuses with `init --wizard` as the fix. When no gateway is running, `run` issues a fresh ephemeral gateway key (`run-<cli>`, chat+models), starts its own headless gateway, waits up to 15 s for `/healthz`, and revokes the key + stops the gateway when the CLI exits (a failed revocation prints the key ID, never the raw key). When a gateway IS already running, `run` reuses it only with `CODVIAN_ROUTER_API_KEY` — a stored key's raw value cannot be recovered from the config (hash only) and a key issued now would be unknown to the already-running process, so without it `run` refuses honestly instead of launching a CLI that cannot authenticate. Docs: `docs/headless-run-wizard.md`. Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): 12 new tests pass (4 server headless + 8 cmd: headless refusal, launcher env/missing-binary/missing-model, wizard happy/skip/unknown-provider paths); full `go test -count=1 ./...` (315 s) — cmd package ok, server package fails only on 2 other-slice async-guardrail tests (`TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit`, `TestMessagesVerbatimAsyncWithholdsNonStreaming`, item 203 slice's new/edited files) and provider fails only on `TestPluginHeadersAppliedToUpstream` (passes standalone — flake/concurrent-edit artifact); live smoke 21/21 green (real binary + Python OpenAI mock, script `$HOME/.tmp/smoke-197-198.py` — scratch, not committed). | ✅ | ✅ |

| 201 | Plugin system (backend, capability gap closed; comparable gateways' plugin hooks for guardrails, transforms, and provider adapters): new leaf package `internal/plugins` — in-process Go-interface plugins registered at startup from config `plugins.enabled` (names, in order = pipeline order), NO dynamic loading by design (no .so / Wasm / JS / binary path in config — loading arbitrary code from config would be arbitrary code execution; `POST /admin/plugin/load` answers 501 by design, guard-table description updated to say so). Hook points: pre-request (guardrail-style allow/deny/transform, after built-in guardrails, before any provider is dialled — also wired into /v1/messages relay and /v1/responses translated paths), post-response (non-streaming before delivery, streaming after the stream completes), provider-adapter (rewrites one outgoing upstream HTTP request per provider ID, applied by the provider client via plain func type `provider.HTTPAdapterFunc` — provider never imports plugins, no cycle; key-test probe clients do NOT carry the adapter). Per-hook failure semantics, tested: pre-request error fails closed (403, -32001, reason plugin_denied), pre-request panic fails closed as a gateway panic (502, -32603, the 165-166 rule), post-response error/panic fails open (logged redacted + counted, response delivered), adapter error/panic fails the attempt closed (unadapted request never sent; normal failover may try the next provider). Every plugin call runs behind its own recover boundary; panicking plugins are counted, not disabled. Two built-in example plugins: `header-add` (adds fixed `X-Codvian-Plugin: header-add` to every upstream request — plugin headers travel on the request context, reserved headers authorization/content-type/content-length/accept/host can never be overridden) and `response-log` (shape-only observations: model, finish reason, content length, token counts — never content; last 20 kept). Config validation rejects unknown/empty/duplicate plugin names naming the entry. Full design in `docs/plugins.md` | ✅ | ✅ |

| 193 | Lossy-compression × semantic-cache interaction (backend; real bug found by the 183-184 smoke): the gateway's semantic cache is keyed on the last user message's text, so a response produced from a LOSSY-trimmed context could be served to a later request with the same last user message but a different (full) context — a trimmed-context answer presented as a full-context answer. Fix, by EXCLUSION (not fingerprint scoping): a lossy-compressed request's response NEVER enters, and is NEVER served from, the semantic layer — one predicate, `degrade.lossyCompressed`, at the two call sites (`lookup`, `store` in `internal/degrade/degrade.go`). Fingerprint scoping was considered and rejected, and the reason is documented in code + `docs/compression.md`: a fingerprint would have to cover the whole (compressed) prompt, so a semantic hit would need exact fingerprint equality and the layer would degenerate into a second exact-match cache for lossy requests, with a new keying scheme and new failure modes, for no benefit (lossy requests already trade answer fidelity for tokens; the exact layer still serves identical lossy prompts). The predicate is fail-closed on two signals: the applied stamp (`req.Compressed.LossyApplied` — the server handler compressed before the wrapper saw the request) and the per-request opt-in (`req.Compression.LossyMaxPromptTokens > 0` — the only signal visible when compression happens inside the engine after the wrapper's lookup, for direct engine callers); a request that asked for lossy is excluded even when the trim ended up a no-op, at the cost of one cache miss. Lossless compression is harmless and keeps sharing — the lossless steps are exactly the canonical normal form (`compress.VerifyLossless`), so the meaning is unchanged — proven by test. Exact-match cache is unaffected: its key is the messages as sent, so a trimmed and a full prompt have different keys, and an identical lossy repeat still hits exactly; the stale fallback is the exact cache too, so it is unaffected for the same reason — both pinned by tests. The /v1/messages relay path needs no equivalent check: it never applies compression (`applyCompression` runs only in `handleChatCompletions`; the Anthropic shape carries no compression opt-in), so its semantic store/lookup can only ever see uncompressed requests — and since lossy responses never enter the shared semantic cache from the gateway path, the relay can never serve one either. Tests (12 new top-level + 7 subtests, all pass): degrade — exact 183-184 repro (`TestSemanticCacheNeverServesLossyResponse`), opt-in-only signal (`TestSemanticCacheNeverStoresLossyOptInResponse`), lossless sharing (`TestSemanticCacheLosslessSharingStillWorks`), exact unaffected (`TestExactCacheUnaffectedByLossy`), stale unaffected (`TestStaleServeUnaffectedByLossy`), stream + chunk-stream variants, symmetric lookup exclusion (`TestSemanticCacheLossyLookupSkippedForLossyRequester`), predicate pins (`TestLossyCompressedPredicate`); server (external `server_test` package — the internal server tests cannot import `degrade` without an import cycle) — HTTP repro, lossless sharing, exact hit (`TestChatCompletionsLossyResponseNeverServedSemantically`, `TestChatCompletionsLosslessResponseStillSharedSemantically`, `TestChatCompletionsLossyExactHitStillWorks`). Sensitivity proven: with the two guards removed, the degrade repro fails (lossy response stored semantically, then served to the full-context stream) and the HTTP repro fails (full-context request gets TRIMMED-ANSWER). Docs: `docs/compression.md` semantic-cache section rewritten from open issue to fixed-with-reasoning. Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): full `go test -count=1 -v ./...` — 1,832 PASS events (1,473 top-level + 359 subtests), 37 packages ok, 1 FAIL in another slice's health test (`TestHistoryPrunesOnWrite`: 2,980 records persisted in 140s under full parallel load vs its 60s budget — passes alone in 4.08s, load flake on the shared VM, not this slice); `go build` clean; `go vet` clean on touched packages (degrade, cache, compress, server); `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `go test -race` clean on degrade/cache/compress + this slice's server tests; live smoke 39/39 green (real binary + 1 Python mock, script `$HOME/.tmp/smoke-193-194/smoke-lossy-cache-193-194.py` — scratch, not committed; measured: lossy request mock saw 2 msgs → TRIMMED-ANSWER, full-context request mock saw 6 msgs → FULL-ANSWER with a fresh provider call, lossless semantic hit, identical lossy repeat exact hit, streaming pair full stream FULL-ANSWER, Overview semantic hits ≥ 1 / exact hits ≥ 1). | 199 | Decision transparency on every gateway response (backend, capability gap closed; a decision header on every response): every response served from a provider carries `X-Codvian-Decision: provider=<id>; model=<model>; strategy=<strategy>; reason=<reason>` — machine-readable, documented in `docs/decision-header.md`. The reason is computed by the engine at the decision moment and stored on `RoutingInfo` (`internal/router/decision.go`: `StrategyReason`, `decisionReason`, `recordDecision`/`recordRoutingDecision`), so the header and the engine's record can never disagree. Reason vocabulary + precedence: `fallback-after-N` (failures AND skips before dialling count; hedged losers do not — they ran in parallel) > `group` > `contract` > `tree` > strategy-derived (`cheapest`, `fastest`/`least-latency`→`latency`, `most-reliable`→`reliability`, anything else its own name). Surfaces: non-streaming chat, streaming chat (engine publishes at commit via `onCommit` in `forwardStream`/`forwardStreamPreread`, server sets the header on first chunk before the first SSE write), Responses (both forms), embeddings + rerank (single-attempt path), translated `/v1/messages`. The header is ABSENT, never faked, when: cache hit (`X-Codvian-Cache` instead), stale serve (`X-Codvian-Stale` instead), failure before any provider served, verbatim relay / relay stream (`X-Codvian-Relay` instead), or the engine's record cannot be matched to this request (model mismatch — withheld, except `auto/*` virtual models, where the record names the resolved concrete model). Cache/stale guard reads the degrade wrapper's published outcome; the degrade ordering fix (`internal/degrade/degrade.go`: `setCacheOutcome`/`setOutcome` now run BEFORE the replayed chunk is delivered in `ChatStream`/`ChatStreamChunks`) makes the guard true on streams. Tests: `internal/router/decision_test.go` (reason table incl. fallback counting, hedged-loser exclusion, precedence), `internal/server/decision_test.go` + `decision_cache_test.go` (header on all four surfaces incl. stream first-chunk + stream fallback-after-1, contract sort, failure absence, cache-hit absence with `X-Codvian-Cache` present, model-mismatch absence, header-value sanitisation). Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): full `go test -count=1 ./...` — 38 packages ok, 0 fail; verbose recount 1,919 PASS events (1,515 top-level + 404 subtests), 0 fail (logs `$HOME/.tmp/fulltest-199-200.log`, `$HOME/.tmp/fulltest-199-200-verbose.log`); `go test -race` clean on router/server/provider/config/degrade; `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt` clean; `node --check` on the dashboard JS clean; live smoke 27/27 green on 2 consecutive runs (real binary + 2 Python mocks, `scripts/smoke-decision-header.py`). | ✅ | ✅ |

| 223 | npm wrapper signature verification (backend, closing the 209-210 honest limitation — the npm wrapper downloaded the signed archives but verified nothing): `bin/codvian-router.js` now verifies before installing, same rule as `scripts/install.sh` — downloads `checksums.txt`, verifies the Sigstore cosign keyless signature over it when `cosign` is installed (identity pinned to the release workflow + this exact tag) and REFUSES on any signature failure (incl. an undownloadable bundle), then verifies the archive's SHA-256 (Node `crypto`, stdlib only) against the manifest and REFUSES on mismatch or a missing entry — before anything is extracted or executed; a failed verification deletes the downloaded archive. No-cosign behaviour (mirrors install.sh): loud warning + checksum-only, unless `CODVIAN_ROUTER_REQUIRE_SIGNATURE=1` refuses. Honest limits (documented in the wrapper header + `docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md`): an undownloadable `checksums.txt` warns loudly and installs WITHOUT verification (transient-GitHub trade-off, same as install.sh); a cached binary (`bin/.cache/`) is not re-verified on later runs (verified at download; delete the cache to force re-download + verification); the SBOM signature is not checked here (auditors use `scripts/verify-release.sh`); `CODVIAN_ROUTER_BINARY` bypasses download AND verification. New testing-only overrides `CODVIAN_ROUTER_DOWNLOAD_BASE_URL` / `CODVIAN_ROUTER_CACHE_DIR` (used by the test suite; whatever they point at is still verified exactly as a GitHub download would be); `install.sh` gained the same base-URL override, default behaviour unchanged. | 215 | Bedrock event-stream incremental delivery (backend, closing the 205-206 honest limitation — `readEventStream` did `io.ReadAll` first, so no chunk was delivered until the whole body arrived and a never-ending stream yielded nothing): `internal/wire/bedrock.go` — `readEventStream` is now a true streaming parser: it reads one frame at a time with `io.ReadFull` (12-byte prelude, then the frame body) and calls `fn` as soon as that frame's bytes have arrived, so the first chunk reaches the caller long before the body completes and a never-ending stream still streams. Only the frame in flight is buffered (one frame, not the whole body); the prelude CRC is verified before the frame body is allocated, so a corrupt prelude can never trigger a huge allocation. Frame semantics are unchanged — same events, same errors (truncated prelude/message, prelude/message CRC mismatch, no `:event-type`, mid-stream exception mapped via `MapBedrockStreamError`), same CRC32 checks — proven byte-for-byte by `TestReadEventStreamIdenticalToBufferedReader` (9 recorded-stream cases incl. text, tool-use + reasoning, empty, truncated, both CRC failures, exception, no-event-type) against a test-only copy of the old `ReadAll` reader. Cancel discipline from 205-206 is preserved: the client's `emit` still checks `ctx.Err()` before delivering each chunk (comment updated — it no longer claims the reader buffers the whole body). The 205 cancel test `TestBedrockStreamCancelAbortsInflightRead` was updated to the new semantics (its old `seen == 0` assertion was the limitation itself: chunks now DO arrive before the abort; it now asserts ≥ 1 chunk, `context.Canceled`, < 2 s, mock stops writing); `TestBedrockStreamCancelStopsBufferedFrames` is unchanged and still passes. No dashboard change: no user-visible status changes (transport behaviour only). | ✅ | ✅ |

Sensitivity proven: with the old reader restored (git stash), all 3 timing tests FAIL and pass again with the fix (an earlier run exposed a bug in the test's own trickle reader — it dropped frame bytes on short reads — fixed by resuming partial chunks). | 231 | `TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit` hardening (backend): REAL RACE/ORDER BUG, found and fixed — not a load-only flake. Root cause: `ChatStreamWithChainStrategy` returned on its entry `ctx.Err()` check without publishing any route trace. When the async BLOCK verdict lands before the engine's first attempt starts, the server's cut goroutine cancels the stream context with the `AsyncBlockError` cause before the engine is entered, so `ChatStreamChunks` → `ChatStreamWithChainStrategy` exits at that check and `LastTrace()` stays nil (fresh engine) or stale — the previous request's trace (reused engine). On a quiet machine the verdict wins that race every time (the 5/5 "trace is nil" failures for the 217–218 slice); under load the engine wins it and the cut lands on the attempt instead (1-attempt trace — why the sprint 11 coordinator gate passed). The non-streaming path never had this hole (`ChatWithChainStrategy` builds the trace first and publishes it on every early return, as do the contract/group rejections on the stream path). Fix (1 site, `internal/router/router.go`, mirroring the contract-error precedent in the same function): the early `ctx.Err()` return now publishes an attempt-less trace (endpoint + compression, no contract — none was applied yet, no attempt was made) before returning the error, so every request leaves a trace and `GET /dashboard/trace` never shows a stale one. Regression test `TestStreamCutBeforeEngineEntryPublishesTrace` (new, `internal/server/async_guardrail_test.go`): seeds a successful stream (so a stale trace exists to catch), then calls `ChatStreamChunks` on a context pre-cancelled with the `AsyncBlockError` cause — deterministic, no timing, no HTTP — and asserts a non-nil, 0-attempt trace. Sensitivity proven: FAILS without the fix (stale 1-attempt success trace survives, `trace attempts = [{... Outcome:success ...}], want none`), passes 10/10 with it. The existing `TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit` now passes in both branches (0-attempt trace when the verdict wins, 1-attempt cut trace when the engine wins) and logs which branch each run took (`t.Logf`, for the repeated-run record). Scope note: the fix is a 3-line change in `internal/router/router.go`, outside the slice's stated `internal/server` file list — it had to live there because only the engine can publish a route trace (the server has no API for it); no other behaviour changes (same error returned, same callers). | ✅ | ✅ |

What changes is an existing surface's correctness: `GET /dashboard/trace` (`handleTrace` renders `LastTrace()` verbatim) now shows the attempt-less trace for a pre-entry cut instead of the previous request's trace — verified by the regression test's stale-seed assertion, no dashboard code touched. | 237 | Responses branching / tree (backend): `POST /v1/responses` with `previous_response_id` branches a conversation from an earlier response — two creates naming the same parent are two branches, each keeping the parent's history (parent input + parent reply + new turn, re-sent as plain chat messages); the tree (parent → children) is kept durably in the ledger (`response_tree` table, created on first open of older DBs via `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, no backfill) as well as in the in-memory store (`Store.Children`); `GET /v1/responses/{id}` reports `child_response_ids` (ledger first, store fallback). Branched requests fail over mid-branch like any other request: the Responses-continuation replay exemption is removed from `router.ReplayExemption` (the transcript is gateway-held, so no upstream state exists to double-apply) and `pinChain` is now a reorder (original provider first, rest of the chain after) instead of a narrowing to one attempt. Unknown `previous_response_id` → honest 404 naming it, never a silent fresh start. | ✅ | ✅ |

BUILT — cost-cap (`internal/plugins/costcap.go`, `CostCapName = "cost-cap"`): pre-request hook, chat surfaces only (`/v1/chat/completions` + `/v1/messages`; embeddings/rerank/images/Responses pass through untouched). Estimate = local tiktoken prompt count (`tokencount.CountPromptFull`: text + tools + framing) × model-level input price + (`max_tokens` if set, else 1,024 assumed) × model-level output price; model-level prices only (pair prices cannot apply pre-routing — no provider is known yet). Over cap → 402 naming the estimated cost and the cap (new `RefusalDetails` interface on the registry, honoured by `applyPlugins`; the message carries numbers + model name only, never prompt content). No complete model-level price → 402 naming the missing price (unknown, never free — same fail-closed rule as key budgets). Cap 0/absent = unlimited (counts, never denies); negative cap and cap-without-plugin fail config load; positive cap with no price lookup fails plugin construction. Wiring: `plugins.cost_cap_usd_per_request` in `PluginsConfig`, `LoadBuiltinsConfigured` with cap + `priceBook.LookupModel` lookup in `buildPluginRegistry` (`cmd/codvian-router/main.go`); `docs/plugins.md` cost-cap section. | 243 | Failover cluster, research entries 222–224 (backend): (a) a pinned deployment/session-affinity pin is escapable on a retryable failure — a retryable failure on the pinned deployment must escape the pin; (b) Responses fallback must never replay a tool call the user already saw (delivered tool calls are never re-executed on fallback); (c) a stream that breaks before any output is a same-model-group retry, not a fresh chain; (d) global model_fallbacks must not recurse — fallback config never re-applies to its own fallback calls; (e) reset the upstream connection when a stream closes early so an aborted stream stops generating upstream (billed spend with no user); (f) per-turn token admission for long-lived Responses sessions — count a turn's tokens before the next turn is admitted; (g) refuse blocked and over-limit keys before reading the request body (admission before I/O); (h) name the deployment in stream error alerts. BUILT — (a) `pinFirst()` re-applied after the quota-hint sort in both `ChatWithChainStrategy` and `runStream` (`internal/router/failover_243.go`): the sort was demoting the pin (probe FAILED before the fix). Pin escapes counted once per request (`Failover243Stats`: `PinEscapes`, `LastPin`, `LastEscapedTo`, `LastEscapeAt`). (b) `chatStreamDrainChunks` filters delivered tool-call deltas via `DeliveredToolCallIDs` (`provider.ChatRequest`) + `filterDeliveredToolCalls` — a resume re-delivered a delivered call before the fix (probe FAILED); server-side `stripDanglingToolCalls` drops dangling calls from the Responses fallback request (stored transcript untouched). (c) pre-output break stays a same-model-group retry in `runStream` — verified already holding, regression tests only. (d) refusal/context fallback chains apply once — verified already holding (`tried` map / single application), regression tests only. (e) early close unwinds via ctx — verified already holding at engine level, regression test only (per-provider event-stream abort is provider-client code, outside this slice). (f) per-turn session admission: `responses.Record` gains turn/session token fields; `checkSessionTokenAdmission` refuses 429 `session_token_limit` when used + prompt estimate exceeds `WithResponsesSessionTokenCap` (default 0 = counting only); session totals accumulate on the stored record. (g) `preBodyAdmission` in `handleChatCompletions` + `handleResponses`: over-budget (`checkKeyBudgetForModel`) and over-traffic (`wouldRefuse` peek, no slot recorded) keys refused 402/429 before the body is read; unknown/revoked/expired keys already refused pre-body. (h) `streamErrorDetail` + `errorDetail.Deployment`: committed-stream SSE error frame and log line name the deployment from `LastTrace` (absent when no trace — never invented). Tests: 9 new router tests + 10 new server tests + 2 new degrade tests (21 total). Live-smoke findings, fixed in-slice: (1) the server holds the degrade-wrapped engine, so `StaleEngine` needed a `Failover243Stats()` forwarder (mirrors `RefusalChainStats`, `internal/degrade/degrade.go`) — without it the guardrails feed showed no `failover_243` at all; (2) the trace names the deployment by registry ID (`prov_…`), so the server resolves it to the operator-configured display name via the provider lister (ID fallback when unknown) — alerts, log lines and the feed now say "flaky", never an opaque ID. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 245 | Security cluster, research entry 226 (backend): (a) reject client-supplied Vertex location/project unless the admin opts in — a client must never override which cloud project/location a request lands in; (b) admin deployment config outranks client-supplied body fields — a body field must never silently override a deployment setting; (c) mask token-shaped secrets in stored request/response logs — redaction must match secret *shapes*, not just named fields; (d) reject unknown guardrail names on key/user/team/project writes — a typo'd guardrail name fails loudly at write time, never silently leaves a key unguarded. BUILT — (a) `provider.DetectVertexOverride` + `ChatRequest.ClientVertexOverride` (`internal/provider/client.go`): `vertex_location`/`vertex_project`/`vertex_ai_location`/`vertex_ai_project` detected at decode; server rejects with a 400 naming the field unless `allow_client_vertex_override: true` (config, default false), on chat completions, Responses, Messages and embeddings/rerank, before any provider is dialled; the value is never logged or echoed; refusal counted as `vertex-override`. (b) `Model.FixedParams` (`temperature` [0,2], `top_p` (0,1], `max_tokens` ≥ 1, range-checked at config load) + `provider.ApplyFixedParams`: the pin always wins, signalled by `X-Codvian-Deployment-Override`; enforced on chat, Responses and translated Messages (verbatim relay forwards the body unchanged by design, so no pin applies there). (c) `internal/guardrails`: new shape rules `aws-session-key` (`ASIA…`), `sendgrid-key` (both segments), `digitalocean-token` (`dop_v1_…`), plus the generic-token rule — a ≥32-char `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` run mixing digit + lowercase + uppercase is masked in any field; hex SHAs, UUIDs, long words and URL paths are untouched (gated on a single-pass shape scan, so clean lines pay no extra scan). (d) `guardrails.Config.ValidateGuardrailNames` (registry = built-in kinds `pii`/`secret`/`injection` + configured external names) wired into config load (every gateway key's `guardrails` list) and `POST /admin/keys/{id}` (400 naming the unknown name, counted as `unknown-guardrail`); `GatewayKey.Guardrails` added, inherited by rotate, shown in the keys feed. | 249 | Hot-path CPU profiling, E1 follow-up (backend): per-request CPU is the RPS ceiling (E1 verdict) — profile the hot path under harness load and cut the biggest CPU: known churn from the memstats slice — tiktoken regex ~83 MB, `ExactCache.touch` ~71 MB, `Provider.Clone` ~61.5 MB per ~24k requests — plus whatever the profile shows. Fix with a regression/benchmark proof (before/after added latency + RPS on the same harness), never a claim without a measured run. DONE 2026-11-18: CPU profile on the real binary under harness load (before run: `CountPromptWire` 4.30 s cum, 11.6% of all CPU — the same prompt encoded 3× per request; `CheckBudgetOrError` 4.09 s, 11.1% — the same budget row read twice per request; `GetBudget` 4.07 s; `_sqlite3Prepare` 3.49 s; `ExactCache.touch` 0.46 s; `Provider.Clone` 0.34 s, 0.9% — measured, not a lever, untouched; `guardrails.scan` 0.97 s — named, not touched, owned by slice 245–246). Fixes, all with regression/benchmark proof: (1) token-count memo in `internal/tokencount` on (encoding, fallback, SHA-256(text)), bounded 4,096 — collapses the 3 encodes per request to 1 (memo hit ~0.3 µs vs cold encode ~21–51 µs; the memo key MUST carry the fallback flag — keying on encoding alone returned the wrong Fallback for unknown models, caught by existing `TestCountKnownTexts` during this slice and fixed before any bench run); (2) single budget-row read per guard (`checkBudgetDetailed` in `internal/ledger/budget.go`, used by `CheckBudget`/`CheckBudgetOrError`/`CheckBudgetForEstimate`); (3) prepared `GetBudget`/`GetKeyBudget` statements (lazy, fallback to direct query, closed in `Close`) — NO separately measurable per-call win under contention (~42 µs/call before vs ~52 µs after), so none is claimed; the ledger win is the dedup, not the prepare; (4) allocation-free `ExactCache.touch` in place (`copy`, 0 allocs per touch). Profile-share proof on the same HEAD tree ± fixes: `CountPromptWire` 11.55% → 2.95% of router CPU (83 → 18 µs/request), `CheckBudgetOrError` 11.75% → 8.59%, `touch` 1.32% → 0.92%. Harness before/after pair (clean HEAD tree ± fixes, clean by the direct-mock canary, direct 16,218/15,560 before, 15,171/17,906 after): added latency 0.708 → 0.697 ms pass 1, 0.814 → 0.570 ms pass 2; RPS 692 → 1,650 pass 1, 764 → 650 pass 2 — the passes disagree under opposite load regimes (before rising 1.7 → 9, after falling 8.5 → 3.7), so NO RPS verdict is drawn; the numbers are reported as recorded, targets still MISSED (0.697/0.570 ms vs < 0.5 ms; 1,650/650 RPS vs > 3,000). Full report `docs/cpu-profile-2026-11-18.md`, merged file `docs/benchmark-2026-11-18-cpu.json` (+ `-cpu-before.json` / `-cpu-after.json`), `scripts/merge-bench-cpu.sh`. Two earlier pairs were polluted and discarded (worktree after-run canary 10,566/11,741; clean-tree attempt 1 canary 5,488/10,867 and 5,714/7,290); the worktree pair attempt died at `go build` on concurrent slices' mid-flight breakage (`responses.go`, `failover_243.go`), which is why the pair ran on the HEAD tree. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 251 | Test flakes + trace hole (backend): (a) fix the pre-existing `TestTrainingPairs` ordering flake in `internal/feedback` (map-iteration order under parallel load — passes 3/3 in isolation, fails under load; fix the ordering deterministically, never by skipping); (b) fix the adjacent hole the 231–232 slice flagged deliberately NOT fixed: the `len(chain) == 0` early return in `ChatStreamWithChainStrategy` publishes no trace — publish the same attempt-less trace there. DONE 2026-11-18: (a) Root cause: the test recorded signals by ranging over a `map[string]Signal` (random order) while `TrainingPairs` returns outcome (time) order, so pairs[0]/pairs[1] flipped whenever the map order did — reproduced failing in isolation on a 30× run (pair 0 = chat/discard with an earlier timestamp than pair 1 = coding/accept). Fix, two halves, neither weakening the assertion nor skipping the test: the test now records in a fixed slice order (dec-1 accept, dec-2 discard, dec-3 accept), and production `TrainingPairs` now orders `ORDER BY e.ts ASC, e.id ASC` — the insertion-id tiebreak makes the "oldest first" contract total, since same-millisecond signals were previously in unspecified SQLite order. (b) The `len(chain) == 0` early return in `ChatStreamWithChainStrategy` (`internal/router/router.go`, one hunk only) now publishes the same attempt-less trace the 231–232 fix publishes for the pre-entry ctx-cancel return (endpoint + compression, no attempts, no contract — none applied yet) before returning the same "empty fallback chain" error. Regression test `TestStreamEmptyChainPublishesTrace` (new, `internal/server/async_guardrail_test.go`): seeds a successful stream (so a stale trace exists to catch), then calls `ChatStreamChunks` with a model no chain serves — deterministic, no timing, no HTTP. Sensitivity proven: FAILS without the fix (stale 1-attempt success trace survives), passes 10/10 with it. Note: the first version also asserted a non-empty Endpoint — wrong, dropped: `traceEndpointFor` returns "" for plain chat completions (set only for /v1/messages), the same shape the 231–232 fix publishes; the stale-seed check is the invariant. | ✅ | ✅ |
