| 257 | Multi-region failover order, research entry 232 (backend): failover order is region-aware — same model in another region first (preserves the model and the prompt-cache shape; the 180–220 ms cross-region penalty is far cheaper than a model switch that rebuilds the cache), then an equivalent model from another provider, then a self-hosted open-weight model. Region is a first-class routing dimension on provider/model entries (operator-declared, never guessed). BUILT — `provider.Provider.Region` + `Model.Region` (operator-declared, `""` = unknown, never a default, never inferred; `EffectiveRegion`/`RegionFor` = model wins, else provider; `ValidateRegion` single-token shape check, never verified against a vendor list) with round-trip through `ToConfig`/`ProviderFromConfig`/`Clone`; `routing.model_equivalents` (operator-declared equivalents in preference order, self-list/duplicate/empty rejected at load); engine `Config.RegionFor`/`SelfHosted` (self-hosted = catalog-local or Ollama wire ONLY — a loopback base URL alone never counts, it may be a local proxy to a hosted provider; caught live by the smoke, which first mis-tiered every loopback mock as self-hosted) + `applyRegionTiers` in `orderAttempts` (flat path only, after quota sort + strategy hook, primary stays first: same-model-other-region → same-model-same-region → equivalent → self-hosted; explicit contract order stays final, tree-ordered chains untouched); `chainFor` in `main.go` appends declared equivalents (deduped by exact provider+model) so the tiers have targets, AND appends every model offered by a self-hosted provider — a self-hosted model is not an equivalent, so without this the last tier could never fire (caught live: R3 502'd with the local model never attempted); loop-top `noteRegionFailoverFromTrace` counts each step once per failure (only 429/5xx/timeout/empty-stream trigger it; terminal 400s, content refusals and skips never count); trace annotation in `setLastTrace` stamps `Region` + `FailoverStep` per attempt; `RoutingInfo.RegionStep` feeds the decision header — computed against the model the winner actually served, not the requested model (naming it against the requested model reported no step at all for equivalent-model/self-hosted winners; caught live, R2c/R3b). Tests: 8 new router tests (order proven by trace attempt sequence, incl. streaming; sensitivity proven — all 3 order tests FAIL with tiering disabled), provider region tests, config validation tests; `docs/region-failover-257-258.md` + `internal/config/SCHEMA.md`. | ✅ | ✅ |
| 258 | Multi-region failover order (dashboard, same slice): the decision header / route trace names the region step when a cross-region failover happens; the live view shows region-aware failover counts. BUILT — `X-Codvian-Decision` gains trailing `; region_step=cross-region|equivalent-model|self-hosted` only when the serving attempt stepped (absent when the first target served); `GET /dashboard/trace` carries `region` + `failover_step` per attempt and the Live Routing view renders both; `GET /dashboard/guardrails` gains `failover_257` (live cross-region / equivalent-model / self-hosted counts + most recent step, since process start; absent when the engine reports none) rendered in full in the Guardrails view (`#grFailover257`) and as a one-line count in the Live Routing card (`#liveRegionFailover`, refreshed with the live feed); `StaleEngine` forwards the stats. `docs/decision-header.md` + README.md/README.v2.md updated in the same slice. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 261 | Supply-chain release hardening, research entries 233 + 235 (backend/docs): the LiteLLM v1.107.0 lesson carved into the release checklist before the bulk push — (1) revoke, don't just delete: any key that ever touched a public repo is revoked immediately; (2) release approval sits with one dedicated account, owner work moves to separate accounts that cannot be used through tokens or the `gh` CLI; (3) published artifacts are verified against their provenance (Sigstore signature + SBOM + attestation) before anything is announced; (4) an independent check of published packages. Build the verification tooling (`codvian-router release verify` or equivalent) that checks a published release's signatures/SBOM/attestation and fails loudly on any mismatch — the Sigstore signing (209–210) and image signing (221–222) already exist; this slice wires the *verification* side. Real-layout proof: GoReleaser v2.20.2 snapshot build proved checksums.txt lists archives AND SBOMs (SPDX-2.3/SPDXRef-DOCUMENT) and the engine reads that real dist/ correctly. Gaps (honest): Windows cross-compile broken at HEAD (`internal/tray/supervisor.go` syscall.Kill/Setpgid + `internal/provider/tfo.go` Unix-only socket option, no Windows guard — a real release fails at the Windows build step, release-blocking, other slices' code); keyless/Sigstore live path unproven until the first tag; real-cosign sign/verify end-to-end unproven in this VM (timestamp service unreachable; cosign v3.1.4 rejects --tlog-upload); provenance-change comparison stays a manual step; `goreleaser check` fails in the no-remote worktree by design (green in a scratch clone with a remote). Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on internal/release + cmd/codvian-router; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `go test -count=1` 71 PASS / 0 FAIL / 1 SKIP (real-cosign, by design) across both touched packages; `go test -race` clean on both; `goreleaser check` green (scratch clone). Live binary smoke: `release verify` against the real snapshot dist/ fails loudly and correctly (unsigned snapshot — missing bundles + missing Windows archives); remote download against a local server fails loudly on the missing bundle (404). See the gate section at the end of this file. | ✅ | ✅ |

DONE 2026-11-18: `supervisor.go` no longer touches `syscall` directly — `supervisor_unix.go` (`Setpgid`, SIGTERM→SIGKILL to the group) and `supervisor_windows.go` (`CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, CTRL_BREAK_EVENT to the group then `Process.Kill`; the Go runtime delivers the break as SIGINT, which `serve` handles, so it is a real graceful stop) implement `configureChildProcessGroup`/`stopChildProcessGroup`; `tfo.go` calls a per-OS `tfoSetSocketOption` (Windows: `SetsockoptInt(syscall.Handle(fd), TCP_FASTOPEN=15)` — the int-fd call does not compile on Windows; Linux 30, macOS 0x105, other platforms honestly error). `golang.org/x/sys` is now a direct dependency (v0.22.0). Tests are platform-split too (`cmd.exe`/`timeout`/`exit 1` + `OpenProcess` liveness on Windows). `GOOS=windows go build ./...` and `go vet` clean; snapshot build green (see 268). Gate section below. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 289 | Release-verify CI wiring (backend): add the `release verify` step to the release workflow so the Windows archives + SBOM + bundle are checked on every tag in CI (the local snapshot proof exists; CI is where the Windows binary finally executes — record that execution is still unproven until the first CI run). Built + Verified 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4, this slice): `.github/workflows/release.yml` gains a `Verify the built release with release verify` step in the `release` job, immediately after the GoReleaser build step and before the image-digest/attestation steps — it builds the verifier from the checked-out tree and runs `release verify "<tag>" --dir dist --skip-image --skip-attestation` against the `dist/` the job just produced (all six archives incl. both Windows zips + SBOMs + Sigstore bundles, cosign installed earlier in the job; the tool fails closed when cosign is missing). No new `uses:` entry (pinning policy untouched), no change to the `release verify` tool itself. Honest limits, recorded in the workflow comment + docs: GoReleaser builds AND publishes in one step, so the check runs after publish — a tripwire, not a pre-publish gate, and it cannot un-publish; it never executes a binary, so Windows execution stays unproven until a CI run runs the exe; the first CI execution is pending the first pushed tag. Docs updated in the same slice: `docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md` (pipeline steps 3–5 + Limitations) and `docs/RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md` (tag+publish bullet). Gate section below. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 305 | Bedrock abort sensitivity (backend, 277 limitation): the two abort tests PASS on the old code too (transport close already aborted) — make the abort proof fail-without-fix: a test that distinguishes the explicit abort from the incidental transport close (e.g. a stream whose connection stays open unless the client cancels, or a time-to-stop bound the old code cannot meet); do not claim the tests fail without the fix unless they actually do. Built + Verified 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4, this slice): new test `TestBedrockAbortOnConsumerErrorStopsUpstreamImmediately` (`internal/provider/bedrock_abort_test.go`) — the mock gates a 1 ms tick behind a signal sent the moment chunk 1 is delivered, so every frame after the signal is purely post-abort; bound ≤2 post-signal frames. Sensitivity PROVEN in a separate worktree (~/workspace/.build-tmp/wt-305-306, committed HEAD db5c626): with the 277 hunks reverted (`git show ed41af3^:internal/provider/bedrock_client.go`) the test FAILS 29/30 runs (mock emits 10–50 frames after the abort — the deferred body close is ~10–50 ms late); with the fix restored it PASSES 30/30 (0 post-signal frames, incl. 5 under -race). The two original 40 ms-tick tests still pass on the old code (re-verified 5×), so they prove the behaviour but not the fix — the new test is the fail-without-fix proof. Tried and rejected: a Content-Length burst candidate (whole stream delivered, then handler blocks) fails on the NEW code too — once the body is fully read the transport legitimately keeps the connection for reuse (verified with a standalone transport probe: Content-Length + cancel + close → server never notices; chunked + close → closes in ~100 ms) — not an abort scenario, so it was not kept. Gate section below. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 307 | Release-verify pre-publish gate verdict (backend, 289–290 limitation): the wired step runs AFTER GoReleaser publishes, so it is a tripwire that cannot un-publish a bad release — investigated as a restructure (build-only → verify → publish the same dist/) and PROVEN impossible with the pinned OSS GoReleaser v2.20.2: `goreleaser publish` does not exist in OSS (the binary prints the release help) and `release --prepare` is rejected as an unknown flag — both are GoReleaser Pro-only (goreleaser.com/cmd/goreleaser_publish, /goreleaser_release); a second `release` run cannot publish the verified dist/ instead (`build` is not a skippable step in v2.20.2, and a run without --clean refuses an existing dist/ with `dist is not empty, remove it before running goreleaser or use the --clean flag`, so it wipes and rebuilds) — measured locally 2026-11-18: two consecutive snapshot builds of the same committed tree produced different SHA-256 for all 12 archives + SBOMs, so a rebuild's bytes are provably not the verified bytes. Draft-release gating rejected (gates only the GitHub blobs while the Docker image is pushed, signed and :latest-tagged in the same run before any verify, and a failed verify cannot un-push it) and manual `gh release create` + `docker push` + `cosign sign` re-publishing rejected (re-implements GoReleaser's publish pipe by hand with unpinned tooling and no local proof for the registry half). The tripwire is therefore the FINAL design, recorded in the workflow header + docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md (pipeline step 3 + Limitations) + docs/RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md; no workflow step changed, no change to the `release verify` tool itself. Follow-up, not this slice: a GoReleaser Pro licence would unlock --prepare/publish (maintainer's call). Gate section below. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 311 | Forced-reasoning policy (backend): operator policy forcing a reasoning effort on a model/route (e.g. always-high for a coding tier) — declared in config, applied at the engine attempt, visible in the trace; never silently overridden by a client value unless the policy says the client may override. Built + Verified 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4, this slice): config `reasoning.forced_effort` rules — a concrete model, a virtual model/route (`auto/coding`, `intent/coding`), or `*` for every model; first exact match wins, then the wildcard; a bad rule (empty model, unknown/unnormalized effort, duplicate model) fails config load with the field named. The engine applies the policy at the Chat/ChatStream entry points (requested model + client effort captured before auto-resolution rewrites them): precedence policy > intent-tier > routing-group entry > client, and `allow_client_override: true` is the only way a client value wins (with no client value the policy still forces). The decision rides every attempt including failover hops and group-resolved chains (re-stamped after resolveGroupChain, so a group replacing the entry-point chain cannot drop it) and into the trace per attempt (`forced:high:policy:auto/coding`, `client-override:low:policy:model-b`); the forced effort is forwarded on the wire through the existing effort machinery (OpenAI named effort; Anthropic/Gemini/Vertex numeric budget from `reasoning.effort_budgets`). A client-supplied policy label is dropped (field is `json:"-"` and the engine overwrites it unconditionally). 16 new test funcs pass (3 config lookup/validation/round-trip, 13 router). Gate section below. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 313 | ClientFor empty-key decrypt bug (backend): FIXED — root cause: a provider stored with no key at all carries an empty ciphertext (`APIKeyEncrypted == ""`), and `clientForKey` fed it to `decryptKey` unconditionally; `Vault.Unseal` rejects `""` as an unknown seal format (no `v1:`/`v2:` prefix), so `ClientFor` failed for exactly the providers that need no key — while `RelayTargetForModel` had already been fixed to skip decryption when no key is stored. Fix: `decryptKey` now returns `"", nil` for an empty ciphertext (`internal/provider/registry.go`) — an empty stored key means no key, not a corrupt seal; the downstream path was already correct (`catalog.ResolveAuth` returns empty headers, no error, for AuthNone surfaces with an empty credential; `NewClient` accepts an empty key). An auth provider stored with no key still fails honestly downstream (`ResolveAuth` refuses the empty credential — pinned by a negative-control test). Regression tests (`internal/provider/clientfor_empty_key_test.go`): `TestClientForEmptyKeyLocalNoAuthOllama` (`ClientFor` + `TestKey` + `Chat` against a mock Ollama-wire upstream, no `Authorization` header sent), `TestDecryptedKeyEmptyForNoAuthProvider`, `TestClientForEmptyKeyAuthProviderStillFails`. Gate 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4): all 3 new tests FAIL on the old code (`decrypting provider API key: crypto: sealed value has an unknown format`) and PASS with the fix; `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on provider/crypto; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files (`client.go`'s gofmt flag is another slice's concurrent edit, untouched here); `go test -race` clean on provider/crypto; full `go test -count=1 ./...` on this tree: 2,402 PASS events (1,943 top-level + 459 subtests), 0 fail, 39/39 packages ok (an earlier full run hit a transient foreign build failure in `internal/server` — another slice's mid-flight edit, green on re-run); live smoke 7/7 (real binary + two Ollama-wire mocks, `$HOME/.tmp/smoke-313-314-bin/smoke.py` — scratch, not committed): old binary chat 502 with zero mock hits, fixed binary chat 200 `smoke says hi` from the first mock, second untouched, no `Authorization` header on either. Honest limits: the smoke's no-key state is reached by patching `config.json` (`api_key_encrypted` `""` + `keys` `[]`) after an API add, because the add flow seals the empty key into the pool — a provider added via the API alone never hits the bug path; only a provider stored with a literally empty key does. | ✅ | ✅ |

| 317 | Outcome attribution under concurrency (backend): outcome attribution may use a neighbour's decision/trace under concurrency — attribute each outcome to its own decision/trace (request-scoped attribution, never a global last-decision read). Built + Verified 2026-11-18 (Go 1.27.4, this slice): root cause — the server attributed every finished request by reading the engine-global `LastTrace()` / `LastAutoDecision()` after the engine call returned, and a neighbour finishing in that window left its own decision/trace in the slots (the old code even admitted it in comments: "these stats are approximate telemetry"). Fix — a per-request `router.Attribution` sink carried on the request context (`router.WithAttribution`): the engine publishes the decision at `resolveAuto` time (from the value just computed, never by re-reading the global) and the trace when each chat/stream call finishes (`ChatWithChainStrategy` defer, `runStream` defer, stream entry rejections, coalesced waiters get the shared flight's trace); the server reads the sink first (`requestDecision` / `requestTrace` in `internal/server/server.go`) and falls back to the globals only when no sink was attached. Applied to the outcome-recording path on all three serving surfaces (chat, `/v1/messages` translated, `/v1/responses`): `recordIntentOutcome` (learning-loop cells), `recordMetrics` (provider/model + failovers), `setAutoRouteHeader` + stream-path registration (feedback decision id header), and `checkRequestBudgetOverage` (overage provider). The feedback store itself needed no change (already keyed by decision id). 4 new router test funcs + 2 new server test funcs, incl. a concurrency test that FAILS on the old code (proven in a HEAD worktree: header names the neighbour's `dec-neighbour`, outcome lands in the neighbour's cell) and PASSES with the fix. Gate section below. | ✅ | ✅ |

Verification is the concurrency tests (router: 8 parallel requests with distinct decisions, one delayed 500 ms, each sink holds its own decision/trace; stream path; coalesced-waiter path; empty-chain stream rejection — plus a control pinning that the globals hold the neighbour's values while the slow request is in flight; server: a double that publishes the request's own decision/trace into the sink while the globals report a neighbour's, proving the outcome cell + decision-id header follow the sink, non-streaming and streaming) plus the live smoke 16/16 (`scripts/smoke-attribution-317.py`: real binary + 2 Python mocks — 8 parallel intent chats, 8 distinct decision ids, exact 4/4 suggestion cells, feedback round-trip on the request's own id, 2 parallel streams growing the cells to 5, dashboard with no Playground). README.md + README.v2.md updated in the same slice. Gate section below. | ✅ | ✅ |

BUILT — the refusal stays at request time (the plugin's behaviour since 239): a capped model without a complete model-level price is refused 402 naming the model, not a startup error (the served-model set changes as providers are added live, and refusing to start the whole gateway for one unpriced model would take down every other model; the choice is documented in `docs/plugins.md`). Pinned by `TestPluginsCostCapPairPriceOnlyStillRefused`: the lookup is built from a real price book exactly the way `buildPluginRegistry` in `cmd/codvian-router/main.go` builds it (model-level entries only, both sides required) — a model with only a pair price is refused 402 naming the missing model-level price with zero provider calls, and the same model is allowed (200, one mock hit) once the model-level price is added to the same book. New feed: `GET /dashboard/plugins` carries a `cost_cap` block (`enabled`, `cap_usd_per_request`, `models_checked`, `unpriced_models` sorted by model ID with `has_pair_price`) computed from the live registry + price book; a half-known model-level price (one side missing) counts as unpriced, and pair prices (operator or models.dev-synced) count only as the `has_pair_price` signal, never as a price. `Registry.CostCap()` (new `CostCapper` interface, `costCapPlugin.CostCapUSD()`) reports the cap without parsing the description string. | ✅ | ✅ |

One test bug found by the gate: `TestIntentChatUnmappedTierRefused` asserted on the raw JSON body for `"best"`, which is escaped there (`\"best\"`); fixed to decode the error envelope.
- `go test -race` — clean on all four touched packages (autorouter 3.4s, config 1.8s, cmd 3.9s, server 134.6s), 0 data races.
- `go test -count=1 ./...` (full suite) — **39/39 packages ok, 0 fail**.

- Live smoke (real binary + two Python OpenAI mocks, ports 21451–21453, scratch HOME `~/.tmp/smoke-259-260`, script `scripts/smoke-intent-tiers.py`): **31/31 green** — preview resolves intent/fast to model-b at effort low; unmapped intent/best previews 502 and chats 400 naming the tier; /v1/models lists all three intent/* names; 12 intent/fast chats served by model-b (solid mock hit 12×, flaky 0); runtime remap fast→model-a + 10 failing intent/fast + 24 failing auto/coding produce one intent_remap (evidence 10 vs 12 requests, 0% vs 100% success) and a coding tier_demote; approve moves fast back to model-b (live chat 200 with X-Codvian-Intent-Tier: fast / X-Codvian-Intent-Effort: low), revert restores model-a and the suggestion is pending again; demote approve/revert round-trips the same way; the reverted mapping routes the final failing chat to model-a (flaky +1, model-a requests 11).
- Smoke-script fixes made during the gate (all in the script, not the product): the script built the binary from a hardcoded main-worktree path instead of its own tree, so the first gate run did not test the committed snapshot; B6 called GET /v1/models without the gateway key (401 → empty id list); B4 asserted on the raw JSON body for `"best"`; the C1 prompts were one template with a trailing number, so the semantic cache served 11 of 12 from cache (solid hit once) and no remap evidence was recorded; E6/F1 chats were breaker-skipped (5 consecutive failures open the breaker for 30s) — the script now polls GET /dashboard/cooldown until the target provider's breaker closes. First smoke run was 19/30; every failure traced to the script, none to the product (except the unit-test assertion above).
- Staging hazards found and fixed during this slice (for the coordinator): (1) `git apply --cached --unidiff-zero` on a U0 hunk-selective patch MISPLACED a pure-insertion hunk by its new (worktree) line number — the first commit put the setAutoRouteHeader block outside the function (would not compile); re-staged from a U3 patch with context pinning. (2) A merged U3 hunk carried the 255–256 slice's guard_test.go edits (64 MANAGEMENT + stream-lifetime) into this slice's commit; repaired by hand-building the file's staged blob. (3) A `git commit --amend` landed on the 263–264 slice's just-pushed commit and rewrote it; restored from the reflog (0804293) and committed the fix separately instead. Slice commits: 7f41174 (slice), 8763307 (smoke-script fixes), 19964c9 (breaker waits). No amend was used after (3).
- Honest limitations (also in the README + dashboard): the resolved effort is reported (decision, headers, stats), not yet carried onto the engine attempt (item 255's Attempt.Effort wiring is the follow-up); approved changes apply at runtime only (feed says persisted=false); suggestion evidence is in-memory and resets on restart; DB wipe does not clear the in-memory outcome stats; a demote revert re-adds the model appended at end rather than restoring the exact previous list; intent models on /v1/messages relay and /v1/responses were NOT verified (an unmapped tier there likely surfaces as a generic empty-chain error, not the tier-naming 400); outcome attribution under concurrency may occasionally use a neighbour's decision/trace (same approximation as recordMetrics).

## Route-guard tiers gate (2026-11-17, Go 1.27.4, measured by subagent)

- `go build ./...` — OK; `go vet ./...` — OK; `gofmt -l cmd internal web` — clean
- `go test -count=1 ./...` — **all 19 packages ok, 487 PASS events, 0 fail** (was 475 at the TFO gate by the same counting method; +12 new: route-table gate, register panic, loopback-peer matrix, local-only-before-auth, shutdown-without-hook, not-implemented Tier 1 routes, always-protected-ignores-relaxed, management-auth-default, settings-export-without-config, bootstrap loopback-only, bootstrap-without-issuer, guard feed)
- `go test -count=1 -race` on touched packages (server, cmd) — **clean**
- `scripts/check-route-guard.sh` (bonus CI entry point for the table gate) — **pass**
- Dashboard JS: extracted `<script>` from `web/index.html`, `node --check` — **clean**; "Playground" absent from the served dashboard
- Live smoke (real binary, isolated HOME, router on 20199 with `--listen 0.0.0.0:20199`, 2026-11-17; script kept at `/tmp/guard-smoke/smoke.sh` — ephemeral, not in the repo): **28/28 checks passed**
  - **G0** startup log: `route guards: ON (Tier 1 LOCAL_ONLY enforced before auth by real TCP peer — no Host/X-Forwarded-For, no manage-scope bypass; Tier 2 ALWAYS_PROTECTED always needs a gateway key; Tier 3 MANAGEMENT auth required)` + `bootstrap: PENDING`
  - **G1** `GET /dashboard/guard` → 200, tiers LOCAL_ONLY 7 / ALWAYS_PROTECTED 1 / MANAGEMENT 13, `bootstrap_pending: true`, `loopback_only_enforced: true`
  - **G3** `POST /admin/bootstrap` loopback → **201**, key returned (36 chars); **G4** second call → **409**; **G4b** guard feed then reports `bootstrap_pending: false`
  - **G6** `POST /admin/plugin/load` loopback + key → **501** (guard passed, feature not built); **G6b** `POST /admin/shutdown` loopback without key → **401** (loopback alone is not enough)
  - **G7** `POST /admin/settings/export` no key → **401**; with key → **200**, says "redacted", `gateway_key_count: 1`, no `base_url` in the payload
  - **G8** `GET /admin/status` no key → **401**, with key → **200**
  - **G9** `GET /dashboard` → 200, "Route guards" nav + `view-guard` + `loadGuard` present, "Playground" absent
  - **G10** `POST /admin/shutdown` loopback + key → **202** and the server process actually exits
- Non-loopback 403s (the core of this slice) are proven by unit tests, not by the live smoke — and that is an environment limit, not a gap in the code: this VM's sandbox resets every TCP connection to a non-loopback address (raw socket to 198.19.0.2 → `ConnectionResetError`, IPv6 egress → `Operation not permitted`, rtnetlink/netns operations denied), so no live non-loopback client can reach the server from here. The unit tests send real httptest requests with non-loopback `RemoteAddr` values through the full `ServeHTTP` stack: `TestLocalOnlyBeforeAuth` (non-loopback + valid key → 403, non-loopback + no key → 403 not 401, loopback + key → 202 with the hook run, ::1 → 202), `TestBootstrapLoopbackOnly` (non-loopback + forged `X-Forwarded-For: 127.0.0.1` + `Host: localhost` → 403, issuer never runs), `TestGuardFeed` (denial counter is a real 1 after one refused request). A parent agent with a live browser / second host should re-run G2/G5 live (bootstrap and shutdown from a non-loopback address with a valid key → 403) outside this sandbox.
- One test failure found and fixed during the gate (not hidden): the settings-export leak-word assertion tripped on the honest word "hashes" inside the redaction notice itself ("gateway-key hashes are never exported") — the assertion now checks for actual secret material (`"base_url"`, `"api_key"`, `sha256`) instead of the substring "hash"
- cross-gateway comparison (this slice): the `ROUTE_GUARD_TIERS.md` (three static tiers, evaluated before any other auth branch, `403 LOCAL_ONLY` before auth, bootstrap loopback-only by real peer, `403 LOCAL_ONLY` logged as the guard working). Codvian Router now matches that shape at its own scale — 21 routes classified vs the sector-typical ~40 prefixes/patterns — with two deliberate strictenings: (1) NO manage-scope bypass at all (the sector baseline lets a `manage`-scoped key reach `/api/mcp/` from off-host; Codvian has no bypassable Tier 1 route), and (2) loopback alone never suffices for Tier 1 — a valid key is still required after the loopback check (the sector baseline allows loopback with any/no Bearer). Where Codvian is still behind: a comparable gateway's breadth (MCP/CLI-tools/services/tunnels/VNC/login flows as live spawn-capable routes, the `SPAWN_CAPABLE_PATTERNS` regex companion for dynamic segments, the operator-credential second check on credential exports, and the OpenAPI `x-loopback-only`/`x-always-protected` consistency scripts) — Codvian's five spawn routes are honest 501s, DB export/import/wipe have no route yet, and the settings export's second operator-credential check does not exist because the export redacts secrets instead of returning them. Next step toward parity: implement one real Tier 1 feature (e.g. tunnel start) and one real Tier 2 destructive route (DB export with the operator-credential boundary) behind the already-built guards.

## TFO + adaptive-concurrency gate (2026-11-17, Go 1.27.4, measured by subagent)

- `go build ./...` — OK; `go vet ./...` — OK; `gofmt -l cmd internal web` — clean
- `go test -count=1 ./...` — **all 19 packages ok, 475 PASS events, 0 fail** (was 433 at the auto-router gate by the same counting method; +42 new: 8 adaptive limiter, 3 provider TFO incl. cold-dial measurement, 4 router concurrency, 3 server concurrency endpoint, 1 metrics concurrency series, plus subtests)
- `go test -count=1 -race` on touched packages (adaptive, provider, router, server, metrics, degrade, cmd) — **clean**
- TFO probe on this VM (measured): supported=true, active=true, `net.ipv4.tcp_fastopen=1` (client bit set), linux/amd64; dial attempts counted live, 0 errors across the gate + smoke (24 attempts in the live smoke run)
- Cold-dial latency, fresh client per request, n=20, localhost (`TestTFOColdDialLatencyMeasured`, 3 runs): **TFO median 0.26–0.31 ms, mean 0.33–0.37 ms | no-TFO median 0.25–0.27 ms, mean 0.52–0.63 ms** — no measurable gain on localhost, as expected: the RTT is ~0, so saving one RTT saves ~nothing. The test asserts only that TFO never makes cold dials pathologically slower, not that it wins here. First-request-after-server-start in the live smoke (fresh transport, cookie warmed by the startup probe, 5ms mock delay): **TFO on mean 8.26 ms / median 8.25 ms | off mean 8.46 ms / median 8.36 ms** — a 0.2 ms difference, within noise. TFO's payoff needs a real RTT; nothing here claims otherwise
- Live smoke (real binary, isolated HOME, Python mocks on 21061 slow / 21062 fast, router on 20199, 2026-11-17; scripts kept at `/tmp/tfo-smoke/` — ephemeral, not in the repo): **18/18 checks passed**
  - **S0** startup log: `tcp fast open: ACTIVE (linux/amd64; TCP_FASTOPEN_CONNECT accepted; net.ipv4.tcp_fastopen=1 (client bit set))` + `adaptive concurrency: ON (Gradient2 per provider: cap starts at 20, floor 2, ceiling 100; … limiter fails open)`
  - **S1–S2** probe + 5 fast (5 ms) requests → slowing provider (mock-A, detected adaptively as the first-tried mock) serves all 6, fast serves 0
  - **S3** slowing provider set to 120 ms (< 150 ms hedge, so no hedge interferes), 6 sequential requests → `/dashboard/concurrency` shows **limit 2.00 (floor), baseline 5.14 ms, smoothed RTT 91.0 ms, 12 samples**
  - **S4** burst of 10 concurrent distinct requests → **slow serves 2, fast serves 8, slow max-concurrent 2, all 10 answered 200**; shed happened via deprioritisation (saturated provider ordered last), so `rejected=0` — the counter only counts `TryAcquire` refusals, and no request needed one
  - **S5** TFO active/supported, kernel `1`, dial attempts 24, errors 0; `/metrics` carries `codvian_router_concurrency_limit` + `codvian_router_concurrency_rejected_total`
  - **S7** dashboard serves, Concurrency section present, "Playground" absent; dashboard JS `node --check` clean (live-browser click-through not run — subagents cannot operate the live browser; the endpoint the JS calls is proven live above)
- Two design corrections found by the tests, not hidden: (1) the baseline-drift factor 0.002/sample moved the baseline +0.79 ms per 400 ms sample (a huge-gap drift), so it is now 0.0002/sample (~0.4 ms over 5 slow samples) — a permanently slower provider still adapts, over thousands of samples, instead of pinning the cap at the floor forever; (2) the first router tests fed 500 ms samples with no fast baseline, so the gradient was correctly 1 and the cap correctly never shrank — the tests now warm a 5 ms baseline first, which is how Gradient2 is meant to be driven
- Honest limits of this slice: the limiter's RTT comes from non-streaming completions only (a stream's wall time is not a serving RTT); hedged streaming attempts are not limiter-counted (the hedge race owns their lifetime); shedding via `TryAcquire` refusal only fires when a request actually attempts a saturated provider — under the deprioritisation ordering most sheds happen before any refusal, so `rejected` stays 0 while shedding is working; the gradient needs a fast baseline first (a provider that is slow from its very first call gets no cap shrink until it has been fast — by design, Gradient2 measures degradation against its own minimum); Windows/macOS TFO constants (15 / 0x105) are from the Winsock/macOS socket-option values and are probe-gated, not verified on those platforms in this VM
- the sector baseline comparison (this slice): a comparable gateway's adaptivity is header/RPM-based — its `rateLimitManager` learns a minimum interval from upstream rate headers (and issue #9763 shows that learning can override an operator-configured minimum, a known bug) — and its in-flight cap is a fixed heavyweight ceiling (`CHAT_MAX_HEAVY_IN_FLIGHT`, unset by default = no cap) plus an auto-derived byte budget. No per-provider RTT-gradient cap and no TCP Fast Open were found in its docs. Where this slice is ahead: Codvian Router now sheds load from a slowing provider BEFORE any 429 appears, from measured RTT rather than headers, with a fail-open guarantee and a measured TFO path — the sector baseline reacts to 429s/headers after the fact. Where it is still behind: the sector-typical breadth (per-account rate limiting with provider-specific profiles, TPM awareness, byte budgets, 18 routing strategies, 268 providers) is far beyond this one mechanism; parity there is a multi-slice job.

- Calibration (honest, not tuned to hide): 11 representative prompts route to the right tier; 2 long reasoning prompts score 0.41–0.44 (below the 0.5 threshold) but still resolve to the reasoning model via the safe fallback — fallback is the strong tier, so no misroute. Unrelated/gibberish prompts score 0.09–0.14 and fall back. Coding/chat/quick-qa prompts that fell back would misroute, so those must clear the threshold — all tested ones do (0.61–0.83 live)
- Live smoke (real binary, isolated HOME, Python mocks on 21051 coder / 21052 chat, router on 20199, 2026-11-17; scripts kept at `/tmp/auto-smoke/` — ephemeral, not in the repo): **26/26 checks passed**
  - **S1 `auto/coding` "Write a Python function to reverse a string"** → coder mock, `X-Codvian-Auto-Tier: coding`, `X-Codvian-Auto-Model: coder-model`, confidence 0.607
  - **S2 `auto` "Tell me a funny joke about cats"** → chat mock, tier `chat`, confidence 0.832
  - **S3 `auto` "zxqv blorp wumpus flibberty"** → fallback to `reasoner-model`, tier `reasoning`, confidence 0.135, `X-Codvian-Auto-Fallback: true`
  - **S4 `GET /dashboard/auto-route?model=auto/coding&prompt=…`** → live decision JSON (tier coding, model coder-model, per-tier scores, latency_micros 91)
  - **S5 `GET /dashboard/auto-route/last`** → `has_decision: true`, the S3 fallback decision (tier reasoning, fallback true)
  - **S6 `GET /v1/models`** → all 5 virtual models listed, `owned_by: codvian-router`
  - **S7 `GET /dashboard`** → 200, Auto-router nav + "Route it" present, honest empty states ("No routing decision yet", "No auto-routed request yet"), "Playground" absent
  - **S8 streaming `auto/coding`** → SSE chunks from coder mock + `[DONE]`
  - Dashboard JS syntax-checked with `node --check` (live-browser click-through not run — subagents cannot operate the live browser; the endpoints the JS calls are proven live above)
- Concurrency note: another worker edited `web/index.html` and `internal/server/server.go` concurrently (dashboard verification badges); all auto-router edits were made against freshly re-read files with anchored edits, and the final gate re-ran after their changes — nothing was clobbered (16 auto-router references in server.go, 11 in index.html, all present at gate time)
- Environment note: the first smoke run failed with "No space left on device" — /tmp is a 512M tmpfs and the smoke HOME had redirected Go's module/build cache there. Fixed in the smoke script (`GOCACHE`/`GOPATH` pinned to the real home); not a product issue
- the sector baseline comparison (this slice): the sector baseline already ships an `auto` model with 22 routing strategies, 16-factor auto-scoring and a 4-tier cascade — Codvian Router is NOT at parity on strategy breadth (one local strategy, no quality signal, no cost-aware tiering yet). Where this slice is ahead: the decision costs ~30 µs measured with zero extra LLM call and zero network call (the sector-typical "milliseconds" failover is a vendor claim with no published benchmark), the fallback is explicit and honest (never a guess), and the whole path is ToS-clean with keys encrypted at rest. Next step toward parity: a second strategy (cost/latency-aware tier ordering via the existing scoring signals) and a measured quality signal from the eval harness.

## Live benchmark — Phase 2–4 integration gate (real binary, Python mock upstreams, 2026-11-17, run by coordinator)

Same setup as Phase 1 (Mock A 21001 + Mock B 21002 answering in 50 ms,
router on 127.0.0.1:20199, auth ON), plus the new wiring checks. Mocks were
made content-aware in the scratch harness only (capital question → "Paris…",
greeting → "Hello!…", else "ok") so the eval smoke cases are meaningful.

| Scenario | Result |
|---|---|
| A returns 429 instantly → failover to B (5 reqs) | **5/5 OK, 52.4–55.0 ms** |
| Failover switch cost | **~1–2 ms overhead** vs direct B |
| A slow (2,000 ms) → hedge at 150 ms, B wins (5 reqs) | **5/5 OK, 203.8–205.9 ms** |
| A quota hint 0 → A skipped entirely, B serves (5 reqs) | **5/5 OK, 52.4–52.8 ms** |
| `/metrics` after the failover run (auth required; no key → 401) | `codvian_router_requests_total{provider="prov_mock_b",outcome="ok"} 5`, `codvian_router_failovers_total{from_provider="prov_mock_a",to_provider="prov_mock_b"} 5`, `codvian_router_in_flight 0` |
| Stale degradation: fresh request, then both mocks killed | Same prompt → **200 with `X-Codvian-Stale: true`, age ~519 ms**; fresh prompt → **502**; fresh requests carry no stale header |
| `eval --model bench-model` (smoke cases vs live mocks) | **3/3 passed (100%)**, exit 0 |
| `validate` subcommand | Valid config → exit 0; invalid listen address → exit 1 with the field named |
| `--listen 192.168.1.5:20199` | Refused, exit 1, names `--listen`; `--listen 0.0.0.0:20198` serves + prints the all-interfaces warning |
| `tray` subcommand | Child serves healthz; `kill -9` on the child → auto-restarted, healthz OK again; SIGTERM on the supervisor → exit 0, child down |

Ledger verified against the SQLite file after the stale run: 2 successes
record prompt 10 / completion 5 / total 15 with `split_known=1`, cost $0;
the 2 post-kill requests each record 2 failed attempts (A then B) with
0/0/0, `split_known=0`, cost $0 — the stale response itself is served from
cache and adds no ledger row, which is honest: no provider was billed.

## Live benchmark (real binary, Python mock upstreams, 2026-11-17, re-run after hardening)

Setup: Mock A (port 21001) + Mock B (port 21002), both serving `bench-model`;
B answers in 50ms. Router on 127.0.0.1:20199, auth ON. Config built with the
real Vault (Argon2id) sealing the mock keys; gateway key issued by the
router's own config code.

| Scenario | Result |
|---|---|
| Direct to Mock B (no router, 50ms upstream delay) | 51.2 ms (baseline floor, previous run) |
| A returns 429 instantly → failover to B (5 reqs) | **5/5 OK, 52.4–55.8 ms** |
| Failover switch cost | **~1–2 ms overhead** vs direct B |
| A slow (2,000 ms) → hedge at 150 ms, B wins (5 reqs) | **5/5 OK, 203.9–204.4 ms** (150 ms hedge + 51 ms B) |
| A quota hint 0 → A skipped entirely, B serves (5 reqs) | **5/5 OK, 52.2–53.5 ms** (quota wiring proven end-to-end) |
| Streaming with 429 failover | SSE chunks `bench ` + `ok` + `stop` + `[DONE]` — works (previous run) |
| No gateway key | 401 as expected |
| Unknown model | Clean JSON error, no crash |
| Startup to healthz-ready | **73 ms** (this run, 50 ms poll granularity; previous run 12 ms) |
| Idle memory (RSS) | **~32 MB** (this run; previous run ~10.6 MB — the rise is the Argon2id 19 MiB arena plus runtime; still tens of MB) |
| Binary size | 16.9 MB single binary |

Ledger verified against the SQLite file after the run: a failed A attempt
records prompt 0 / completion 0 / total 0 with `split_known=0`; a successful
B attempt records prompt 10 / completion 5 / total 15 with `split_known=1`
and cost $0 (no per-model prices configured — never invented). A hedged
loser is recorded as a failed attempt with zero tokens; a quota-skipped
provider is never attempted and leaves no row.

Targets (design goals, now measured against): detect failure 2–3 s, switch
0.1–0.3 s, route under 1 s — measured switch after a 429 is ~1–2 ms on
localhost mocks (real-world numbers will depend on upstream latency).

## Known limitations / follow-ups (not blockers)

- Crypto: master secret is still a local 0600 file — Argon2id only slows
  an attacker who has sealed values but not the secret. Rotation is manual:
  `Vault.ReSeal` each sealed value before replacing `master.key` (or
  changing the passphrase); losing `kdf.salt` is equally fatal. No
  passphrase by default and nothing prompts for one yet; a lost passphrase
  is unrecoverable by design.
- Ledger: streaming usage is unknown to the engine (`bridgeStream`
  discards the upstream `ChatResponse`, which can carry usage from a final
  SSE chunk), so successful streams record zero tokens with cost 0 —
  documented at the call site, never invented. Cost is 0 when no
  per-model prices are configured.
- Registry quota hints are wired into the engine (live read on every
  request) and now also auto-derived in one direction: the health watcher
  sets hint 0 when a key probe fails with a quota-type error (429 /
  `QuotaError`). Hints are never auto-raised and never invented from a
  successful probe; a quota reset is not detected until the next probe
  succeeds and the operator (or a future reset signal) restores the hint.
- Semantic cache is a deterministic hashed bag-of-words similarity cache,
  NOT a neural embedding model: paraphrases with different words will
  generally miss. It IS now wired into the request path (items 55–58):
  proactive exact-then-semantic serving behind `Policy.ServeCache`,
  honest `X-Codvian-Cache` headers, no provider billing on hits. Startup
  prints the honest description ("token-overlap similarity … not neural
  embeddings").
- Scoring cost/quality signals are unknown (0) until per-model prices are
  configured and a quality signal exists, so ModeCheap/ModeQuality
  currently rank mostly on the other signals; cost and quality components
  score equally for every candidate and cannot distort ordering.
- Metrics attribution is approximate under concurrency: the server reads
  the engine's most recent RouteTrace, so a request may occasionally be
  attributed to a neighbour's trace. The ledger (not metrics) is the
  billing record. A streaming response served stale carries no
  `X-Codvian-Stale` header (staleness is known only after the stream
  ends); it is logged server-side instead.
- Packaging: the Docker image was NOT built or run in this environment
  (no Docker in the VM) and no release exists yet, so the npm wrapper and
  `install.sh` happy paths are untested (error paths only). The Dockerfile
  binds loopback by default; use `--listen 0.0.0.0` or
  `network_mode: host` deliberately, on a trusted network only.
- `go.mod` was concurrently edited by parallel agents mid-build; it has converged
  (`github.com/raunakiitp/codvian-router`, `go 1.22`, modernc.org/sqlite v1.34.0, golang.org/x/crypto v0.24.0).
- Catalog wiring (2026-11-17): the Bedrock native wire transport is now implemented and proven live (see items 52–54 and the Bedrock integration gate above) — the client speaks OpenAI wire, Anthropic Messages wire, Gemini generateContent wire, Cohere Chat API v2 wire, and Bedrock Converse wire. The Anthropic wire IS proven live (see the Anthropic integration gate above): the mock spoke real Anthropic Messages and the router answered with correct tool calls + finish reason. The Gemini wire is likewise proven live (see the Gemini integration gate above). The Cohere wire is likewise proven live (see the Cohere integration gate above).
- NO-FAKE-DATA (2026-11-17, auto-router slice): this slice adds no sample/fake data — the auto-router decision view shows only live decisions (typed-prompt classify + last real `auto/*` request) with honest empty states, and the shipped utterances are labelled config defaults, never presented as measured results. BUT the pre-existing dashboard still ships hardcoded sample data (Overview KPIs/traffic chart, provider/model catalogs, request logs, usage spend bars in `web/index.html`) presented as if real. Removing or labelling it is out of this slice's scope — it would have conflicted with the concurrent verification-badges work on the same file — and remains open: any slice touching those sections must replace them with real endpoints or honest empty states.
- Catalog wiring: DeepSeek's chat path has NO `/v1` per the catalog note, but the client always appends `/v1` — per-endpoint path support is still open. The catalog note itself conflicts with DeepSeek's own docs (which use base `https://api.deepseek.com/v1`); verify against DeepSeek docs before changing either side.
- Catalog wiring: exchange auth (Vertex service-account JSON, OpenRouter JWT, Azure Entra) is refused loudly, not implemented — Bedrock SigV4 is the exception, implemented in this gate (pure-Go signer, client signs each request). ToS-grey providers are refused as routing targets. Dashboard surfacing of per-provider verification status is now done (items 61–62: GET /api/providers + live dashboard badges, no sample providers).
- Anthropic wire (2026-11-17): the Bedrock native transport is now built (see items 52–54 and the Bedrock integration gate). Exchange auth for Vertex / OpenRouter JWT / Azure Entra still refused loudly. Tool-call deltas are NOT replayed across a mid-stream resume — only the text prefix is continued (documented in the router); the returned response still carries the full reassembled tool calls. A stale stream replay carries text only: the exact-match cache stores content, not tool calls, so a stale stream cannot reconstruct the original tool calls. DeepSeek `/v1` path question still open (catalog note says no `/v1`, DeepSeek's own docs use base `https://api.deepseek.com/v1` — verify before changing either side).
- Catalog wiring: `ResolveAuthForBaseURL` scans all 423 catalog entries per `ClientFor` call (no cache). Fine at current rates (startup + 60 s health probes); revisit if `ClientFor` gets hot.

## Single-flight coalescing gate (2026-11-17, Go 1.27.4, measured by this run)

- `go build ./...` — OK; `go vet ./...` — OK; `gofmt -l cmd internal web` — clean
- `go test -count=1 ./...` — **all 19 packages ok, 456 PASS events, 0 fail** (was 433 by the same counting method; +23 new: 6 coalescing package, 8 router engine, 2 ledger, 1 metrics, 3 server endpoint, 2 degrade forwarding, plus 1 subtest)
- `go test -count=1 -race` on touched packages (coalescing, router, server, degrade, ledger, metrics, cmd/codvian-router) — **clean**
- Dashboard JS syntax: `node --check` on the extracted inline script — clean; "Playground" absent from the page (0 occurrences)
- Live smoke (real binary, isolated HOME, Python mock on 21061 with a 300 ms upstream delay so concurrent requests overlap, router on 20199, 2026-11-17; scripts kept at `/tmp/coal-smoke/` — ephemeral, not in the repo): **42/42 checks passed**
  - **C1 10 concurrent identical non-streaming requests** → mock recorded exactly **1 upstream call**, all 10 got 200 with "coal-mock says hi"
  - **C2 2 concurrent different prompts** → 2 upstream calls (1 each), both answered
  - **C3 2 concurrent identical streaming requests** (fresh prompt) → **2 upstream stream calls** — streams are NOT coalesced; both SSE streams delivered both chunks + `[DONE]`
  - **C4 5 concurrent identical requests, upstream 500** → exactly **1 upstream call**, all 5 got an honest 502
  - **C5 `GET /dashboard/coalescing`** → `{"requests":17,"flights":4,"shared_calls":13,"upstream_avoided":13,"quota_saved_calls":13,"in_flight":0}` — exactly the measured counts (9 shared from C1 + 4 from C4; streams never entered the group)
  - **C6 /metrics** → `codvian_router_coalesced_total 13`; without a key → **401**
  - **C7 ledger (SQLite)** → 19 rows: 6 billed + 13 shared; shared rows 9 ok + 4 failed, all zero tokens and zero cost; billed tokens 45 (3 successful non-stream calls × 15; the failed leader records 0, the 2 streams record unknown/0) — usage accounting stays honest, no double-count
  - **C8 `GET /dashboard`** → 200, Coalescing nav + `/dashboard/coalescing` fetch + "No coalesced request yet" empty-state text present, "Playground" absent
- Design notes from this slice: the first build put the group in `internal/router`, which broke the build — `server` importing `router` creates an import cycle because `router_test` imports `server`. The group now lives in the new leaf package `internal/coalescing` (same reason `routetrace` exists). One smoke check initially failed on the author's own wrong expectation (billed_tokens 60 vs measured 45 — the failed C4 leader legitimately records 0 tokens); the expectation was corrected, no product code changed. One `wait` bug in the first smoke run (bare `wait` also waited on the servers) was fixed in the script.
- the sector baseline comparison (this slice): the sector baseline advertises request deduplication as a feature, but ships no published measurement of it; Codvian Router's coalescing is now measured live (10→1 upstream calls, 13 avoided calls across the smoke run, ledger-proven zero double-billing) with the exclusion rules (streaming, tool-call turns) enforced by test. Where Codvian Router is still behind: no cross-instance coalescing (single process only — the sector-typical distributed setup can dedupe across nodes) and no token-level quota-saved accounting (quota saved is counted in calls, not tokens, because per-model prices are not configured — never invented).
- Note: three transient failures during the gate were concurrent-edit races with parallel agents in the shared worktree (a `router` test-build failure, a `config` test failure, and a `main.go` rebuild failing mid-write with `undefined: buildAutoRouter` while another agent landed autorouter wiring) — each passed on immediate re-run with no change to this slice. No fake data remains in the Providers section or any provider-driven render; the remaining "Demo data" sections (Models, Logs, Usage, KPIs, router keys) are labelled, not hidden — their live feeds are separate slices.

- Live smoke (real binary, isolated HOME, Python mock on :18080 standing in for api.openai.com via a namespaced /etc/hosts bind-mount, router on 20199, script `~/workspace/.build-tmp/smoke-131-132.sh` — scratch, not in the repo): **22/22 checks passed**
  - **C1** chat with a system prompt → mock recorded `prompt_cache_key: codvian-810eeb5e…` (HMAC, `codvian-` prefix); response `usage.cached_tokens` 800 of 1000 prompt
  - **C2** different user turn, same system prompt → the SAME auto key (user messages excluded)
  - **C3** client-supplied key forwarded verbatim (`my-own-key`); **C4** explicit `null` preserved as JSON null, never replaced
  - **C5** `/dashboard/logs` shows `cached_tokens: 800` per row and contains no `prompt_cache_key` / `codvian-` string anywhere
  - **C6** `/dashboard/overview` cache block: 4 requests with cached tokens, 3200 cached of 4000 prompt, top combo gpt-4o (4 requests, 3200 cached); **C8** `/dashboard/usage` summary agrees (cached 3200)

- Real bug found and fixed by this gate (not by unit tests): the async ledger batch writer (`commitBatch`) computed `cachedTokens` via `normalizeUsage` but its INSERT never listed the column, so the first live smoke recorded 0 cached tokens in the ledger while the client-facing response correctly showed 800. The INSERT now carries the column; `TestAsyncWriterCarriesCachedTokens` is the regression test. The sync `RecordUsage` path was never affected — which is why the unit tests passed while the live binary lost the numbers.
- Smoke-script lessons (script bugs, not product bugs): the mock first failed to bind :80 (no CAP_NET_BIND_SERVICE in the user namespace) → moved to :18080 with the base URL `http://api.openai.com:18080` (host check uses the hostname only); the router's provider client followed the cell's egress proxy to api.openai.com instead of /etc/hosts → proxy env vars unset inside the namespace; C4's first form (identical messages to C3) was correctly served from the router's exact-match cache without an upstream call → its user turn was made distinct.
- Concurrent-edit notes (other slices, not this one): while this slice ran, parallel agents landed the DB import/wipe routes (`runDB` in main.go), the 400-model-not-found taxonomy (`internal/provider/taxonomy.go`, which briefly redeclared this slice's `EndpointChatCompletions` constant — renamed to `CacheKeyEndpointChatCompletions` here), and cooldown-mode changes. The full suite failed once mid-flight on their in-progress files (feedback package + 4 server tests: cooldown contract, feedback weights, guard-table count) and passed on re-run after they landed; the README.v2.md this slice first created was replaced by a fuller version from another agent, and the cache-affinity section was re-added to it. Nothing was pushed to GitHub.
- Benchmark: NOT re-run in this gate — no hot-path code changed, so the published numbers stand.

- Live smoke (real binary, isolated HOME per case, two Python mocks on :18081 dead / :18082 fallback, router on 20199, script `~/workspace/.build-tmp/smoke-133-134.sh` — scratch, not in the repo): **43/43 checks passed**
  - **A** empty stream (`data: [DONE]` only) → 200 with the fallback's content, no error frame, dead 1 hit / fallback 1 hit, trace `[dead empty_stream preread empty, fallback success]`
  - **B** primary 500 before first byte → 200 with fallback content, dead 2 / fallback 2 total, trace `[dead error, fallback success]`
  - **C** one content chunk then a malformed SSE line → 200, "Hello " delivered exactly once, honest `"upstream provider error"` frame, no `[DONE]`, no fallback content, dead 3 / fallback 2 total (fallback never hit), trace `[dead stream_error preread held]`
  - **D** `/dashboard` 200 with the preread labels ("failed over before commit", "preread:", "never replayed"), no Playground; `init --key-file` 0600 with the raw key hidden from stdout
- Real bugs found by this gate (not by unit tests): (1) `chatStreamDrainChunks` returned on Done before `runStream` published the route trace — `LastTrace()` (and `GET /dashboard/trace`) could read the previous request's trace or nil after a successful stream; the drain now waits for channel close (the trace is set in a defer before close), with `TestChatCompletionsStreamingPrereadGate` pinning it. (2) Two concurrent-slice bugs fixed in passing and flagged to the coordinator: `recordAttempt`'s KeyedLedger branch shadowed its `ok` parameter with the type-assertion result (every keyed ledger row recorded ok=true), and `NewEngine` pinned the adaptive limiter at 100/100/100 so its cap could never shrink (contract: initial 20 / floor 2 / ceiling 100). A later concurrent edit replaced the pinned config with `adaptive.Config{}` — identical behaviour via `withDefaults()` (20/2/100), verified by the concurrency tests passing on the current tree.
- Flakes recorded honestly, not hidden: (1) `TestChatCompletionsSameRequestFailoverDeadModel/dead-hang` failed once in a verbose full run (`dead mock hits = 0, want 1`, under heavy concurrent CPU load) and passed 5/5 on immediate isolated re-run. (2) `TestRunDoctorFreshInitPasses` failed once in a verbose full run (the doctor found port 20128 already in use — another agent's server was listening there at that moment — so the dashboard check passed instead of warning) and passed 3/3 on re-run with nothing on the port. Neither test is in a package this slice touches.
- Smoke-script lessons (script bugs, not product bugs): the first run's cases B and C never reached the dead mock — the live binary's "balanced" strategy re-scores providers by observed reliability, so after case A's failure the dead provider sank below the fallback (even across a restart, the ledger remembers). The script now uses a fresh HOME per case so each case independently proves its behaviour; trace assertions check outcomes + preread rather than provider IDs (the CLI assigns `prov_<hash>` IDs, not the `--name`).
- Concurrent-edit notes (other slices, not this one): while this slice ran, parallel agents landed the `internal/cache` package, a `relay.go` signature refactor, a `guardrails.go` struct change, token-counting (`tokencount`), caller-key ledger, warm pools, MCP selector, guardrail refusal codes, per-key budgets and SSE batching slices — the build broke three times mid-flight on their in-progress files (guardrails struct literals, missing `internal/cache` import, relay call-site arity) and passed on re-run after they landed; `NewEngine` was rewritten from a stale copy once, reverting this slice's limiter fix (re-applied, then superseded by their `Config{}` default, same behaviour). The README status lines were advanced by another slice's gate to "1,134 test events pass, 0 fail, across 30 packages" while this gate ran; this slice added items 133–134 to that line's included-slices list rather than overwriting their newer count with its older 956. Nothing was pushed to GitHub.
- Benchmark: NOT re-run in this gate — no hot-path code changed (the drain-to-close wait is in-memory bookkeeping after the terminal event), so the published numbers stand.

Tree rows recorded before this slice do not exist (no backfill). A stream's terminal re-record moves its tree row to the end of its siblings' creation order (INSERT OR REPLACE changes rowid) — creation order within one second is otherwise insertion order.

## Async-guardrail cut-before-commit gate (items 231–232, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- Root cause (real race/order bug, not a load-only flake): `Engine.ChatStreamWithChainStrategy` returned on its entry `ctx.Err()` check without publishing any route trace, so a stream cut by an async BLOCK verdict landing before the first attempt left `LastTrace()` nil (fresh engine) or stale (reused engine — the previous request's trace). Quiet machine: the verdict wins the race every time (the 5/5 "trace is nil" failures); loaded machine: the engine wins it and the cut lands on the attempt (1-attempt trace — why the sprint 11 gate passed).
- Fix: one site in `internal/router/router.go` — the early `ctx.Err()` return now publishes an attempt-less trace (endpoint + compression, no contract, no attempts) before returning the same error, mirroring the contract/group rejections in the same function. Scope note: the fix sits outside the slice's stated `internal/server` file list because only the engine can publish a route trace; no caller behaviour changes (same error, same callers: chat completions, relay, Responses streams).
- Regression test `TestStreamCutBeforeEngineEntryPublishesTrace` (new, `internal/server/async_guardrail_test.go`): seeds a successful stream, then calls `ChatStreamChunks` on a context pre-cancelled with the `AsyncBlockError` cause — deterministic, no timing, no HTTP. Sensitivity proven: FAILS without the fix (stale 1-attempt success trace survives), passes 10/10 with it. `TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit` also gained a `t.Logf` branch line (0- vs 1-attempt) for the repeated-run record; no sleeps added anywhere.
- Repeated-run evidence (logs `$HOME/.tmp/cut-50x-fixed-isolation2.log`, `$HOME/.tmp/cut-50x-fixed-underload.log`): `TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit` **50/50 in isolation** (all 50 took the 1-attempt branch under this machine's scheduling) and **50/50 under full-suite load** (concurrent with 1 full-suite run + an 8-pass full-suite loop). Unfixed baseline: 5/5 passed on the loaded machine; the first unfixed 50× attempt timed out at the 10m `go test` timeout after ~20 passing iterations (each iteration costs ~30s in `slowSrv.Close()` — see honest limits).
- Whole async-guardrail file 3× (log `$HOME/.tmp/async-file-3x.log`): **7/7 tests × 3 runs = 21 PASS, 0 fail** (`TestAsyncGuardrailCutsStream`, `TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit`, `TestAsyncGuardrailWithholdsNonStreaming`, `TestAsyncGuardrailAllowPassesThrough`, `TestSyncExternalGuardrailBlocksPreCall`, `TestGuardrailsPostPreservesAsyncEntries`, `TestStreamCutBeforeEngineEntryPublishesTrace`).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on router + server; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `go test -race` clean on router (30.8s) + server (102.2s), 0 data races (log `$HOME/.tmp/race-gate-231-232.log`); full suite `go test -count=1 ./...` **39/39 packages ok, 0 fail** (log `$HOME/.tmp/full-gate-231-232.log`).
- Dashboard: no new surface — the fix changes no user-visible guardrail behaviour; `GET /dashboard/trace` now shows the attempt-less trace for a pre-entry cut instead of a stale previous trace (verified by the regression test's stale-seed assertion, no dashboard code touched).
- Honest limits: (1) The 50× runs never exercised the 0-attempt branch end-to-end (all 100 took the 1-attempt branch — the verdict never won the race to engine entry under this machine's scheduling); the 0-attempt branch is covered only by the deterministic regression test, which is the point of it. (2) Each `TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit` iteration costs ~30s under repetition: the mock's bounded 30s hold fires once the chunk was written before the cut landed (the mock never reads the POST body, so the server can't notice the client-side close — a test-harness quirk, not a product behaviour; a quiet single run completes in 0.30s, proving the cut closes the connection promptly when it lands first). The 50× runs needed `-timeout 30m` (the default 10m timeout killed the first unfixed attempt at ~20 iterations). (3) The full-suite loop run alongside the under-load 50× showed the known pre-existing `TestTrainingPairs` ordering flake in `internal/feedback` (passes 2–3 of 8; passes 3/3 in isolation — same signature item 230 recorded; untouched here, not mine). (4) Adjacent, deliberately NOT fixed: the `len(chain) == 0` early return in `ChatStreamWithChainStrategy` also publishes no trace (empty-chain config error, unrelated to the guardrail cut) — a follow-up could publish the same attempt-less trace there. (5) No live smoke: no user-visible behaviour changed, so no new live check was warranted; the existing async-guardrail live coverage (items 185–186) stands.
- Concurrent-edit notes (other slices, not this one): while this slice ran, the E1 limiter slice (233–234) and the Responses-branch slice (237–238) landed uncommitted hunks in `internal/router/router.go`, `CHECKLIST.md`, `README.md`/`README.v2.md` and `web/index.html`; this slice's commit stages ONLY its own hunks (hunk-selective `git add`), and the service/VM restarted once mid-run (killing the first 50× isolation run at 21/50, 0 fail — re-run detached to completion).
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit for this slice (see `git log`).

## Test flakes + trace hole gate (items 251–252, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- Root cause (a — flake, not a production race): `TestTrainingPairs` recorded its three signals by ranging over a `map[string]Signal`, so dec-1/dec-2 were recorded in random order, while `TrainingPairs` returns outcome (time) order — pairs[0]/pairs[1] flipped whenever the map order did. Reproduced failing in isolation on a 30× run (pair 0 = chat/discard, TS .574, pair 1 = coding/accept, TS .665 — the later-recorded signal carried the earlier timestamp). A second, quieter nondeterminism sat underneath: signals landing in the same millisecond had no defined order (`ORDER BY e.ts ASC` alone).
- Fix (a): two halves — the test records in a fixed slice order (dec-1 accept, dec-2 discard, dec-3 accept), and production `TrainingPairs` (`internal/feedback/feedback.go`) now orders `ORDER BY e.ts ASC, e.id ASC` (AUTOINCREMENT insertion id breaks same-millisecond ties, making "oldest first" total — the order the learned model's 70/30 time split relies on). No assertion weakened, no test skipped, no behaviour change for distinct timestamps.
- Root cause (b — real trace hole, same shape as 231–232): the `len(chain) == 0` early return in `ChatStreamWithChainStrategy` returned "empty fallback chain" without publishing any trace, so `LastTrace()` stayed nil (fresh engine) or stale (reused engine). The 231–232 slice flagged this hole and deliberately left it open.
- Fix (b): one hunk in `internal/router/router.go` — the early return now publishes the attempt-less trace (endpoint + compression, no attempts, no contract) before returning the same error, mirroring the group rejection just above it and the ctx-cancel return just below it. Same error, same callers.
- Regression test `TestStreamEmptyChainPublishesTrace` (new, `internal/server/async_guardrail_test.go`, next to the 231–232 test): seeds a successful stream, then calls `ChatStreamChunks` with a model no chain serves — deterministic, no timing, no HTTP. Sensitivity proven: FAILS without the fix (stale 1-attempt success trace survives — verified by stashing only the router hunk), passes 10/10 with it. The existing `TestStreamEmptyChain` (`internal/router/router_test.go`, error-only) is untouched.
- Repeated-run evidence (logs `$HOME/.tmp/trainingpairs-50x-isolation.log`, `$HOME/.tmp/trainingpairs-50x-underload.log`): `TestTrainingPairs` **50/50 in isolation** + **50/50 under full-suite load** (concurrent with a full-suite run). Unfixed baseline: failed inside a 30× isolation run.
- Whole-file / multi-test runs (logs `$HOME/.tmp/emptychain-10x.log`, `$HOME/.tmp/trace-tests-3x.log`): new regression test **10/10**; the three trace tests (`TestStreamEmptyChainPublishesTrace`, `TestStreamCutBeforeEngineEntryPublishesTrace`, `TestAsyncGuardrailCutBeforeCommit`) **3× green**.
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on feedback + router + server; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `go test -race` clean on feedback (13.3s, full package) + router (35.1s, skipping the 243–244 probe tests) + the server trace tests (3.4s), **0 data races in every race run** (logs `$HOME/.tmp/race-feedback-251-252.log`, `$HOME/.tmp/race-router-251-252-skip.log`, `$HOME/.tmp/race-trace-tests-251-252.log`); full suite `go test -count=1 -v ./...` **2,134 test events pass (1,682 top-level + 452 subtests) with 1 fail** (log `$HOME/.tmp/full-gate-251-252-final.log`) — the 1 fail is `TestDashboardCatalogAudit`, the in-flight 253–254 catalog-triage slice's stale expected counts; an earlier full run on this tree with this slice's changes was **39/39 packages ok, 0 fail** (log `$HOME/.tmp/full-gate-251-252.log`).
- Dashboard: no new surface — the fix changes no user-visible behaviour; `GET /dashboard/trace` now shows the attempt-less trace for an empty-chain stream refusal instead of a stale previous trace (verified by the regression test's stale-seed assertion, no dashboard code touched).
- Honest limits: (1) The full suite never went fully green in a single run during this slice's gate: the tree is shared by 5+ concurrent slices, and the final verbose run is 38/39 with the single failure owned by the 253–254 slice (`TestDashboardCatalogAudit` — catalog now 455 providers vs 423 expected in the test); the clean 39/39 run predates that slice's edits. (2) Concurrent slices broke the tree transiently four times during the gate (autorouter `tierModels` field/method collision; `probe_243_test.go` missing `time` import; `responses.go` referencing not-yet-landed helpers; `failover_243_test.go` referencing nonexistent `routetrace.Attempt`) — each gate step re-ran after the owning slice repaired it; none of the breakage was in this slice's files. (3) Full-package `go test -race` on router and server could not go fully green while the 243–244 probe tests (intentionally failing until that slice lands its fixes) and the 253–254 catalog test fail; the race verdicts rest on the `-skip` runs plus the targeted trace-test race run — 0 data races in every race run, and no failure in any of them touches this slice's code. (4) `internal/feedback/eval.go` has two more `ORDER BY e.ts ASC` queries (evaluation pairs + ts list) with the same same-millisecond tie nondeterminism — out of scope here (no failing test implicates them), flagged for a follow-up, not fixed in this slice. (5) The regression test's first version asserted a non-empty Endpoint and failed 10/10 on it — the assertion was wrong, not the fix: `traceEndpointFor` returns "" for plain chat completions (set only for /v1/messages); the assertion was dropped, the stale-seed check is the invariant.
- Concurrent-edit notes (other slices, not this one): while this slice ran, the 243–244 slice landed failover hunks in `internal/router/router.go` (pin escape, `pinFirst`, delivered-tool-call suppression, `auto/code` alias) plus `probe_243_test.go` / `failover_243_test.go`, the 245–246 slice edited `internal/guardrails/guardrails.go` + `internal/server/guard.go`, the 247–248 slice edited `internal/autorouter/autorouter.go` + added `code.go`, and the 253–254 slice edited `internal/catalog/` — this slice's commit stages ONLY its own hunks (its whole files except `internal/router/router.go`, where exactly one hunk is staged via a filtered patch).

## Items 255–256 gate (2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- Built: engine stream-lifetime cap (`internal/router/streamlifetime.go`: `DefaultStreamMaxLifetime` 30m, `Config.StreamMaxLifetime`, `ErrStreamLifetimeExceeded`, kill counter; `runStream` runs under a `WithCancelCause` lifetime ctx — hedge, bridge, forwarders and the 429 sleep all bound; expiry counts the kill, records a failed ledger row with unknown usage, never fails over, surfaces the typed terminal error); preread buffer and Bedrock incremental stream bounded by the same ctx (proven by tests, not assumed); verbatim `/v1/messages` relay cap (`internal/server/relay.go`: `forwardVerbatim` timer cancels the upstream call for streaming relays, terminal Anthropic error event in both flusher and non-flusher paths; `handleMessagesStream` names the cap on the drained error); `GET /dashboard/stream-lifetime` feed + guard-table entry (Tier 3 MANAGEMENT, auth-exempt) + `handleEngineError` 504 mapping + committed-stream SSE lifetime detail; `reasoning_effort` on `provider.ChatRequest` (validated at the server, 400 naming the levels; forwarded on the OpenAI wire only — see limits); `Attempt.Effort` + `groupChainFor` stamping + trace `Effort` (stamped in the router funnels, no builder-signature change); `config.ParseGroupMember` (`provider` / `provider@effort`, last-`@` split, unknown effort rejected) + groups validation dedupes on the (provider, effort) pair; `routing.stream_max_lifetime_ms` in config + SCHEMA.md; `scoring.BestEffortMove` (5% tolerance — effort move beats a switch inside it, loses outside it, strict improvement with no switch candidate, no churn) + `Candidate.Effort` + hook carrying effort through; `degrade.StaleEngine` forwards both reporter methods; dashboard: Live Routing stream-lifetime line (`loadStreamLifetime` on the refresh cycle), effort chip on group members, `effort: <level>` on trace attempts; README.md + README.v2.md in the same slice; `scripts/smoke-stream-lifetime.py`.
- Real bug found by the gate and fixed in-slice: the route `GET /dashboard/stream-lifetime` was registered without a guard-table entry, so `New` panicked (`route has no guard-tier classification`) — the table entry was missing from the pre-compaction implementation; added the entry plus the pinned counts in `guard_test.go` (87 routes, MANAGEMENT 64). The same full-server run also surfaced two failures in OTHER slices' in-flight code, untouched here (see below).
- Tests (22 new test funcs, all pass): router (11) `TestStreamLifetimeCapKillsHeldStream`, `TestStreamLifetimeCapTerminatesUpstream`, `TestStreamLifetimeCapBoundsPreread`, `TestStreamLifetimeCapDisabled`, `TestStreamLifetimeCapDefault`, `TestStreamLifetimeCapDoesNotFireOnFastStream`, `TestStreamLifetimeCapNoFailover`, `TestGroupSameModelTwoEfforts`, `TestGroupSameModelTwoEffortsStream`, `TestGroupEffortOverridesClientEffort`, `TestGroupNoEffortPassesClientEffort`; scoring (6) `TestBestEffortMovePrefersEffortOverModelSwitch`, `TestBestEffortMoveLosesWhenClearlyWorse`, `TestBestEffortMoveStrictImprovementWithNoSwitch`, `TestBestEffortMoveNoSameModelEffort`, `TestRankKeysSignalsByEffort`, `TestHookKeepsEffortAttempts`; config (3) `TestParseGroupMember`, `TestConfigRoutingGroupsEffortValidation`, `TestConfigStreamMaxLifetimeMs`; provider (4) `TestChatReasoningEffortForwarded`, `TestChatNoReasoningEffortOmitted`, `TestValidReasoningEffort`, `TestBedrockStreamAbortsOnLifetimeCancel`; server (5) `TestServerStreamLifetimeFeed`, `TestServerStreamLifetimeSSECap`, `TestServerValidateReasoningEffort`, `TestServerGroupsFeedEffort`, `TestMessagesVerbatimRelayStreamLifetimeCap`. Two test-harness bugs found while writing the router tests and fixed in the tests (not the product): the stall mock's chunk counter counted a post-cap chunk the bridge had refused (now counts only bridge-accepted chunks), and the mock's `returned` flag raced the channel close (now polled with a deadline). A first `TestBedrockStreamAbortsOnLifetimeCancel` assertion was wrong in the opposite direction — the Bedrock reader propagates the cancel *cause*, so the error names the lifetime expiry rather than wrapping `context.Canceled`; the assertion now checks the cause text (and one edit initially landed on the pre-existing `TestBedrockStreamNeverEndingDeliversChunks` assertion instead of the new test — reverted, diff verified additions-only).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-255/build.log`); `go vet` clean on the touched packages (router, server, config, scoring, provider, degrade, routetrace; log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-255/vet.log`); `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files (one fix: `internal/provider/client.go` struct alignment after the `ReasoningEffort` field landed); `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; full suite `go test -count=1 -v ./...` (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-255/full-verbose.log`): **2,239 test events pass (1,784 top-level + 455 subtests), 2 fail, 38/39 packages ok** — both failures are other slices' in-flight code, untouched here: `TestDashboardCatalogAudit` (catalog 265–266 slice: pinned 455 vs live 497) and `TestIntentChatUnmappedTierRefused` (259–260 slice: its own new `intent_test.go`, untracked); `go test -race` clean on router (45.1 s) + config + scoring + provider + degrade + routetrace and on the targeted server subset (all 5 new server tests + guard-table tests, 3.3 s) — 0 data races (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-255/race-touched.log`, `$HOME/.tmp/gate-255/race-server-targeted.log`).
- Live smoke `scripts/smoke-stream-lifetime.py` (real binary + 2 Python mocks, isolated HOME): **14/14 green** — feed reports the configured 2 s cap, enabled, 0 killed; a held stream (one chunk, then silence) returns in ~2 s with the chunk, then `{"error":{"message":"router: stream lifetime exceeded (cap 2s)","type":"server_error","deployment":"stall"}}`, then `[DONE]`, and the feed's `killed_total` is 1; a group chat to `efforts` fails over inside the group (low 429s, high serves) and the upstream saw `reasoning_effort` low then high; the trace records both efforts; the groups feed lists the group as two entries (same provider, shared live counters — attempts 2, successes 1, rate 0.5 — as documented); a bogus `reasoning_effort` is a 400 naming the field; the dashboard serves the stream-lifetime line and the effort loader.
- Honest limitations: (1) the server per-request timeout (default 120 s) still binds HTTP streams first by default; the cap is the engine-level bound, binding for non-HTTP streams and whenever the operator configures a shorter cap or raises the server timeout. (2) `reasoning_effort` is forwarded only on the OpenAI wire; other wires ignore it (the trace records the effort asked for, so the gap is visible). (3) No per-effort signal source exists yet: the strategy hook ranks same-pair different-effort attempts by the pair's signals (tie → config order); `BestEffortMove` is the primitive for callers that have per-effort signals. (4) The verbatim relay cap binds only when the operator raises the server timeout or configures a shorter cap (the 120 s `http.Client` timeout binds first by default). (5) The 259–260 slice's README bullet still says the resolved effort is "not yet carried onto the engine attempt" — that clause is now stale (this slice is the follow-up it names); the bullet belongs to the 259–260 slice, so it is flagged here rather than edited.
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit by this slice (hunk-selective staging — shared files carry other slices' concurrent hunks).
