## Failover cluster gate (items 243–244, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- Two real bugs found by probes before the fix (sensitivity-proven): (a) the quota-hint sort demoted the pinned deployment — `TestPinLeadsDespiteQuotaHint` FAILED before `pinFirst` (engine served "bee" from B, never tried pinned A); (b) a resume re-delivered a delivered tool call — the drain's skip logic let a re-emitted call through once text skipping finished. (c), (d), (e) verified already-holding with regression tests only (same-group retry in `runStream`; single application via the `tried` map; ctx unwind stops upstream reads). (f), (g), (h) were new code.
- New tests (21 funcs, all pass): router (9) `TestPinLeadsDespiteQuotaHint`, `TestPinEscapesOnRetryableFailure`, `TestPinEscapeCountedOnce`, `TestResumeDoesNotReplayDeliveredToolCall`, `TestResumeWithoutDeliveredIDsDeliversEverything`, `TestPreOutputBreakIsSameGroupRetry`, `TestFallbackConfigNotReapplied`, `TestContextFallbackChainAppliedOnce`, `TestEarlyCloseResetsUpstream`; server (10) `TestStripDanglingToolCalls`, `TestResponsesContinuationStripsDanglingToolCall`, `TestResponsesSessionTokenAdmission`, `TestResponsesSessionTokenCapOffCountsWithoutRefusing`, `TestPreBodyAdmissionRefusesOverBudgetKey`, `TestPreBodyAdmissionRefusesOverTrafficKey`, `TestStreamErrorNamesDeployment`, `TestStreamErrorWithoutTraceNamesNothing`, `TestStreamErrorNamesDeploymentByDisplayName`, `TestStreamErrorUnknownDeploymentKeepsItsID`; degrade (2) `TestStaleEngineForwardsFailover243Stats`, `TestStaleEngineFailover243StatsZeroWithoutInner`.
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on router + server + responses + provider + degrade; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `node --check` on the extracted dashboard inline script clean; full `go test -count=1` on touched packages — router ok (25.9s), responses ok, provider ok, degrade ok, server ok (46.3s final run); `go test -race` clean on router (25.6s) + responses + provider + degrade and on the server subset covering all 10 new tests (2.2s), 0 data races.
- Live smoke (real binary + 2 Python mocks): `scripts/smoke-failover-243.py` **20/20 checks passed** — pinned continuation escapes flaky 429 to live in one request (1 failed attempt + 1 live hit; feed `pin_escapes: 1, last_pin: flaky, last_escaped_to: live`); delivered call `call_smoke` never reaches the upstream on continuation (stripped_tool_calls 2, then 3 after a second continuation proves the stored transcript keeps it); continuation feed entry `session_tokens: 36`; stream of 10 deltas dying mid-stream ends with `{"error":{"message":"upstream provider error","type":"server_error","deployment":"flaky"}}` after the deltas (`stream_errors_named: 1`); dashboard serves the Failover card + Session tokens column, no Playground; RPM=1 key admits one request then answers 429 (not 400) on a malformed body with exactly one engine hit (`early_refusals: 1`).

## Sprint-13 follow-ups gate (items 263–264, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- Gate ran in a scratch worktree (`$HOME/.tmp/verify-263`, HEAD `20d1108` + this slice's patch only), NOT the main worktree: the main worktree is unbuildable mid-flight — the 255–256 slice registered `GET /dashboard/stream-lifetime` without a guard-tier classification, so `TestAsyncGuardrailCutsStream` panics at server startup there — and the 259–260 commit `b802208` landed a broken `server.go` (a stray top-level `if dec.IntentTier` block at line 3323) and was reset to `20d1108` while this slice ran (later re-committed fixed as `7a4dd77`). The patch was verified complete before the gate: every scratch file matches the worktree except for foreign hunks, and no scratch diff line carries this slice's markers. Before staging, the same patch was re-applied cleanly to HEAD `7a4dd77` and re-verified file-by-file (no foreign lines in the applied diff, all of this slice's changes present); the committed snapshot is re-verified in a fresh worktree after the commit (see below).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on config + server + feedback + guardrails + cache + cmd; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files in the scratch worktree (in the main worktree `config_test.go` is flagged only for the foreign 259–260 hunk, not mine); `go test -count=1` — config ok (0.297s), feedback ok (7.988s), guardrails ok (0.286s), cache ok (0.488s), cmd/codvian-router ok (1.857s), server ok (86.623s); `go test -race` clean on config + feedback + guardrails + cache + cmd and on the server subset covering the new tests (2.560s), 0 data races.
- Live smoke (real binary built from the scratch worktree, one Python mock, `scripts/smoke-session-cap-263.py`): **14/14 checks passed** — cap 10: startup log names it (`responses session token cap: 10 tokens per session`); root create admitted (200, `resp_` id), feed `session_token_cap: 10`, root `session_tokens: 11`; a 300-word continuation is refused with 429 `session_token_limit` ("session has used 11 tokens and this turn needs about 320 more, over the 10-token session cap") and the mock is hit ZERO extra times (hits stay 1); feed `session_admission_refused: 1`; dashboard serves the cap-rendering code, no Playground. Cap unset (restart, `responses` block removed): startup log says counting only; the SAME 300-word continuation is admitted (200), mock hits +1; feed `session_token_cap: 0`, continuation `session_tokens: 334` > root `11` — counting still happens.
- (b) `guardrails.scan` before/after (minima, `BenchmarkScanCleanPrompt` / `BenchmarkScanPromptWithDigits` / `BenchmarkRedactLogLine`): clean prompt 4,097 → 2,652 ns/op (−35%), digits 4,561 → 2,753 (−40%), `RedactLogLine` 4,563 → 1,902 (−58% vs first-run before; −32% vs the clean-HEAD re-run minimum of 2,808). After-profile: no `strings.Contains` left in the top; `needlePresence` 52% cum, `foldLower` 16%, `scanDigitShapes` 12%. The win is the single-pass needle prefilter (first-byte dispatch → presence bitset; digit-shape gate precomputed at init); `foldLower` stays — on-the-fly ASCII-only folding would be unsound for non-ASCII fold-equivalents (`ſ`→`s`, Kelvin `K`→`k`).
- (c) `getScoped`: NO cheap safe win — the ≤0-dot skip was tried and reverted (before/after minima overlap: 12,180 vs 12,155 ns/op shared-vocab, 2,039 vs 2,365 disjoint); divisions are not on the critical path (max-scan loop bound by per-slot checks/loads, divide latency hides behind out-of-order execution). Both new benchmarks are kept as the measurement harness.
- (d) eval tiebreak: `TestEvaluateSameMillisecondOrder` passes (insertion order + 25× byte-identical results) — but the test does NOT discriminate today: with the tiebreak temporarily reverted it still passes, because SQLite's sorter preserves insertion order in practice. The tiebreak is still the correct fix (SQLite tie order is unspecified; it is observable at the `evalRowCap` = 100000 boundary). `CalibratedNudge` is a pure sum (order-independent) and `Evaluate` sorts `times` in Go, so verdicts are order-independent except at the cap.
- Post-commit verification (fresh worktree `$HOME/.tmp/verify-263-final` at `32e7b5c`, 2026-11-18 ~04:54 UTC): `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on the touched packages; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; targeted tests pass (`TestConfigResponsesSessionTokenCap`, `TestResponsesSessionTokenAdmission`, `TestResponsesSessionTokenCapOffCountsWithoutRefusing`, `TestEvaluateSameMillisecondOrder`, `TestNeedlePrefilterMatchesContains`); live smoke re-run from the committed tree — `scripts/smoke-session-cap-263.py` **14/14** (same numbers as the gate run: root `session_tokens: 11`, refusal "used 11 … about 320 more, over the 10-token session cap", continuation `session_tokens: 334` with cap unset).
- Honest limits: (1) server-package tests and the live smoke ran in the scratch worktree (see above) because the main worktree is broken mid-flight by another slice; the committed snapshot was re-verified in a fresh worktree after the commit (see below). (2) Benchmark minima were taken under a heavily shared VM (load 2.78–6.89, ±2× run-to-run noise) — the direction and rough size of the (b) win are solid, the exact percentages are not. (3) The dashboard cap text is rendered by JS from the feed at runtime, so the smoke asserts the served page carries the rendering code; the runtime rendering itself is proven by the feed values (C3/C5). (4) `knownConfigFields` was already stale for `models_dev` etc. before this slice (pre-existing, not fixed here). (5) Patch discipline note: an earlier draft of the patch-filter (block mode on CHECKLIST) swallowed the 266 slice's adjacent row-266 Built mark — caught by the file-by-file verification before staging, filter fixed to line+section mode for CHECKLIST, leak removed.
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit by this slice (hunk-selective staging — shared files carry other slices' concurrent hunks: 255–256 stream-lifetime, 257–258 region failover, 259–260 intent tiers, 265–266 feedback outcomes, 266 catalog triage round 2).

## Multi-region failover order gate (items 257–258, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on router + provider + config + routetrace + server + degrade + cmd/codvian-router; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files (one false alarm while running it: a `.py` smoke script passed to gofmt, not a formatting failure); `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; targeted tests pass (router region tests incl. the 2 new `LastRouting.RegionStep` assertions, provider region tests, config validation tests, server guardrails-feed + decision-header tests); full suite `go test -count=1 -v ./...` (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-257-258/full-verbose.log`): **2,261 test events pass, 1 fail, 2 skip, 38/39 packages ok** — the single failure is foreign, untouched here: `TestDashboardCatalogAudit` (catalog 265–266 slice: pinned 455 providers vs live 497; fails identically without `-race`, so it is a stale-count failure, not a race); `go test -race` clean on router (33.8 s) + provider (13.3 s) + config + routetrace + degrade and on cmd/codvian-router (3.9 s) and on the targeted server subset (1.7 s) — 0 data races; the full server package under `-race` fails only on the same foreign `TestDashboardCatalogAudit`.
- Live smoke (real binary + 4 Python mocks, `scripts/smoke-region-failover-257.py`): **22/22 checks passed** (3 consecutive runs after the conditional rewrite below, exercising both branches) — R1 probe learns the hook's primary (A, us-east-1) with all live, killing it makes B (same model m, eu-west-1) serve 200 `from-eu`, trace `[A error us-east-1, B success eu-west-1 failover_step=cross-region]`, header `provider=b; model=m; strategy=balanced; reason=fallback-after-1; region_step=cross-region`, feed `cross_region=1`; R2 (A+B dead) C serves m2 200 `from-equiv`, header `region_step=equivalent-model`, serving trace attempt C `failover_step=equivalent-model`, feed `equivalent_model=1`, D never hit; R3 (A+B+C dead) D serves m3 200 `from-local`, header `region_step=self-hosted`, serving trace attempt D `failover_step=self-hosted`, feed `self_hosted=1`; R4 dashboard serves the "Failover — region-aware order" card + live counts, no Playground, JS names the region step in the trace render. The step-naming assertions (R2c–e, R3b–d) are conditional on a failover actually happening in that request: the balanced hook ranks by live signals, so after R1/R2 record failures for A/B/C it can promote the never-failed C (R2) or D (R3) to primary, and the target then serves first with no step to name — legitimate (the region order governs failover after the primary, not the pick), so the smoke asserts the first-target form instead (header carries no `region_step`, trace is exactly the single success, feed count unchanged). The first committed snapshot failed this in a fresh-worktree re-run (19/22: R3's 3 step assertions, D primary) — that failure is what the conditional rewrite fixes, not a product bug.
- Three real bugs found and fixed by the smoke (all three were green in unit tests — the unit tests did not cover the live paths): (1) self-hosted detection counted EVERY loopback mock as self-hosted (loopback base URL in the rule), so B tiered last instead of cross-region — rule narrowed to positive identification only (catalog-local or Ollama wire); a loopback OpenAI-compatible endpoint may be a local proxy to a hosted provider. (2) The self-hosted last resort was never in the chain at all (chainFor appended only same-model + equivalents), so R3 502'd — chainFor now appends every model offered by a self-hosted provider. (3) The decision header named no step for equivalent-model/self-hosted winners — `recordDecision` was passed the REQUESTED model, so the step was computed same-model-vs-same-model — the serving model is now threaded through (`res.att.Model` / `streamAtt.Model`) and two `LastRouting.RegionStep` assertions were added to the equivalent-model and self-hosted unit tests. The smoke also caught its own cache bug (identical prompts made R2/R3 cache-hit R1's response) — every scenario now sends a distinct prompt — and R1's primary flakiness (the balanced hook ranks by live probe signals, so the primary varies run to run) — R1 now probes the live primary first, then kills it.
- Honest limits: (1) the full suite's one failure is the foreign `TestDashboardCatalogAudit` above — this slice does not own the catalog counts. (2) Region tiering does not apply to strategy-tree-ordered chains or explicit contract/group orders; the primary pick is chosen by scoring, not region (if scoring picks a self-hosted target first, it serves first — the smoke's R3 trace shows the hook ranking the R2 winner C first in R3's chain, and the tiering keeps it primary; in two of three post-rewrite smoke runs the hook promoted C (R2) or D (R3) to primary, so those runs prove the first-target form and the exact tier order rests on the unit tests' deterministic trace assertions). (3) The 180–220 ms figure is Parasail's measured penalty (research-log entry 232), not a measurement of this router. (4) The smoke's R1 cross-region assertions skip with an explicit note if the hook ever picks a non-same-model primary (allowed by design); R2 still proves the tier order in that case.
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit by this slice (hunk-selective staging — shared files carry other slices' concurrent hunks).

## Supply-chain release hardening gate (items 261–262, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: `docs/RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md` (the six LiteLLM v1.107.0 rules from research-log entry 233 + the repo-visibility rule + a release-day procedure: pre-push → tag → verify-before-announce → independent check → announce, plus the fail-closed verification rules and the "if something goes wrong" playbook); `codvian-router release verify <tag>` — engine `internal/release/verify.go` (expected GoReleaser layout, SBOM shape, Sigstore bundle offline structural check, cosign/gh orchestration via an injected ToolVerifier) + front end `cmd/codvian-router/release.go` (GitHub download with a testing base-URL override, `--dir` local mode, `--key` keypair mode); flags registered globally in `parseArgs`/`options` (the provider add-catalog precedent — the global parser rejects subcommand flags, found live when the first smoke run failed with `unknown flag "--dir"`); README.md + README.v2.md Release-binary rows updated. No new dashboard surface (releases are not served by the gateway); no tag or release created; nothing pushed (bulk-push rule); no other repo touched.
- Real-layout proof (the claim every check rests on): GoReleaser v2.20.2 (downloaded, checksum-verified; commit 1bf59683… matches the pinned SHA in docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md) + syft 1.57.2 (checksum-verified) ran `release --snapshot --skip=docker,sign` in a scratch clone — it proved `checksums.txt` lists archives AND SBOMs (8 entries: 4 archives + 4 SBOMs), SBOMs are SPDX-2.3 with SPDXID SPDXRef-DOCUMENT, and snapshot output carries no `.sigstore.json` (unsigned by design). The engine reads that real dist/ correctly (`TestVerifyReleaseDirRealSnapshotLayout`, env-gated on CODVIAN_ROUTER_SNAPSHOT_DIST). The full 6-platform snapshot build FAILED at the Windows build step — see gaps.
- New tests (33 funcs): engine (22 — tampered archive, tampered SBOM, missing SBOM/bundle, unsigned snapshot, lookalike-identity bundle (the 2026 supply-chain lookalike scenario), bundle digest mismatch, bad SBOM JSON, missing-cosign fail-closed, cosign rejection, image check, attestation without gh + RequireAttestation, SBOM shapes, bundle identity unit tests, real-snapshot layout, exec argv contract via stub cosign/gh, real-cosign integration) + cmd (11 — arg parsing, global-flag registration + opts seeding, dispatch, local happy/tampered/unsigned, remote happy/tampered-download/missing-release/cosign-rejection via httptest). Sensitivity: the tampered-archive, tampered-SBOM, lookalike-identity, and missing-bundle tests fail without their checks (each asserts a named failure, not just a non-zero exit).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-261-262-build.log`); `go vet` clean on internal/release + cmd/codvian-router (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-261-262-vet.log`); `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `go test -count=1 -v` 71 PASS events / 0 FAIL / 1 SKIP (the real-cosign integration test, skipped by design — see gaps) across both touched packages (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-261-262-tests.log`); `go test -race` clean on both packages (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-261-262-race.log`); `goreleaser check` green in a scratch clone with a remote (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-261-262-goreleaser-check.log`).
- Live binary smoke (real binary, not just tests): `release verify v0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-20d1108 --dir <real snapshot dist/>` fails loudly and correctly — checksums 8/8 match, 4 linux/darwin archives + SBOMs ok, 2 Windows archives missing, 5 bundles missing, signature checks fail closed (cosign absent from that shell), attestations SKIP with the local-mode note, exit 1; remote mode against a local HTTP server serving the same dist/ fails loudly on the missing `checksums.txt.sigstore.json` (HTTP 404), exit 1.
- Gaps found (honest, filed here and in docs/RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md): (1) **Windows cross-compile broken at HEAD** — `internal/tray/supervisor.go` uses `syscall.Kill`/`Setpgid` and `internal/provider/tfo.go` uses a Unix-only socket option, with no Windows build guard (committed in sprint 8); a real `goreleaser release` fails at the Windows build step, so no release can be cut until this is fixed — release-blocking, other slices' code, not fixed here. (2) Keyless/Sigstore live path unproven until the first tag (no OIDC identity outside CI). (3) Real-cosign sign/verify end-to-end unproven in this VM: cosign v3.1.4's `sign-blob` needs a signing config for offline use (`--tlog-upload` deprecated and rejected) and `timestamp.sigstore.dev` times out from this VM; the exact cosign/gh invocations are proven by stub tests instead. (4) Provenance-*change* comparison stays a manual checklist step. (5) `goreleaser check` fails in the push-rule worktree itself (no remote by design) — green only where a remote exists (CI / scratch clone). (6) Pre-existing, found while wiring the command: the global arg parser rejects subcommand-specific flags, so `catalog audit --out-dir` and `eval-feedback --feedback-db` are both broken today; this slice registered its own flags globally, fixing those two is a follow-up for their owners. (7) `docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md`'s Files table does not yet list the new command (this slice's file list was the checklist doc + READMEs + code); a one-line follow-up.

## CLI arg-parser bug fix gate (items 271–272, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: the 261–262 finding fixed — the global arg parser (`parseArgs` in `cmd/codvian-router/main.go`) now registers all nine subcommand flags the help documents (`--out-dir`, `--web-json`, `--models-dev-url`, `--skip-reachability`, `--concurrency`, `--probe-timeout`, `--triage-summary` for `catalog audit`; `--feedback-db`, `--cutoff` for `eval-feedback`), in both `--flag value` and `--flag=value` form, with the old handlers' validation bounds (non-empty out-dir/models-dev-url, concurrency ≥ 1, probe-timeout ≥ 1 s, RFC3339 cutoff); `runCatalogAudit` and `runEvalFeedback` read the flags from `options` instead of parsing their own args (the provider add-catalog precedent). ## Windows cross-compile fix gate (items 267–268, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: platform guards for the two Unix-only call sites that blocked every Windows release build — `internal/tray/supervisor_unix.go` + `supervisor_windows.go` (own process group via `Setpgid` on Unix, `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP` on Windows; `Stop` is SIGTERM→SIGKILL to the group on Unix, CTRL_BREAK_EVENT to the group then `Process.Kill` on Windows — a real graceful stop, since the Go runtime delivers the break as SIGINT and `serve` shuts down on SIGINT; if the break cannot be delivered at all — no console — Stop escalates to `Process.Kill` immediately instead of waiting out a grace period for a signal that never arrives) and `internal/provider/tfo.go` + per-OS `tfoSetSocketOption` (Windows: `SetsockoptInt(syscall.Handle(fd), IPPROTO_TCP, TCP_FASTOPEN=15)`; Linux 30, macOS 0x105, other platforms honestly error). The supervisor tests are platform-split the same way (`supervisor_platform_unix_test.go` / `supervisor_platform_windows_test.go`: `sleep`/`false`/`sh` + `syscall.Kill(pid, 0)` vs `cmd.exe`/`timeout`/`exit 1` + `OpenProcess`/`GetExitCodeProcess`). `golang.org/x/sys` v0.22.0 promoted from indirect to direct (the stdlib `syscall` package has no `GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent`). Side effect fixed: `GOOS=freebsd go build ./internal/provider/` now compiles — `tfo.go` used to reference `tfoLevel`/`tfoOption`, which only the linux/darwin/windows files defined. Dashboard: no change — the existing TCP Fast Open card already renders the per-platform probe (platform, reason, dial attempts/refusals) from `GET /dashboard/concurrency`, so the Windows probe flows through it with no new UI. README.md + README.v2.md updated in the same slice (Release-binary row, "What's new" bullet, direct-dependency count 2→4 — the old "two" was already stale: it omitted tiktoken-go) and docs/INSTALL.md's Windows row + internal/tray/README.md (build-tag rule, restart policy, Windows limitation).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `GOOS=windows go build ./...` OK; `GOOS=windows go vet ./internal/tray/ ./internal/provider/` clean (compiles the Windows test files too); `go vet` clean on both touched packages (linux); `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `go test -count=1 -v ./internal/tray/ ./internal/provider/` **202 top-level + 99 subtests pass, 0 fail** (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-267-268-tests.log`); `go test -count=1 -race` clean on both touched packages (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-267-268-race.log` — tray 3.1 s, provider 12.8 s, 0 data races); full suite `go test -count=1 ./...` **39/39 packages ok, 0 fail, exit 0** (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-267-268-fullsuite.log` — run in the shared worktree while other slices' in-flight edits were present; the tree was green at that point).
- Snapshot build (the release-blocking proof): GoReleaser v2.20.2 (GitCommit 1bf59683…, the pinned SHA from docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md) + syft 1.57.2 ran `goreleaser release --snapshot --clean --skip=publish,sign,docker` in the worktree — **succeeded after 4m20s, exit 0, all six archives** including `codvian-router_0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-none_windows_amd64.zip` (22,494,720-byte `codvian-router.exe` + `README.md`) and `..._windows_arm64.zip` (21,058,048-byte exe + `README.md`); `checksums.txt` lists all 12 files (6 archives + 6 SBOMs). `goreleaser check` fails in the no-remote worktree by design (same known limitation as 261–262: "no remote configured to list refs from"); the snapshot build itself does not need a remote.
- `release verify` against the real `dist/` (real binary `$HOME/.tmp/codvian-router-267`, log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-267-268-release-verify.log`): `release verify v0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-none --dir dist/ --skip-image --skip-attestation` — **checksums 12/12 SHA-256 match, all six archive checks ok (present + listed + hash-verified, including both Windows zips), all six SBOM shape checks ok (SPDX-JSON)**; the 7 bundle + 7 signature checks FAIL as expected for an unsigned snapshot (no `.sigstore.json` by design — a snapshot is unsigned) and the 6 attestation checks SKIP in local mode, so the overall verdict is FAILED, exit 1 — correctly, per release.go's own rule. The expected-layout code needed NO change: the real `dist/` names match `ExpectedArchives("0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-none")` exactly, verified against the real layout, never assumed.
- Honest limits: (1) **The Windows binary has not been executed** — Wine is not installed in this VM (`which wine wine64` empty, no wine packages), so `help` / `--version` under Wine was impossible; execution is unproven until CI runs the Windows binary. (2) Windows has no group SIGKILL — `Process.Kill` terminates only the direct child, so a grandchild that ignores the break event can outlive the gateway (documented in supervisor_windows.go + internal/tray/README.md). (3) The Windows supervisor tests have not been executed here either (same no-Wine reason); they compile under `GOOS=windows go vet` and run in CI. (4) Mid-gate the shared tree went red from another slice's in-flight edit (`internal/server` call sites lagging a `applyGuardrails` signature change — `go build ./cmd/codvian-router` failed with "not enough arguments"); that slice landed its fix and the tree is green again — foreign, not touched here. (5) `goreleaser check` in the no-remote worktree fails by design (see above).
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit by this slice (hunk-selective staging — shared files carry other slices' concurrent hunks: 269–270 intent effort, 271–272 arg-parser, 265–266 catalog triage, 263–264 follow-ups, plus in-flight server/guardrails/router edits).

## Items 269–270 gate (2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built (picking up the interrupted previous agent's partial work — kept whole, verified, not redone): `resolveAuto` carries the tier-resolved effort onto the request (`req.ReasoningEffort` — the tier wins over a client-supplied value, the same precedence as a routing-group entry) and returns it; the five server-facing entry points (`Chat`, `ChatStream`, `ChatStreamFrom`, `ChatStreamChunks`, `ChatStreamFromChunks`) stamp it onto the chain with `withResolvedEffort` (attempts with no effort of their own — a group entry's own effort still wins on that entry), so `reqWithModel` sends it to the wire and `stampTraceEffort` records it per attempt. Per-wire forwarding audited and recorded on `provider.ChatRequest.ReasoningEffort`: only the OpenAI wire sends `reasoning_effort`; Anthropic (numeric `thinking.budget_tokens`), Gemini (numeric `thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget`), Cohere (no reasoning control) and Bedrock Converse (no effort field) do not forward it — no invented budget mapping. Dashboard: decision view (preview card + live last-decision card) shows a "Provider received effort" line taken from the latest trace's served attempt for the decision's model ("not served yet" when the trace has none — never invented); the Live Routing trace already rendered per-attempt effort (255–256) and now carries the tier's effort on the serving hop. Intent-tiers card copy fixed (no longer calls the carrying a follow-up) + per-wire forwarding note. README.md + README.v2.md updated in the same slice (stale 259–260 bullet fixed + 269–270 gate numbers). The 277–278 slice's `/v1/messages` relay + `/v1/responses` hunks were not touched.
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on router + autorouter + provider; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; `go test -count=1` — router ok (19.6 s), autorouter ok (0.4 s), provider ok (9.4 s); 8 new test funcs in `internal/router/intent_effort_test.go` all pass (effort reaches the provider, differs by tier, overrides the client value, unpinned passes through, group entry wins, stream path, stamping helper, resolveAuto request carrying); `go test -race` clean on router (41.5 s) + autorouter (2.9 s) + provider (30.0 s, log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-269-270-race-provider.log`), 0 data races; `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script.
- Live smoke (real binary + two Python OpenAI mocks, `scripts/smoke-intent-effort.py`): **20/20 checks passed** — preview resolves intent/fast to model-a at effort low and intent/best to model-b at effort high; mock-a records `reasoning_effort` low for intent/fast and mock-b records high for intent/best (the two differ exactly as the tier definitions say); trace serving attempts carry the same efforts (model-b high, model-a low); tier overrides a client-supplied high on the wire (mock records low); streaming intent/best carries high to the mock; unpinned tier passes client medium through with no trace effort; dashboard serves the "Provider received effort" line, stale copy gone, no Playground.
- Honest limits: (1) `reasoning_effort` is forwarded only on the OpenAI wire — Anthropic, Gemini, Cohere and Bedrock Converse providers never receive the tier's effort (the trace still records the effort the attempt ran at, so the gap stays visible, never silent); no effort→token-budget mapping is invented. (2) The "Provider received effort" line reads the latest trace's served attempt for the decision's model — a preview, or a different request served last, shows "not served yet", never an invented value. (3) The full suite was not re-run for this slice (touched packages fully green incl. race; the coordinator gate covers the tree).

## Sprint-14 follow-ups batch gate (items 277–278, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: (a) Bedrock abort is explicit at the provider client — `ChatStreamChunks` runs under a child context the client cancels the moment the consumer errors or the parent context ends, so the ConverseStream connection drops and billed generation stops upstream (ConverseStream has no abort API — closing the connection IS the abort). (b) Pre-body admission (`preBodyAdmission`: budget-cap + RPM peek, `early_refusals` counted) now runs before the body is read on `POST /v1/messages` and `POST /v1/embeddings` (and `/v1/rerank` via the shared handler), as well as chat + Responses. (c) An unmapped intent tier on `/v1/messages` and `/v1/responses` answers 400 naming the tier (`intent tier "best" is not mapped…`), via `checkIntentTier` before budget/guardrails; mapped tiers flow to the translate path / engine `AutoRoute`. (d) Per-key guardrail scoping is enforced — `CheckScoped` / `CheckExternalSyncScoped` / `StartAsyncScoped` filter to a key's listed guardrails (`nil` = global behaviour); `keyGuardrailScope` reads `config.GatewayKey.Guardrails` (empty = global); request, async, response, relay-translated, Responses and embeddings paths all pass the scope; `GET /dashboard/guardrails` reports `key_scoping` (scoped/total keys, `scoped_evals`, per-key lists) and the dashboard Guardrails view renders it. (e) Vertex AI now routes — new `vertexClient` (wire `vertex`, distinct from Gemini): service-account JSON key → RS256 JWT (RFC 7523, cloud-platform scope, 1 h) exchanged at `token_uri`, token cached until ~1 min before expiry, `Authorization: Bearer <minted token>` per request; `generateContent` / `streamGenerateContent?alt=sse` / ListModels (publisher prefix stripped); `ClientVertexOverride` (`vertex_location`/`vertex_project`) routes that one request only; fails loudly on non-JSON key, wrong type, missing `client_email`/`private_key`, unparseable PEM/PKCS#8-or-PKCS#1 key, rejected exchange, or a base URL without project/location — never a faked static header. Registry: `vertexHeaders()` mirrors `sigv4Headers` (vertex wire only when the exchange kind is `service-account-json` AND the key parses as SA JSON); catalog-linked and base-URL branches both resolve it. Dashboard: Providers view shows a Vertex line (count + exchange description, honest empty state); `GET /dashboard/providers` lists the vertex-wire provider.
- New tests (25 funcs, all pass): provider — 2 Bedrock abort (`TestBedrockAbortOnContextCancelStopsUpstream`, `TestBedrockAbortOnConsumerErrorStopsUpstream`, slow mock streaming one frame per 40 ms until disconnect), 9 Vertex (token mint + route, token cache, stream, ListModels prefix strip, client override, missing-credentials fail-loud ×4, exchange-rejection fail-loud, base-URL-without-project fail-loud, registry AddCatalog); server — 7 admission/intent (`followup_277_test.go`: over-budget 402 + `early_refusals` on Messages and embeddings, RPM 429 on Messages, unmapped-tier 400 + mapped-tier 200 on Messages and Responses), 4 guardrail scoping + dashboard elements (`guardrail_scope_277_test.go`); guardrails — 3 scoping (`CheckScoped`, `CheckExternalSyncScoped`, `StartAsyncOnScoped`). Sensitivity: with the server (b)/(c) hunks stashed, the 5 refusal tests FAIL and the 2 mapped-served controls PASS; with the fix restored, all 7 PASS. The existing Vertex refusal tests still pass unchanged (invalid SA JSON still refused).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on provider + server + guardrails; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; `go test -count=1` — provider ok (9.3 s), guardrails ok (0.3 s), server ok (83.6 s); `go test -race` clean on all three touched packages (server 108.3 s, provider 12.9 s, guardrails 1.3 s, 0 data races); full suite `go test -count=1 -v ./...` **2,301 PASS events (1,842 top-level + 459 subtests), 0 fail, 39/39 packages ok, exit 0** (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-277-278/full-test.log`; the 15 `^FAIL` lines in the log are the release-verify tamper-proof test's own expected-failure output inside a passing package, not failures). The old foreign failure `TestDashboardCatalogAudit` (455 vs 497 pin) now PASSES at this HEAD.
- Live smoke (real binary + 4 Python mocks, `scripts/smoke-followups-277.py`): **30/30 checks green** — Bedrock add (wire `bedrock-converse`); never-ending stream delivers 3 chunks, then 0 frames >0.6 s after the client closes the connection (6 frames total) and the router stays healthy; valid Messages under an RPM=1 key serves 200, garbage body is 429 with mock chat hits 1→1; same on embeddings (429, embed hits 1→1); uncapped controls are 400 on both surfaces; `early_refusals` = 2; Messages + Responses `intent/best` are 400 naming `"best"` with zero mock hits, `intent/fast` serves 200 on both; secret text under the pii-only key serves 200 while the unscoped key answers 403/-32001 and PII under the scoped key is 403; Vertex chat 200 with the minted token on the right project/location path, 3-part JWT assertion, exactly 1 exchange at add (key test) + 1 at first chat and none at the second (cached); dashboard carries the per-key scoping card + Vertex line with no Playground; guardrails feed reports 1 scoped key of 4 with `scoped_evals` ≥ 1; providers feed lists one vertex-wire provider.

## Sprint-15 follow-ups batch, part 1 gate (items 283–284, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: (a) the Vertex minted-token cache is now shared across client instances for the same service-account key — a process-wide cache in `internal/provider/vertex_client.go`, keyed by a SHA-256 fingerprint of the service-account identity (client_email, token_uri, hash of the private-key PEM), never by raw key material and never logged or persisted; each fingerprint holds its own mutex + token + expiry + mint/hit counters, so same-key clients share one exchange while different-key clients mint in parallel, and a failed mint is never cached. `provider.VertexTokenCacheStats()` reports the live totals (mints, hits, distinct keys) since process start. (b) `POST /api/providers` accepts a `models` field: validated (every entry a non-empty string, else 400) and explicitly not stored — the provider's models always come from the key test's `/v1/models` probe — with the 201 response carrying `models_note` saying so ("models field accepted but not stored (N supplied) — models come from the upstream /v1/models probe"). Dashboard: `GET /api/providers` carries `vertex_token_cache` (mints/hits/keys) and the Providers view's Vertex line renders it live ("token cache since start: N mints, M cache hits across K keys"); the add form's flow is the same POST the smoke drives, so no 400 on a `models` field.
- New tests (5 funcs, all pass): provider — `TestVertexTokenCacheSharedAcrossClients` (two clients, same SA JSON, two chats, exactly 1 exchange; stats delta mints 1, hits 1) and `TestVertexTokenCacheSeparatePerKey` (two different SA keys mint twice — sensitivity for the sharing test); server — `TestAddProviderModelsFieldAccepted` (custom add with `models` → 201, models are the probe's [mock-model-a mock-model-b], `models_note` names the probe), `TestAddProviderModelsFieldEmptyEntry400` (empty-string entry → 400), `TestAPIProvidersVertexTokenCache` (feed's `vertex_token_cache` equals the live `VertexTokenCacheStats()` numbers).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on provider + server; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; targeted `go test -count=1 -run 'TestVertex|TestAddProvider|TestAPIProviders'` — provider ok, server ok; `go test -race` clean on provider (13.2 s) and on server (197.4 s, all tests except the foreign `TestDashboardCatalogAudit` — see honest limits), 0 data races.
- Live smoke (real binary + 3 Python mocks, `scripts/smoke-followups-283.py`): **13/13 checks green** — Vertex add 201 wire vertex, key test minted exactly once; first chat 200 "hi there" with the minted token on the right project/location path and still exactly 1 exchange total; second chat 200, still 1 exchange; providers feed `vertex_token_cache` = {mints 1, hits 3, keys 1} (the third hit is the health probe reusing the same token); custom add with `models: ["client-model-x","client-model-y"]` → 201 with models [model-a] and the `models_note`; empty-string models entry → 400; dashboard carries the Vertex line + "token cache" text, no Playground.
- README.md + README.v2.md updated in the same slice (283–284 bullet, with honest limits).
- Honest limits: (1) **Vertex is still proven only against mock token + mock Vertex servers** — no live Google call has been made. (2) **Foreign failure in the tree, not this slice**: `TestDashboardCatalogAudit` FAILS at this HEAD (reachability 753 vs pinned 756, triage record no longer "108 added") because the in-flight 281–282 triage round-4 slice has uncommitted edits to `web/catalog-audit.json` + `internal/catalog/providers_triage.go` + `internal/catalog/verified.go` while `catalog_audit_test.go` still pins the round-3 numbers — none of those files are this slice's; the server race run above excludes only that one test and is otherwise fully green. The full suite was not re-run for this slice (touched packages fully green incl. race; the coordinator gate covers the tree). (3) The shared token cache lives for the process lifetime with no eviction of expired entries' map slots — entries are re-minted on expiry, so the map grows by at most one entry per distinct service-account key ever used. (4) The dashboard add form itself has no `models` input field — the fix is server-side acceptance, verified by the form's exact POST shape in the smoke; an operator client that sends `models` no longer 400s.

## Release-verify CI wiring gate (items 289–290, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: (a) workflow — `.github/workflows/release.yml` gains a `Verify the built release with release verify` step in the `release` job, immediately after the GoReleaser build step and before the image-digest/attestation steps: it builds the verifier from the checked-out tree and runs `release verify "<tag>" --dir dist --skip-image --skip-attestation` against the `dist/` the job just produced (all six archives incl. both Windows zips + SBOMs + Sigstore bundles, cosign installed earlier in the job; the tool fails closed when cosign is missing). Any failure fails the job, skips the attestation steps and blocks the `verify` job. No new `uses:` entry (pinning policy untouched); the `release verify` tool itself was NOT changed (its behaviour is the thing being wired). (b) Dashboard — new `GET /dashboard/release-verify` feed (`internal/server/release_verify.go`, embedded record `web/release-verify.json` + `web/web.go`, route + guard-table entry, MANAGEMENT 64→65) serving the recorded run verbatim, and a Release verification card in the Benchmark view (`loadReleaseVerify`, refresh + view-switch wired) rendering it. The record is the 267–268 snapshot proof re-run by this slice (`docs/release-verify-2026-11-18-snapshot.json`, identical copy in `web/`): tag `v0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-none`, checksums 12/12, archives 6/6 (Windows 2/2), SBOM 6/6, bundles 0/7 + signatures 0/7 failed as expected for an unsigned snapshot, attestations 6 skipped, verdict FAILED exit 1 — shown as a failure with its reason, never rewritten as a pass. (c) Docs + READMEs in the same slice: `docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md` (pipeline steps 3–5 + Limitations), `docs/RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md` (tag+publish bullet), README.md + README.v2.md (Release-binary row clause + What's-new bullet).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on internal/server + web (one transient vet failure mid-gate was a concurrent slice's mid-flight edit in `internal/router/effort_forwarding.go` — `EffortBudgetFor`/`EffortForwarding` not yet landed — re-run clean after that slice landed); `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; "Playground" absent (0 occurrences). `go test -count=1 ./internal/server/` — all pass except the single foreign failure `TestDashboardCatalogAudit` (the working copy's `web/catalog-audit.json` carries another slice's in-flight triage-round-4 counts: reachability 753/21 vs the test's pinned 756/18 + the triage record; neither file is touched by this slice); `go test -race -count=1 -run TestDashboardReleaseVerify|TestRouteTable|TestRegisterPanicsOnUnknownRoute` PASS (3/3, 0 data races); full `go test -race ./internal/server/` — 0 data races, the only failure the same foreign catalog-audit test. New test `TestDashboardReleaseVerify` pins the recorded numbers (tag, FAILED, exit 1, 12/12, 6/6, 2 Windows, 6/6 SBOM, 0/7 bundles, 0/7 signatures, 6 skipped attestations, totals 13/14/6/33, "unproven" Windows execution, 289-290 CI wiring).
- Workflow YAML validated: actionlint/yamllint are NOT installed in this VM, so validation is a strict YAML parse (custom loader rejecting duplicate keys) + a manual step-by-step check — the new step's run block is exactly the dry-run command, it sits after `Build and publish the release` and before `Get published image digest`, and it adds no `uses:` entry (no pin to resolve).
- Local dry-run (the step's exact command, log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-289-290/release-verify.log`, binary `$HOME/.tmp/gate-289-290/codvian-router`, cosign from `$HOME/.tmp/bin`): `release verify v0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-none --dir dist --skip-image --skip-attestation` against the real snapshot `dist/` — exit 1, 13 ok / 14 failed / 6 skipped (checksums 12/12, archives 6/6, SBOM 6/6; 7 bundle + 7 signature checks fail as expected for an unsigned snapshot). Output is byte-identical to the 267–268 proof log (`$HOME/.tmp/gate-267-268-release-verify.log`) except the invoked path (`dist` vs `dist/`). The command works; exit 0 in CI is unproven until the first tag, because bundles only exist on a real signed release.
- Live smoke (real binary with this slice's changes, isolated HOME `$HOME/.tmp/smoke-289-290`, port 20128, log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-289-290/smoke-server.log`): `GET /dashboard/release-verify` returns 200 with the recorded snapshot numbers (tag v0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-none, verdict FAILED, checksums 12/12, archives 6/6 Windows 2/2, bundles 0/7); the served dashboard HTML contains the Release verification card (`benchReleaseCard`/`loadReleaseVerify`, "Release verification" heading).
- Honest limits: (1) The in-job verify step runs AFTER GoReleaser publishes (one step builds + publishes) — it is a tripwire that fails the job, not a pre-publish gate, and it cannot un-publish a bad release. (2) Neither the local dry-run nor the CI step executes any binary — the Windows exe is checked (present, listed, hash-verified) but never run; Windows-binary execution stays unproven until a CI run executes it. (3) CI execution itself is unproven until the first pushed tag — no tag has been pushed; the step's exit-0 path (signed bundles verifying) has never run anywhere. (4) The full-suite `go test ./...` was not re-run for this slice (touched packages fully green incl. race apart from the foreign catalog-audit failure above; the coordinator gate covers the tree).

## Sprint-15 follow-ups batch, part 2 gate (items 285–286, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: (a) the operator-declared effort→budget table — `reasoning.effort_budgets` in the config file (`internal/config/effort_budgets.go`, `ReasoningConfig` + `EffortBudgetTable`, validated at load); (b) per-wire forwarding in the engine (`internal/router/effort_forwarding.go`): the attempt's effective effort resolves through the table to a numeric budget for Anthropic/Gemini, carried as `ChatRequest.ThinkingBudget` — the Anthropic client sends `thinking: {"type": "enabled", "budget_tokens": N}` and the Gemini client sends `generationConfig.thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget: N` (both chat and stream paths, including when no sampling parameter is set, so Gemini builds the generationConfig block for the budget alone); with no table or no entry, no thinking field is sent at all. OpenAI keeps sending the named `reasoning_effort`; Cohere/Bedrock record `not-forwarded:no-effort-control`; Vertex records `not-forwarded:vertex-out-of-scope` (its client is untouched by this slice). (c) The trace: every attempt carries `effort_forwarding` (`forwarded:...` with the wire's own field and value, or `not-forwarded:...` with the reason) into `routetrace.AttemptTrace`, stamped by both trace funnels. (d) Dashboard: Live Routing rows render the label verbatim; the decision view's "Provider received effort" line adds the humanized forwarding; the intent-tiers card copy states the per-wire truth. (e) `main.go` wires `EffortBudgetFor` from the loaded config.
- New tests (14 funcs, all pass): config — `TestEffortBudgetTableLookup` (both wires, normalization, no-entry/nil-table/zero-budget all resolve to nothing), `TestEffortBudgetTableValidate` (unknown level, unnormalized key, "none", zero/negative budgets all fail naming the field), `TestEffortBudgetTableConfigRoundTrip` (loads from `reasoning.effort_budgets`, survives save/load, bad table fails `Config.Validate`); provider — `TestAnthropicThinkingBudgetForwarded` (1024 sent as `thinking.enabled` on chat + stream), `TestAnthropicNoThinkingBudgetSendsNothing`, `TestGeminiThinkingBudgetForwarded` (2048 sent as `thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget` on chat + stream), `TestGeminiNoThinkingBudgetSendsNothing`; router — `TestResolveEffortForwarding` (all 13 wire/table combinations), `TestEffortBudgetReachesAnthropicAttempt` (intent/fast 1024 vs intent/best 16384, budgets differ by tier, trace labels match), `TestEffortBudgetReachesGeminiAttempt` (8192), `TestEffortBudgetAbsentSendsNothing`, `TestEffortBudgetUnmappedEffortSendsNothing`, `TestEffortBudgetNoEffortControlWires` (Cohere + Bedrock), `TestEffortBudgetStreamPath` (stream carries 1024 + the label).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on config + provider + wire + routetrace + router + cmd/codvian-router; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; `go test -count=1` — config ok, provider ok, wire ok, routetrace ok, router ok; `go test -race` clean on config (1.7 s) + provider (27.1 s) + wire (5.4 s) + routetrace (1.0 s) + router (54.1 s) + cmd/codvian-router (9.9 s), 0 data races; full suite `go test -count=1 ./...` (log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-285-286/full.log`): **38/39 packages ok, 1 fail** — the single failure is foreign (see honest limits).
- Live smoke (real binary + 2 Python mocks, `scripts/smoke-intent-effort-budgets.py`): **20/20 checks passed** — providers added as catalog `anthropic`/`gemini` with base_url overrides at the mocks (201, wire_format anthropic/gemini); preview resolves intent/fast to claude-sonnet-4-5 at low, intent/best to gemini-2.0-flash at high; intent/fast chat 200, Anthropic mock records `thinking.budget_tokens` 1024; intent/best chat 200, Gemini mock records `thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget` 8192 (budgets differ by tier); trace serving attempts carry `effort_forwarding` `forwarded:thinking.budget_tokens=1024` / `forwarded:thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget=8192`; both streaming chats carry the same budgets; with the `reasoning` block removed from the config file and the router restarted, the Anthropic mock records NO thinking field (`<absent>`) and the trace says `not-forwarded:no-operator-budget-table`; dashboard carries the new forwarding copy + decision-view forwarding text, no Playground. One smoke bug found and fixed in-slice: the Gemini mock's stream branch tested `endswith(":streamGenerateContent")` but the URL carries `?alt=sse`, so the stream POST fell through to the JSON branch (502) — fixed to a substring test, 20/20 after.
- Post-commit verification (fresh worktree `$HOME/.tmp/verify-285-286` at `48e5867`, 2026-11-18): `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on config + provider + wire + routetrace + router + cmd/codvian-router; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; all 14 new test funcs pass; `TestDashboardCatalogAudit` PASSES here (server ok) — confirming the main-worktree failure was the round-4 slice's uncommitted catalog data, not this slice; `go test -race` clean on config (1.9 s) + provider (15.4 s) + router (28.7 s), 0 data races; live smoke re-run from the committed tree — `scripts/smoke-intent-effort-budgets.py` **20/20** (same numbers as the gate run).
- README.md + README.v2.md updated in the same slice (intent-tiers bullet per-wire sentence + 285–286 gate numbers; 255–256 honest-limits clause fixed — it still said forwarding was OpenAI-only).
- Honest limits: (1) **Foreign failure in the tree, not this slice**: `TestDashboardCatalogAudit` FAILS in the main worktree (catalog Total 570 vs pinned 531, models 9,981 vs 9,978, reachability 853/833 vs pinned 774/756) because the in-flight 281–282 catalog-triage-round-4 slice has uncommitted edits to `internal/catalog/providers_triage.go` + `verified.go` + `providers_triage_round4.go` + `web/catalog-audit.json` while `catalog_audit_test.go` still pins the round-3 numbers — none of those files are this slice's. Proof: the same test PASSES on a clean HEAD worktree (`$HOME/.tmp/verify-285-head`, `go test -run TestDashboardCatalogAudit ./internal/server/` ok). The committed-snapshot re-verification below re-runs it with only this slice's patch applied. (2) Vertex shares Gemini's wire shape but its client is untouched by this slice: a Vertex attempt records `not-forwarded:vertex-out-of-scope` and sends no thinking budget — the budget lookup is keyed on the wire, and Vertex's wire string is distinct, so no budget can leak onto it. (3) The budget resolves from the attempt's *effective* effort (tier > group entry > client value, the same precedence as `reqWithModel`), so a client-supplied `reasoning_effort` on an Anthropic/Gemini wire also maps through the table when an entry exists — that is the table's purpose, but it means the operator's table governs client efforts too, not just tiers. (4) Hedged-stream forwarding is applied in `hedgedStream` for both racers via the same helper, but no dedicated test exercises a hedge race — the stream test covers the non-hedged path only. (5) The Anthropic `thinking` block requires the model to support extended thinking; the gateway sends the operator's declared budget as-is and does not validate it against the model — an upstream 400 for an unsupported model fails over like any other request error. (6) `ThinkingBudget` is set per attempt after request coalescing (like `ReasoningEffort` it is not part of the coalescing key) — two concurrent identical requests differing only in effort already share a flight key today, unchanged by this slice.
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit by this slice (hunk-selective staging — shared files carry other slices' concurrent hunks: 279–280 key-budget hot-path, 281–282 catalog triage round 4, 283–284 Vertex/per-key follow-ups, 289–290 release-verify).

## Ledger write-path gate (items 287–288, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: multi-row INSERT for the async writer's `commitBatch` (`internal/ledger/async.go` — `multiRowInsertChunk = 32`, new `insertChunk` helper; the budget UPDATE stays one aggregated UPDATE per gateway key). Driver finding: modernc.org/sqlite v1.34.0 re-parses its SQL on every Exec (`prepareV2` inside `exec`/`query`, statement finalized after), so no prepared statement is ever reused — fewer statements is the only lever. New tests: `TestAsyncWriterMultiRowChunkBoundaries` (70 rows / 3 chunks / 2 keys, exact budgets $8.00/$1.50) and `BenchmarkCommitBatch256` (256-row batch). ## Key-budget hot-path gate (items 279–280, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: per-request key-budget memo (`internal/ledger/keybudget.go`: `WithKeyBudgetMemo`, `keyBudgetMemo` — only successful reads memoised, a found row or `sql.ErrNoRows`; DB errors never cached; `SetKeyBudget`/`DeleteKeyBudget` through the same request context invalidate that key's entry, so the dashboard budget-update flow — read previous row, write, roll back on failed config persist, all in one request — never sees a stale row) attached once per HTTP request in `withLogging` (the outermost wrapper), plus `CheckKeyBudget` passing the already-read row into `keyBudgetStatusWithBudget` instead of re-reading it inside `KeyBudgetStatus`. New tests: `TestKeyBudgetMemoSharesOneRead` and `TestCheckKeyBudgetUsesMemo` (prove the memo by mutating the DB behind its back) and `BenchmarkGetKeyBudgetMemoHit` (87 ns/op vs 21,665 ns/op uncached miss, ~250×).
- Dashboard: Benchmark section gains the "Key-budget hot-path fix (279–280)" card (`benchKeyBudgetCard`, meta + verdict) and the feed gains `keybudget_remeasurement` (`internal/server/benchmark.go`: `benchKeyBudget` — verdict, clean, method, load_trace, `get_key_budget_before_pct`/`after_pct`, `top_cost_after`); `scripts/merge-bench-keybudget.sh` merges a run the same way `merge-bench-definitive.sh` does, carrying over the definitive + CPU cards verbatim.
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on `internal/ledger` + `internal/server`; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; `go test -count=1 ./internal/ledger/` ok (18.0 s); `go test -count=1 -run 'TestDashboardBenchmark' ./internal/server/` 7/7 pass (incl. new `TestDashboardBenchmarkKeyBudget` against the verbatim merged file); `go test -count=1 -race ./internal/ledger/` ok (55.8 s) and `go test -count=1 -race ./internal/server/` ok (107.5 s), 0 data races.
- Benchmark (Geekflare-style, `scripts/bench-geekflare.sh`, profile via `CODVIAN_CPU_PROFILE`): verdict run attempt 3 (07:09–07:11 UTC, HEAD `ec029d5`, load 1.05 → 0.91, canaries 21,732/21,805 RPS): added latency 0.842 ms pass 1, 0.691 ms pass 2; RPS 1,451 pass 1, 1,432 pass 2 (errors 4,752/33,786 pass 1, 4,394/33,113 pass 2); ready 441/559 ms, idle 41.8 MiB. ## Vertex effort forwarding gate (items 303–304, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

## Release-verify pre-publish gate verdict (items 307–308, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- Verdict: the tripwire is the FINAL design. A true pre-publish gate (build-only → verify → publish the same `dist/`) cannot be built with the pinned OSS GoReleaser v2.20.2. No workflow step changed; no change to the `release verify` tool itself.
- Proof 1 — the split mechanism does not exist in OSS: the pinned binary itself (downloaded v2.20.2 release tarball, GitVersion 2.20.2, GitCommit `1bf59683fabedd7b446d089ec31b1e343b472ce6` — the exact commit pinned in docs/RELEASE-SIGNING.md) has no `publish` command — `goreleaser publish --help` prints the `release` help — and `goreleaser release --snapshot --prepare` fails with `error=unknown flag: --prepare`, exit 1. The official docs agree (`goreleaser.com/cmd/goreleaser_publish`: "This command is only available in GoReleaser Pro"; `goreleaser.com/cmd/goreleaser_release`: `--prepare` "(Pro only)", implying `--skip=publish,announce,after`).
- Proof 2 — a second `release` run cannot publish the verified `dist/` instead: `build` is not in v2.20.2's valid `--skip` list (announce, archive, aur, aur-source, before, changelog, chocolatey, docker, flatpak, homebrew, iru, ko, makeself, mcp, nfpm, nix, notarize, publish, sbom, scoop, sign, snapcraft, srpm, validate, winget — no `build`), and a run without `--clean` refuses an existing `dist/` (`release failed after 0s error=dist is not empty, remove it before running goreleaser or use the --clean flag`, exit 1, log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-307-308/noclean-probe.log`). So the second run wipes and rebuilds. Measured: two consecutive snapshot builds of the same committed tree (clean worktree `$HOME/.tmp/wt-307-308` at HEAD `db5c626`, GoReleaser v2.20.2 + syft 1.57.2, logs `$HOME/.tmp/gate-307-308/snapshot1.log` / `snapshot2.log`) produced DIFFERENT SHA-256 for all 12 archives + SBOMs (hash lists `$HOME/.tmp/gate-307-308/hashes-build1.txt` / `hashes-build2.txt`; build 1 took 2m29s, build 2 51s) — a rebuild's bytes are provably not the verified bytes, and in CI it would also be re-signed with fresh per-run keyless certificates.
- Workarounds evaluated and rejected: (a) draft release (`--draft`) gates only the GitHub blobs — the Docker image is still built, pushed, signed and `:latest`-tagged in the same run before any verify, and a failed verify cannot un-push it, plus undrafting needs the unpinned `gh` CLI and a cleanup path for the pushed image; (b) manual re-publish (`gh release create` + upload + `docker push` + `cosign sign`) re-implements GoReleaser's publish pipe by hand (changelog generation, image signing/SBOM, asset upload) with unpinned tooling and no local proof for the registry half — a missed asset would ship an incomplete release, caught only by the same post-publish verify job. Signing is not the blocker: `sign` and `publish` are independent skips and checksums + signing are the integrity phase preceding the publishing phase, so a `--skip=publish` run in CI would still sign the build-only `dist/` — the blocker is purely that OSS GoReleaser has no publish-the-built-dist step.
- Follow-up, not this slice: a GoReleaser Pro licence would unlock `--prepare`/`publish` — the maintainer's call, not assumed here. No new flag is needed on the `release verify` tool (its existing `--skip-image --skip-attestation` invocation already works against a build-only `dist/`).
- Local dry-run (build-only → verify, clean committed-HEAD worktree `$HOME/.tmp/wt-307-308`, GoReleaser v2.20.2 + syft 1.57.2, cosign from `$HOME/.tmp/bin`): `goreleaser release --snapshot --clean --skip=publish,sign,docker` succeeded (all six archives incl. both Windows zips, `dist/checksums.txt` SHA-256 `f2bef90c6996587dae020f73235838592dfdd85d8c7349fdee31007ffd8e5c66`), then the workflow's exact command `release verify v0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-none --dir dist --skip-image --skip-attestation` (binary built from the same worktree, log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-307-308/release-verify.log`) behaved exactly as the recorded proof: exit 1, 13 ok (lowercase `ok` lines: checksums 12/12, archives 6/6, SBOM 6/6) / 14 failed (7 bundle + 7 signature, missing `.sigstore.json` as expected for an unsigned snapshot) / 6 skipped (attestations) — `FAILED: v0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-none — 14 check(s) failed`. The verify step works on a build-only `dist/`; what is missing is only the OSS publish step for it.
- Gate (run in a separate worktree `$HOME/.tmp/wt-verify` at temp commit `5530b65`, the staged tree = HEAD + this slice only, log `$HOME/.tmp/gate-307-308/gate.log`): `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on `internal/server` + `web`; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; `TestDashboardReleaseVerify` PASS (incl. the new gate-wording assertion); `go test -race -count=1 ./internal/server/` ok, 181.5 s, 0 data races; `node --check` clean on the extracted `loadReleaseVerify` function; "Playground" 0 occurrences in `web/index.html`; workflow YAML parses (Python `yaml.safe_load`, actionlint/yamllint not installed in this VM) with jobs `release` + `verify`, the 13 release steps in the same order/names, and all 9 `uses:` pins byte-identical to the pinned toolchain table; live smoke (real binary from the verified tree, isolated HOME `$HOME/.tmp/gate-307-308/smoke-home`, port 20128): `init` exit 0, `GET /dashboard/release-verify` 200 with tag `v0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-none`, verdict FAILED, `gate` starting "Gate: POST-PUBLISH TRIPWIRE, not a pre-publish gate (items 3…", served dashboard HTML contains "Release verification" (2×) and `d.gate` (4×).
- Honest limits: (1) CI execution itself is unproven until the first tag run — the in-job verify's exit 0 on a real signed release, the keyless signing in CI, and the multi-arch Docker build all still await the first tag. (2) The snapshot `dist/` is unsigned by design, so the bundle/signature failures in the dry-run are expected, not a defect. (3) The rebuild-differs measurement is on snapshot builds (sign + docker skipped, as in 267–268); the cause (SBOM/archive timestamps vs binary non-determinism) was not isolated — only the outcome matters: all 12 hashes differ, so a rebuild is not the verified bits regardless of cause. (4) During this slice's gate the main worktree was mid-flight for concurrent slices (`internal/catalog/verified.go` carried a syntax error from the 301–302 slice, breaking `go build ./...` and `go vet` there) — the gate above ran in the clean separate worktree instead, and the real commit is verified in a fresh worktree after committing, with the result in this slice's final report to the parent agent.
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit by this slice (hunk-selective staging — shared files carry other slices' concurrent hunks: 283–284 Vertex/per-key follow-ups, 285–286 + 303–304 intent effort forwarding incl. Vertex, 299–300 budget memo, 301–302 catalog triage round 5, 309 benchmark history, 310 Vertex token-cache eviction). Staged via HEAD-copy + `hash-object` for the shared files (README.md, README.v2.md, web/index.html) so the staged diff contains zero foreign lines; the staged diff was verified file by file before committing.

## Bedrock abort sensitivity gate (items 305–306, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- New test (1 func): `TestBedrockAbortOnConsumerErrorStopsUpstreamImmediately` (`internal/provider/bedrock_abort_test.go`) — signal-gated 1 ms mock tick, consumer errors on chunk 1, bound ≤2 post-signal frames.
- Sensitivity PROVEN in a separate worktree (~/workspace/.build-tmp/wt-305-306, committed HEAD db5c626): with the 277 hunks reverted (`git show ed41af3^:internal/provider/bedrock_client.go`) the new test FAILS 29/30 runs (mock emits 10–50 frames after the abort: 10, 17, 17 in the first 3-run probe, then 19–50 across a 20-run batch — the deferred body close is ~10–50 ms late); with the fix restored it PASSES 30/30 (0 post-signal frames in 20 runs, 0 in 10 earlier runs incl. one 1, 0 in 5 runs under `-race`). The two original 40 ms-tick tests still pass on the old code (re-verified 5×), so they prove the behaviour but not the fix. The old code's close is late because the transport only drops the connection after Translate unwinds and the deferred body close runs; the explicit cancel() in emit drops it while Translate is still unwinding.
- Tried and rejected: a Content-Length burst candidate (whole stream delivered in one flush, handler blocks) FAILS on the new code too — the mock never notices the disconnect within 2 s. Standalone transport probe (`$HOME/.tmp/probe.go`): Content-Length + cancel + close → server never notices, connection never closed (the fully-read body returns the connection to the idle pool — cancelling a completed request is a no-op); chunked + cancel or close-only → server notices and connection closes in ~100 ms. The burst shape is therefore not an abort scenario (generation already finished; nothing left to stop upstream) and the candidate was deleted, not committed.
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet ./internal/provider/` clean; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's file; `go test -count=10 -run TestBedrockAbort ./internal/provider/` — all 3 abort tests PASS 10/10; `go test -race -count=1 ./internal/provider/` ok (19.7 s), 0 data races. Full suite `go test -count=1 -v ./...` (log `$HOME/.tmp/fulltest-305-306-verbose.log`): **2,354 PASS events (1,895 top-level + 459 subtests), 0 fail, 38/39 packages ok** — the single failure is `TestRouteTableSpawnCapableAreLocalOnly` (internal/server: route table 89 entries, expected 88 — another slice's uncommitted route landed without updating the guard table; it PASSES on the clean committed HEAD in the worktree, so it is not this slice's — this slice touches only `internal/provider/bedrock_abort_test.go`, a `_test.go` file that is not compiled into the binary).
- Live smoke re-run (real binary + 4 Python mocks, `scripts/smoke-followups-277.py`, log `$HOME/.tmp/smoke-305-306.log`): **28/30 green; B1–B4 all PASS** — never-ending Bedrock stream delivers 3 chunks, upstream silent after client close (frames_after_close=0, total_frames=6), router healthy + normal Bedrock chat after abort, /healthz 200. The 2 failures (F2/F6, Vertex token-exchange counts) are pre-existing on the committed tree (same 28/30 reproduced in the clean worktree) and outside this slice. Honest limit: the smoke's B2 bound (0.6 s) proves the live path stops, not the millisecond gap — the millisecond gap is proven only by the Go test.
- No dashboard change in this slice (item 306): the abort gains no visible signal — no stream-abort counter is plumbed through router/server (other slices' packages) — so nothing was added to the page; "Playground" remains absent.
- README.md + README.v2.md updated in the same slice (277–278 bullet honest-limits correction + new 305–306 bullet).
