## Benchmark history + Vertex token-cache eviction gate (items 309–310, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built (309): `GET /dashboard/benchmark/history` (new route + guard-table entry, TierManagement, AuthExempt like the single-run feed) serves every recorded `benchmark-*.json` in the directory named by `CODVIAN_BENCH_HISTORY_DIR` (the repo's `docs/` in practice; unset = honest `recorded=false`). One entry per file, oldest first: file, date, kind (`key-budget` / `definitive` / `ledger` / `cpu` / `limiter` / `run`), clean flag, verdict, passes, idle MB, ready ms. The primary-run rule is shared with the single-run feed via `summarizeBenchFile` (the single-run handler was refactored onto it, behaviour unchanged — the existing benchmark tests still pass byte-for-byte on their fixtures). Merged files carry their recorded verdict/clean (ledger's verdict is its `cpu_profile` verdict, its fix text rides along as `fix`); plain runs get the E1 canary rule computed from their own recorded direct-mock RPS (every pass >= 12,000) and the server-computed per-pass target verdicts — nothing invented. Unreadable/corrupt/pass-less files are listed under `skipped` with the reason, never shown as data. The dashboard Benchmark view gains a History card (table newest first + SVG-only pass-1 latency trend, clean runs solid, polluted runs hollow, 0.5 ms target dashed; same string-built SVG pattern as the provider history charts), loaded by `loadBenchmarkHistory()` on every Benchmark refresh. README.md + README.v2.md updated in the same slice (What's-new bullet + Benchmark-section paragraph).
- What was built (310): the shared Vertex minted-token cache is now bounded — cap 1,024 entries (`vertexTokenCacheMaxEntries`, a var so tests can lower it). Inserting past the cap first sweeps expired entries (`vertexTokenCachePruneExpired`), then drops the evictable slot whose deadline is soonest (a live token's expiry, or a stale token-less entry's grace deadline). Token-less entries (a mint that never completed) are reclaimed once stale — past a 5-minute grace for an in-flight first mint — so a key whose mint always fails cannot hold a slot forever; a first mint inside its grace period is never dropped. Lock order is global-mu then entry-mu everywhere (same as `VertexTokenCacheStats`), and a holder that already has an entry pointer keeps minting into the detached entry after a delete (at most one extra mint, never a lost token). `VertexTokenCacheStats` `keys` is now documented as the live slot count, not a process total.
- Found while testing (310): the first cap test failed on the full package (kept 2 test slots, want 3) — a token-less entry left by the exchange-rejection test occupied a cap slot, because the first eviction design never reaped token-less entries. That is a real hole in the bound (every distinct key whose mint fails would hold a slot forever), so the grace-period reaping above was added instead of weakening the test; the cap test now measures pre-existing token-less leftovers up front and asserts the kept set relative to them. The prune test's counts were likewise relaxed to `pruned >= 1` with an exact keys-delta assertion, so a stale leftover cannot make it flaky.
- New tests (6 funcs): history — `TestDashboardBenchmarkHistoryFixture` (plain run + merged CPU file + corrupt file in `testdata/history/`: kinds, clean flags, recorded vs computed verdicts, skipped listing), `TestDashboardBenchmarkHistoryNoDir` (unset / missing / empty dir all answer `recorded=false`), `TestDashboardBenchmarkHistoryRealDocs` (the repo's actual 36 recorded files: 0 skipped, oldest first, key-budget/definitive/ledger/limiter kinds + recorded verdicts pinned); eviction — `TestVertexTokenCacheEvictsExpiredEntries` (two keys mint once each, first token expired in place, sweep reclaims exactly that slot, live key's token stays cached — a second chat mints nothing new), `TestVertexTokenCacheCapBoundsMap` (cap lowered to 3, five inserts keep only the newest slots, oldest-expiry evicted first, a fresh token-less entry survives past the cap), `TestVertexTokenCachePruneReapsStaleTokenlessEntries` (a token-less entry past the grace is reclaimed, a fresh one is kept).
- Route-table fix (this slice's own debt, found by the gate): the first `-race` run failed `TestRouteTableSpawnCapableAreLocalOnly` (89 entries, expected 88; MANAGEMENT 66 vs 65) — the new history route had landed in the guard table without updating the test's pinned expected list and tier counts. Fixed in `guard_test.go` (expected list + 65 → 66 with the 309–310 attribution in the comment). This also clears the same failure two concurrent slices recorded against this slice in their gate sections (301–302, 305–306).
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on `internal/server` + `internal/provider` + `cmd/codvian-router`; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; verbose `go test -count=1 -v ./internal/server/ ./internal/provider/ ./cmd/codvian-router/` (log `$HOME/.tmp/verbose-touched.log`): **906 PASS events (744 top-level + 162 subtests), 0 fail**, all three packages ok (the `FAIL checksums/bundle/signature` lines in the log are the release-verify tamper-proof test's own expected-failure output inside passing packages, not failures); `go test -race -count=1` on the same three packages (log `$HOME/.tmp/race-touched.log`): server ok 112.1 s, provider ok 13.5 s, cmd ok 3.6 s, 0 data races.
- Live smoke (real binary rebuilt from this tree, isolated config/data `$HOME/.tmp/router-live-config.json` + `$HOME/.tmp/router-live-data/` with the key-budget run as `benchmark.json`, `CODVIAN_BENCH_HISTORY_DIR` = repo `docs/`, port 20199): dashboard 200; `GET /dashboard/benchmark/history` 200 — `recorded=true`, **36 runs, 0 skipped**, kinds run 31 / limiter 1 / cpu 1 / definitive 1 / ledger 1 / key-budget 1, first `benchmark-2026-11-17.json` (plain, clean=false — its canary failed, honestly shown), last `benchmark-2026-11-18-keybudget.json` (key-budget, clean=true, 0.842 ms / 1451.386 RPS pass 1, recorded verdict intact); served dashboard HTML contains the History card (`benchHistoryCard` 2×); single-run feed unchanged (`recorded=true`, 2026-11-18T07:11:19+00:00, 0.842 ms pass 1, key-budget record present).
- Honest limits: (1) No global-budget merged file exists yet — "later ones" is covered because the feed reads every `benchmark-*.json` in the directory, so a future global-budget (or any) merged file appears automatically with no code change; the kind for an unknown future record shape falls back to `run` with the canary rule. (2) Merged files re-present a component run that is also listed separately when recorded on its own (e.g. `cpu.json` and `cpu-after.json`) — the view shows the filename on every row so the duplication is transparent, not hidden, and the note under the table says so. (3) The history directory is env-only (`CODVIAN_BENCH_HISTORY_DIR`); a deployment that never sets it shows the honest empty state. (4) Expired entries are reclaimed only when the map grows past its cap or the sweep runs — there is no background sweeper, so `keys` can briefly include expired-but-unreclaimed slots. (5) Vertex is still proven only against mock token + mock Vertex servers (no live Google call has been made).
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit by this slice (hunk-selective staging — shared files carry other slices' concurrent hunks: 299–300 budget memo, 301–302 catalog triage round 5, 303–304 Vertex effort forwarding, 305–306 Bedrock abort, 307–308 release-verify gate, 291–298 UI track). The staged diff was verified file by file before committing, and the committed snapshot was re-verified by building it in a separate worktree.

## Global-budget hot-path gate (items 299–300, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What was built: per-request global-budget memo (`internal/ledger/budget_memo.go`: `WithBudgetMemo`, `budgetMemo` — only successful reads memoised, a found row or `sql.ErrNoRows`; DB errors never cached; `SetBudget`/`ResetSpent`/`RecordUsage` through the same request context invalidate that key's entry — the memoised row carries `SpentUSD`, unlike the key-budget row, so a spend recorded mid-request must invalidate too) attached once per HTTP request in `withLogging` next to the key-budget memo, plus `GetBudget` split into `getBudgetMemoised` (memo layer) / `getBudgetUncached` (the prepared-statement read). New tests: `TestBudgetMemoSharesOneRead`, `TestCheckBudgetOrErrorUsesMemo` and `TestBudgetMemoDoesNotCacheErrors` (prove the memo by mutating the DB behind its back, including drop-table error → recreate → fresh read) and `BenchmarkGetBudgetMemoHit` / `BenchmarkGetBudgetMemoMissHit` (228/248 ns/op vs 15,012 ns/op uncached miss, ~65×).
- Dashboard: Benchmark section gains the "Global-budget hot-path fix (299–300)" card (`benchGlobalBudgetCard`, meta + verdict) and the feed gains `globalbudget_remeasurement` (`internal/server/benchmark.go`: `benchGlobalBudget` — verdict, clean, method, load_trace, `get_budget_before_pct`/`after_pct`, `top_cost_after`); `scripts/merge-bench-globalbudget.sh` merges a run the same way `merge-bench-keybudget.sh` does, carrying over the definitive + CPU + key-budget cards verbatim.
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on `internal/ledger` + `internal/server`; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's Go files; `bash -n` clean on the merge script; `node --check` clean on the extracted dashboard inline script; full suite `go test -count=1 ./...` EXIT 0, 39 packages ok, 0 fail; verbose recount **2,335 test events pass (1,876 top-level + 459 subtests), 0 fail** (log `$HOME/.tmp/gb299-fulltest-verbose.log`); `go test -count=1 -run 'TestDashboardBenchmarkGlobalBudget|TestDashboardBenchmarkKeyBudget' ./internal/server/` pass (incl. new `TestDashboardBenchmarkGlobalBudget` against the verbatim merged file); `go test -count=1 -race ./internal/ledger/` ok (34.3 s) and `go test -count=1 -race ./internal/server/` ok (106.7 s), 0 data races.
- Benchmark (Geekflare-style, `scripts/bench-geekflare.sh`, profile via `CODVIAN_CPU_PROFILE`): verdict run (07:50:39–07:52:12 UTC, HEAD `db5c626` + this slice's uncommitted changes only, load 1.30 → 1.48, canaries 19,599/20,009 RPS, no `go test`/`go build` running — clean): added latency 0.851 ms pass 1, 0.777 ms pass 2; RPS 1,273 pass 1, 1,174 pass 2 (errors 4,432/29,926 pass 1, 5,019/28,517 pass 2); ready 434.8/565.0 ms, idle 42.1 MiB, peak RSS 78.2/108.8 MiB. Targets still MISSED on both passes. Raw run `docs/benchmark-2026-11-18-globalbudget-run.json`; merged `docs/benchmark-2026-11-18-globalbudget.json`. Same pprof harness: `GetBudget` 8.20% → 7.96% cum (39.31 s samples, directly comparable to the 279–280 profile's 42.95 s), per-request cost flat at ~51 µs (3.15 s / 61,443 requests); new top named cost `GetKeyBudget` 8.27% cum. Full report `docs/cpu-profile-2026-11-18-globalbudget.md`; narrative appended to `docs/BENCHMARK.md`.
- Live dashboard check (real binary built from the worktree, port 55159, merged JSON as the data-dir `benchmark.json`, isolated HOME): `GET /dashboard` 200, contains `benchGlobalBudgetCard` + `benchKeyBudgetCard`, 0× "playground" (540,202 B served); `GET /dashboard/benchmark` serves the after-run (0.851 ms / 1,273.018 RPS pass 1, all `met: false`) with `globalbudget_remeasurement` (`clean: true`, verdict, 8.20 → 7.96, top cost `GetKeyBudget` 8.27%) and the carried-over definitive + CPU + key-budget cards; `GET /v1/models` without key → 401. 15/15 checks pass (one scripted check — the Playground grep — ran against an unexpanded path and passed vacuously; re-verified directly afterwards: 0 matches in the saved dashboard HTML).
- README.md + README.v2.md updated in the same slice (global-budget clause appended after the key-budget clause in both).
- Honest limits: (1) **No end-to-end win — within noise, by design, and none is claimed.** The memo has no second read to save on today's hot paths (the guard reads once per request — verified by reading every call site: `enforcePromptBudget` once per chat/responses/embeddings request, one of `CheckBudgetOrError`/`CheckBudgetForEstimate`, never both; pre-body admission checks only the key budget), and the profile confirms it (per-request cost flat at ~51 µs). The harness pair (0.851/0.777 ms, 1,273/1,174 RPS) sits in the same band as the key-budget run (0.842/0.691 ms, 1,451/1,432 RPS) and the definitive run (0.908/0.711 ms, 1,213/963 RPS). The RPS passes are 12–18% below the key-budget run's; that is neither a win nor a regression — the clean-run band spans 963–1,451 RPS and this run sits inside it. (2) The verdict run waited ~13 minutes for a quiet window (load peaked at 17.47 mid-wait with 8 concurrent `go test` processes from other slices; the run started at load 1.30 with none running). No polluted attempt was needed — the first attempt was clean. (3) HEAD moved during the slice: the verdict run was on HEAD `db5c626` plus ONLY this slice's changes (clean worktree `$HOME/.tmp/wt-gb299`); six commits landed before this slice's commit (`0f10f2e` 303–304 Vertex effort, `24be06b` 307–308 release-verify, `92793b8` 305–306 Bedrock abort, `c5a7bd4` 303–304 verification, `e5987e1` 301–302 catalog triage 5, `e1bf147` 309–310 benchmark history + Vertex eviction). Verified by `git diff db5c626 e1bf147`: `internal/ledger/` is untouched, `internal/server/server.go` changed only in the history wiring (field + route), `internal/router/` only in effort forwarding (Vertex table entry) — none touches the ledger budget read path or the guard, and the memo's nil-effect conclusion is structural (one read per request at both HEADs), so the measured numbers stand for the commit tree; the run was NOT re-measured on `e1bf147` (a second quiet window was not available, and the delta is off the hot path). The main worktree carried another slice's uncommitted, then-unbuildable changes at slice start (benchmark-history wiring in `server.go` referencing a handler that only existed uncommitted in `benchmark.go`, plus effort-forwarding changes) — that is why the run was taken from the worktree. While porting my patch into the worktree I caught that it carried 3 foreign `server.go` lines from the shared worktree (the `benchmarkHistoryDir` field + history route) — removed before any measurement; the worktree diff is exactly this slice's files. (4) Port-back note for the coordinator: this slice's commit lands on `e1bf147` by hunk-selective staging (its own files/hunks only). The main worktree still carries another slice's uncommitted work (`internal/provider/vertex_client.go` comment rework; `CHECKLIST.md` rows 307–308 flipped back to unverified — NOT this slice's, left untouched) — that slice must rebase onto this commit, never commit over it. (5) The memo's staleness bound is one request: a budget set, reset or spend recorded after a request started is visible to the next request, never to the in-flight one — the same decision that request already made on its first read.
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit by this slice (hunk-selective staging — shared files carry other slices' concurrent hunks: benchmark-history, effort forwarding, UI track 291–298).

## npm/installer happy paths gate (items 321–322, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- What broke (found by testing the happy path on this VM): **`codvian-router --version` did not exist** — `--version` exited 2 (`unknown flag`) and `version` exited 2 (`unknown subcommand`); the version only appeared on the serve/init startup lines. Fixed in this slice: `-v` / `--version` / `version` print `codvian-router v0.1.0` and exit 0 (same `Version` constant the startup lines use), listed in `help`. New Go test `TestRunVersion` (3 forms + parseArgs mapping). No other breakage: `install.sh`, the npm wrapper, `init`, `provider add`, and `serve` all worked first time against the fixture.
- Live install-flow evidence (real binary, isolated HOMEs, local release fixture + Python mock upstream on :21332, all on this VM): `install.sh` (PREFIX isolated, `v0.1.0`) — download + SHA-256 verified + installed, exit 0; installed `--version` → `codvian-router v0.1.0`; `init --key-file` wrote config + `crk_…` key; `provider add` (mock) reported 1 model; `serve` on :21331 answered healthz `{"status":"ok"}`, 401 on `/v1/models` without a key, and a relayed `mock hello` chat completion with the mock's usage (5/2/7). Same flow through the npm wrapper (`npm pack` + `npm install -g` from the local tarball, first-run download from the local server, checksum verified, binary cached; second `--version` reused the cache, no re-download). Also proven: piped `install.sh` is refused, and no-arg `install.sh` fails cleanly (GitHub API 404 — no release exists yet, exit 1 with a helpful message).
- Permanent tests: `npm test` **12/12** — the 9 pre-existing verification-path tests plus 3 new happy-path tests in `bin/codvian-router.test.js` (install.sh + real binary end to end; npm wrapper + real binary end to end; pipe-guard refusal). The happy-path tests build the real binary, serve it as a release fixture, and run install → `--version` → `init` → `provider add` (local mock upstream) → `serve` smoke in an isolated HOME. One test bug found by the gate: the install.sh test asserted the checksum confirmation on stderr, but `install.sh` prints progress to stdout (the wrapper uses stderr) — fixed to check combined output.
- Gate (clean separate worktree, HEAD + only this slice's patch — the main worktree was mid-flight for concurrent slices and `go build ./...` failed there on another slice's `internal/router/router.go` breakage, `resolveAutoRequest` call sites missing a `context.Context` argument; not this slice's code, untouched here): `go build ./...` OK, `go vet` clean on `cmd/codvian-router`, `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files, full `go test -count=1 -v ./...` (log `$HOME/.tmp/cr-321-322/full-gate-verbose.log`): **2,365 PASS events (1,898 top-level + 467 subtests), 0 fail, 39/39 packages ok** (the `FAIL` lines in the raw log are expected output text from the release-verify tamper fixtures, not test failures — verified: 39 distinct `ok` package lines, 0 `FAIL` package lines). `go test -count=1 -race ./cmd/codvian-router/`: ok, 0 data races. `npm test` in the same worktree: 12/12.
- Docs (item 322): `docs/INSTALL.md` §4/§5 now carry a "Proven on this VM (2026-11-18)" block each, naming exactly what was proven (local-fixture install + wrapper flows, pipe-guard refusal, no-arg 404) and what was not (real GitHub release download, `npm publish`, cosign signature branch — no cosign on this VM, macOS/Windows execution); README.md + README.v2.md install table rows updated with the same honest split. Dashboard: no new surface — there is no install surface in the dashboard, and the router-keys empty state already points at `codvian-router init` factually; nothing in the dashboard claimed an unproven install path.
- Honest limits: (1) No GitHub release exists, so the real download URL, `npm publish`, and the cosign signature branch on a real release are all still unproven — the fixture proves the installer/wrapper code, not the release pipeline. (2) The happy-path tests need `go` + `tar` (they build the real binary); on a machine without Go they fail with a clear error naming the missing tool. ## Cost-cap model-level prices gate (items 319–320, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- Layer choice (319): the refusal stays at request time — a capped model without a complete model-level price is refused 402 naming the model — not a startup error. The served-model set changes as providers are added live, and refusing to start the whole gateway for one unpriced model would take down every other model; the `cost_cap` feed is the proactive surface instead. Pinned by `TestPluginsCostCapPairPriceOnlyStillRefused`: the lookup is built from a real price book exactly the way `buildPluginRegistry` builds it (model-level entries only, both sides required) — a model with only a pair price is refused 402 naming the missing model-level price with zero provider calls, and the same model is allowed (200, one mock hit) once the model-level price is added to the same book.
- Feed + view (319–320): `GET /dashboard/plugins` carries a `cost_cap` block (`enabled`, `cap_usd_per_request`, `models_checked`, `unpriced_models` sorted by model ID with `has_pair_price`); the Plugins view renders it as a "Cost-cap — models without a model-level price" card (Model | Pair price?, cap + models-checked in the head, one-line hint "Pair prices never satisfy the cap — add a model-level price"), refreshed on open + every 5 s + Refresh button, with honest states for not-enabled / no cap / all-priced / model-list-unavailable / no-prices-configured. A half-known model-level price (one side missing) counts as unpriced; pair prices (operator or models.dev-synced) count only as the `has_pair_price` signal. `Registry.CostCap()` (new `CostCapper` interface, `costCapPlugin.CostCapUSD()`) reports the cap without parsing the description string. README.md + README.v2.md (cost-cap bullet, same clause in both) and `docs/plugins.md` (layer choice + feed) updated in the same slice.
- New tests: 1 plugin unit test (`TestCostCapUSDReportsCap` — cap accessor + `Registry.CostCap` present/absent/unlimited), 3 server tests (`TestPluginsCostCapPairPriceOnlyStillRefused`, `TestPluginsCostCapFeedUnpricedModels` incl. cap-off / plugin-absent / no-price-book states, `TestPluginsCostCapFeedAllPriced`). All pass; `go test -race` clean on plugins and on the full server package (225.3 s), 0 data races.
- `go build ./...` — OK; `go vet` clean on the touched packages (plugins, server); `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; dashboard JS `node --check` clean on the extracted inline script (329,481 bytes); "Playground" absent (0 occurrences).
- Live smoke (real binary + 1 Python mock, script `scripts/smoke-cost-cap-model-prices.py` — committed): **9/9 checks passed** (C0 build/ready/provider-add, C1 pair-model 402 naming the missing model-level price + 0 mock hits, C2 long prompt 402 `cost_cap: estimated cost $0.040275 exceeds per-request cap $0.020000 (model "mock-model", 6007 prompt tokens + 1024 assumed completion tokens)` + hits unchanged, C3 short prompt 200 + mock hit once, C4 feed `cost_cap` enabled / cap 0.02 / 2 checked / unpriced=[pair-model, pair=true], C5 dashboard HTML carries the cost-cap card + Pair price column, C6 unauthenticated 401).
- Full suite `go test -count=1 -v ./...` (log `$HOME/.tmp/full-319-320-verbose.log`): **2,402 PASS events (1,943 top-level + 459 subtests), 0 fail, 39/39 packages ok, EXIT 0** (the "FAIL" lines in the log are the release-verify tamper-proof test's own expected-failure output inside passing packages, not failures — same as the sprint-18 gate).
- Concurrent-edit notes (other slices, not this one): while this slice ran, `go vet`/`go build` failed twice on foreign mid-save states — the 317–318 slice's `internal/server/intent.go` (`undefined: context`, import mid-save) and `internal/server/responses.go` (`setAutoRouteHeader` signature changed, call sites not yet updated) — both cleared on re-run once that slice finished saving; no fix of mine touched those files. Shared files carry foreign hunks alongside this slice's: CHECKLIST.md (the 311–312 rows, still uncommitted) and `web/index.html` (other slices' sections) — only this slice's own hunks are committed here (from a clean worktree at `c40c491`, see below); the rest is left for its owners/the coordinator. Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule).
- Honest limits: the feed lists served provider models only — auto-router virtual models (`auto/*`) are not listed (they resolve to concrete models at routing time, and a model-level price is configured per concrete model); a request naming an unlisted model (e.g. typed by hand, never served by a configured provider) is still refused 402 naming the model at request time, so the list is a proactive view, not a complete refusal set. The smoke's pair price is keyed by a placeholder provider id ("some-provider") because provider ids are generated at add time and the pair must exist in the startup config — the feed's `has_pair_price` keys on the model, and the refusal does not depend on the pair at all, so the proof is unaffected.

## Request-scoped outcome attribution gate (items 317–318, 2026-11-18, Go 1.27.4, measured by this slice)

- Root cause: the server attributed every finished request by reading the engine-global `LastTrace()` / `LastAutoDecision()` after the engine call returned — `recordIntentOutcome` (learning-loop cells), `recordMetrics` (provider/model/failovers), `setAutoRouteHeader` + the stream-path registration (feedback decision id), and `checkRequestBudgetOverage` (overage provider) all read the globals. Under concurrency a neighbour finishing in that window left its own decision/trace in the slots, so outcomes could land in the neighbour's cell and a user signal could attach to the neighbour's decision id. The old code admitted it in comments ("these stats are approximate telemetry, never billing data").
- What landed: `internal/router/attribution.go` (new) — a mutex-guarded per-request `Attribution` sink carried on the request context (`router.WithAttribution` / `AttributionFrom`, `PublishDecision` / `PublishTrace`); the engine publishes the decision at `resolveAuto` time from the value just computed (never by re-reading the global) and the trace when each call finishes (`ChatWithChainStrategy` defer, `runStream` defer, stream entry rejections, coalesced waiters share the flight's trace). Server reads the sink first (`requestDecision` / `requestTrace` in `internal/server/server.go`), globals only as fallback when no sink was attached — applied on all three serving surfaces (chat, `/v1/messages` translated, `/v1/responses`). The feedback store itself needed no change (already keyed by decision id). The globals stay: dashboard "most recent" feeds (`GET /dashboard/trace`, `GET /dashboard/auto-route/last`) are inherently last-value displays.
- Sensitivity proof (old code FAILS, new code PASSES): a HEAD worktree with only `internal/router/attribution.go` + `internal/server/attribution_test.go` copied in ran the server tests — both FAIL exactly as predicted (header names the neighbour's `dec-neighbour`; streaming outcome lands in the neighbour's `(quick-qa, model-b)` cell). With the fix both pass. The router test also pins the hazard with a control: while the slow request is in flight and a fast neighbour has finished, `eng.LastTrace()` / `LastAutoDecision()` return the neighbour's values — the sink is what saves the attribution.
- New tests: 4 router (`TestAttributionUnderConcurrency` — 8 parallel requests, distinct decisions, one delayed 500 ms; `TestAttributionStreamUnderConcurrency`; `TestAttributionStreamEarlyErrorPublishesTrace`; `TestAttributionCoalescedWaiterSharesFlightTrace`) + 2 server (`TestOutcomeAttributionPrefersRequestScopeOverGlobal`, streaming variant — a double that publishes the request's own decision/trace into the sink while the globals report a neighbour's). One test-design fix during the gate: the first full-suite run failed `TestAttributionUnderConcurrency` (`request 3: adaptive concurrency limit reached`) — the Gradient2 limiter learned from the slow request's RTT and shed a fast neighbour under load; the test engine now sets `DisableAdaptiveLimiter: true` (limiter behaviour, not attribution behaviour) and passes 3x under `-race`.
- Gate: `go build ./...` OK; `go vet` clean on router/server; `gofmt -l` clean on this slice's files; `go test -race -count=1` router (46.4 s) + server (172.3 s) ok, 0 data races (log `/tmp/race317.log`); full suite `go test -count=1 -v ./...` (log `$HOME/.tmp/fulltest-317-318-verbose.log`): **2,402 test events pass (1,943 top-level + 459 subtests), 0 fail, 39/39 packages ok, EXIT 0** (the `FAIL checksums/bundle/signature` lines are the release-verify tamper-proof test's expected-failure output inside a passing package, not failures); live smoke **16/16** (`scripts/smoke-attribution-317.py`: real binary + 2 Python mocks — 8 parallel intent chats, 8 distinct decision ids, exact 4/4 suggestion cells, feedback round-trip on the request's own id (201), 2 parallel streams growing the cells to 5, dashboard with no Playground).
- Honest limits (two pre-existing issues found, NOT fixed — out of scope): (1) intent decisions can never receive feedback (schema gap): `resolveIntent` builds a Decision with `IntentTier` set but `Tier` empty, and `feedback.RegisterDecision` rejects an empty/unknown tier, so the decision is never registered and `POST /dashboard/feedback` 404s — fixing needs a design decision about what tier, if any, an intent decision belongs to; the smoke's feedback round-trip therefore uses model `auto` (classified, real tier). (2) Stream outcomes always record as failures: `handleChat` calls `recordMetrics(req, nil, err, start)` on the stream branch (resp always nil), so `recordIntentOutcome` computes `ok := err == nil && resp != nil` → false, reason `upstream_error` — fixing needs threading the stream response through `handleChatStream`'s return. Also: the `recordIntentOutcome` proactive-cache-hit early return still reads the global `chr.LastCacheHit()` (the degrade wrapper would need to publish into the sink; `internal/degrade` is outside this slice's files); embeddings/rerank `setLastTrace` sites untouched (no outcome recording from them); remaining global reads are display-only (trace feed, decision headers, continuation pin, panic guard, stream deployment alert).
- Nothing pushed to GitHub (bulk-push rule); local-only commit by this slice (hunk-selective staging — shared files carry other slices' concurrent hunks: 311–312 forced-reasoning, 315–316 Responses transcripts, 319–320 cost-cap, 321–322 npm/installer; only this slice's hunks were staged, verified by grep for foreign markers in the staged diff: 0). The committed snapshot was re-verified by building it in a separate worktree.
